# Flat Wire Planning — Change Log

**Project:** Flat Wire Mill Implementation
**Document Type:** Consolidated change log — the single home for document change history
**Last Updated:** August 25, 2026 — nine Repository-wide rows for the `LatestDocument/` → `MVP-1/ProjectPlan/` sync; the five double-sectioned documents flagged and the 27 with no section recorded *(previously August 23, 2026)*
**Status:** Active — append here, never to an individual document

---

## How this file works

**This is the only change log in the repository.** On 12 Aug 2026 the per-document
`## Change Log` sections were removed from all 63 documents that carried one and their
entries moved here **verbatim**. Do not add a `## Change Log` section to any document —
append a row to the relevant section below instead.

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Where entries go** | Under the `##` heading naming the document they describe. Add the document's section if it has none yet, in repository path order. |
| **Cross-document changes** | Under [Repository-wide](#repository-wide), not repeated under each file touched. |
| **Documents with two sections** | Five have one under their **former** name and one under their current path — `Architecture.md`, `APIs.md`, `REVIEW.md`, `Roadmap.md`, `SprintPlan.md`. Both are kept, because a heading names the document as it stood. **Append to the later of the two**; each pair carries a note saying which. |
| **Documents with no section** | **27 files under `MVP-1/ProjectPlan/` have none**, including **phase files `03`–`11`, `13` and `14`** (only `01a`/`01b`/`01c` and `12` have one), all three `Frontend/` documents, three `Operations/`, three `Testing/`, `PLCTagSpecification.md`, `ExceptionHandling.md` and the `FW-219`/`220`/`223` plans. Their history lives in their own `**Last Updated:**` header trails and under Repository-wide. **Create the section on the next material change** — do not back-fill empty ones. *(Counted 25 Aug 2026.)* |
| **Entries are verbatim** | Dates, wording and column shapes are exactly as migrated. Several entries deliberately cite **superseded** numbering, values and filenames — that is the audit trail, not an error. Read each against the reading note in its section. |
| **Document headers still apply** | Each document keeps its **Project / Last Updated / Status** header block. Update `Last Updated` on the document *and* append the entry here. |
| **Versions live in the Version column** | The thirteen client-review specifications in `MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/Screens/` are versioned. Their sections carry a **`Version`** column; stamp the new version on the row you append, and set the same value in the document's `**Version:**` header. A row that records no version bump carries `—`. |

**The `MVP-1/` client-review specifications no longer carry a `## Document Change History`
block — removed 15 Aug 2026.** All thirteen blocks were deleted and their 39 version rows
folded into the sections below, which is why those sections have a `Version` column. The
`.docx` deliverables no longer print a history section; a specification states its version
in its header block and nowhere else, and this file is the only record of how it got there.
**`MVP-2/RequirementDocuments/` was out of scope for that pass** — its four specifications
(`DieManagement`, `PassScheduleGenerationSpec`, `PassScheduleManagement`, `ShiftSummary`)
still carry their own blocks. `RollAdjust.md` §6 *Change History* and the *Change History
Panel* section of the dashboard design reference are **not change logs and were not
touched** — the first describes a panel in the Roll Adjust dialog listing prior gap
adjustments, the second describes a screen.

> **Load-bearing content lives in this file.** Three things that other documents cite by
> name are change-log entries, and their citations now resolve here:
> the **`Q##` old→new renumbering map** (12 Aug 2026) and the **`[PLC]` v1.0 section and
> `PLC-Q##` mapping tables** (4 Aug 2026), both under
> [`Analysis/FlatWireOpenQuestions.md`](#analysisflatwireopenquestionsmd); and the
> **superseded table-count history** under
> [`LatestDocument/FlatWire_MasterSpecification.md`](#latestdocumentflatwire_masterspecificationmd).

> **Seven Database sections carry `MVP-1/DBChanges/…` display text from before the 13 August 2026
> restructure, and that is deliberate.** The hrefs are correct and current; only the visible label is
> stale. They are frozen because the GitHub-generated **anchor slug** is derived from the display
> text, and those slugs are linked from the `## Index` above and from outside this file — retitling
> them would silently break both. The same reasoning keeps `REVIEW.md`'s old file names.

## Repository-wide

Changes that span the repository rather than one document.

| Aug 15, 2026 | Plan team | **`FlatWire` will ship with no automated backend tests, and the saving is far smaller than it looks.** Decision taken 15 Aug 2026: **no xUnit of any kind** — no unit tests, no validator tests, no stub-fixture/contract suite, no seeded-DB integration suite. `[TS §1.2]`'s pyramid loses its *Unit — .NET* and *Integration — API* rows, the *Contract* row is demoted to manual inspection, and `[SP §9.2]`'s and `[TRP §7]`'s DoD bars drop *"xUnit + validator tests"*. **1A is untouched — Jest holds the 95 % coverage bar** — and that asymmetry is recorded as deliberate in `[TS]`, because a frontend at 95 % beside a backend at zero otherwise reads as an oversight.<br><br>**Four consequences, and three of them are counter-intuitive.**<br><br>**(1) The effort saving is −26 h, not the ~−44 a percentage-of-base assumption gives.** The reduction was **itemised against the deliverable inventory**, which is `[CE §2]`'s own method: `FW-138` 56→42 (the query-endpoint rate falls 4→3 h and the stub contract suite goes), `FW-147` 16→12 (validator tests), `FW-080` 32→28 (hub smoke harness) — while **`FW-207` (32 h) and `FW-150` (16 h) do not move at all**, because both are priced from production artifacts with no test in the basis. Most of 1B is production code. Netted against `SpoolController`'s +3 h the phase goes **541 → 519 h**, Phase 1 **1,132 → 1,110**.<br><br>**(2) The QA uplift is deliberately held, not recomputed.** `[CE §2]` makes QA a mechanical +20 % of the dev base, which would take 1B's 78 h down to 75 and **book a saving on the one line that must absorb the work** — with no automated tests, regression across Phases 4–14 is manual. The mechanical figure would be 516 h; **519 is the published one**, and `[CE §2]`'s QA row now carries an explicit *do not reduce this when a phase loses tests*. Anyone "correcting" 78 to 75 has booked the saving twice.<br><br>**(3) The rate-card change lands on Phases 4–13, not on 1B.** §2's command and query endpoint rows fell **6→5 h** and **4→3 h** — the unit test was priced into them. 1B is costed at the controller rate, so the edit that matters most for the programme barely touches the phase that triggered it. **This has not been absorbed into `[CE §2]`–`§5`**, per the precedent `[CE §8]` already sets.<br><br>**(4) ⚠ Two closed gaps lose their evidence, and neither is re-opened.** `FW-207`'s acceptance criteria are written as test assertions, and two of them were the *only* proof behind closures made the same day: **`G14`'s format half** rested on ***`RodAlpha("ROD-00041")` must throw***, and **`G21`'s domain half** on rejecting a second rod **with the DB index absent** — a test that deliberately disables the index to show the aggregate rule stands alone. | Aug 15, 2026 | Architecture | **`FlatWire` adopts tactical DDD — decision `D-29`, and it is the first UAL service to do so.** The trigger was a direct question: *are we following DDD?* **The answer was no, and two documents said yes.** `[SVC §3.1]` specified an **`AggregatesModel/`** folder that **does not exist in `CoilCheckin`** — copied from the ecosystem guide's idealised description of `ual-api` rather than observed — and `[SVC §3.2]` called five persistence entities *"Aggregates"*. The template is Clean Architecture layering + CQRS + Specification-pattern rules over an **anemic domain model**: `CoilCheckin.Domain/DBModels/Coils.cs` is a pure `{ get; set; }` bag.<br><br>**The decision is better supported than the objection I first raised.** `[ARC §2.2]` makes `CoilCheckin` binding, so a DDD `FlatWire` looked like it would be the only differently-shaped service in `ual-api`. Reading the framework showed otherwise: **`UA.Framework.Domain/EntityModels/` already ships `Entity`** (`DomainEvents`, `AddDomainEvent`, `ClearDomainEvents`, identity equality) **and `ValueObject`** (`GetAtomicValues`, structural equality); `CoilCheckin.Domain/Rules/` ships **`IBusinessRule`** and **`CheckRule`** → `BusinessRuleValidationException`; `CoilCheckin.Infrastructure/MediatorExtension.cs` ships **`DispatchDomainEventsAsync`**. The complete eShopOnContainers tactical toolkit is **in the shared framework and dormant**. **Adopting DDD here is a first use of the platform as designed, not a departure from it** — which is also why `FW-208` costs 8 h rather than 30.<br><br>⚠ **`D-29` overrides only the Domain-layer half of `[ARC §2.2]`.** `CoilCheckin` stays binding for controllers, `Program.cs`, `.csproj`/NuGet, DI, MediatR and pipeline behaviours — stated explicitly, because without that clause it reads as *"CoilCheckin is no longer the template"* and would licence divergence everywhere.<br><br>**Seven aggregate roots** — `FlatWireRun`, `RodStaging`, `WeldEvent`, `Spool`, `CoilOutput`, `RodCheckout`, `WipRejection` — boundaries in `[SVC §3.2a]`. **The exclusions carry as much design as the inclusions:** **`RunReading` is in no aggregate** (10 Hz time series; inside `FlatWireRun` it would materialise thousands of rows per command), `Rod` and `PassSchedule` are read models, and **`WeldEvent` is its own root** because welds are recorded at pre-check-in *before a run exists*. **Value objects** cover six alphas and seven dimensioned quantities. **Domain events** resolve `[SVC §3.2]`'s ban on SignalR types in Application — aggregates raise, `SaveChangesAsync` dispatches after commit, a handler translates to `IFlatWireClient`.<br><br>**Three schema facts were checked before designing, and two would have caused rework:** all seven roots carry `[Id] INT IDENTITY` so `Entity` fits ✅; but **the surrogate is not the identity** — `FlatWireRun` also has `RunId VARCHAR(20)`, *"referenced by all child tables"*, so repositories key on the **alpha value object**, not `int Id`, and `Entity.Equals()` is surrogate-based; and **`[SVC §3.4]`'s `ROWVERSION` list was wrong in both directions**, claiming `PassSchedule` (which MVP-1 never builds) and omitting `RodStaging` (which has one). **`D1` is left open and recorded, not assumed:** three aggregate roots — `WeldEvent`, `RodCheckout`, `WipRejection` — have no token despite being mutated after insert.<br><br>**Effort +72 h on Phase 1B (469 → 541 h):** `FW-207` 32 h · `FW-208` 8 h · `FW-141` 20→28 h · `FW-147` 12→16 h. **`[CE]` §2–§5 deliberately not re-derived** — recorded in `[CE §8]`, per the precedent that section already sets for 1C's ~17 h understatement. |

It was bundled into **`FW-N11` *"Operator session / `ITInhibit`"***, uncosted — and bundling two unrelated subjects on one card is very likely why neither half was ever priced. **Split**: **`FW-205`** (Phase 1B, **16 h RT** — the service, the per-line tag write, options binding, simulate mode, audit, and conditions 3–5's shared ingest watchdog) and **`FW-206`** (Phase 4, 8 h BE — conditions 1–2). **The split unblocks the buildable half**: `[PLC §8.5]` records that condition 2 turns on an *"active material-tracking identifier"* whose format has never been specified (`PLC-Q12`), which blocks conditions 1–2 but not 3–5, those reading only the footage tag the ingest already delivers. The two sum to **24 h**, the top of `[CE §2]`'s 12–24 h non-trivial-service band, so conditions 1–2 are priced once. **`FW-N11` keeps its id** for the operator-session remainder, being cited against Phase 6. ⚠ **The cascade is recorded, not absorbed.** `[TB §7]`'s reconciliation, the three phase files and `[RM]`'s Phase 1 row (1,027 → **1,054**) carry the corrected figures; **`[CE]` §2–§5 deliberately do not**, and `[CE §8]` states why — re-deriving in place moves the TOTAL row (3,660 → 3,687), MVP-1 (3,292 → 3,319), the weekly grid, the FTE columns and the descope ladder, which is a programme re-baseline rather than a documentation fix. This follows the precedent `[CE §8]` already sets for 1C's ~17 h understatement and that `FW-202`/`FW-203` set as *"additive to `[CE §3b]`"*. **All figures are hand-coded** — `[DE]` and `[SSP]` re-derive on their own retention factors and disagree with each other on Phase-1B RT by up to 19 h, so per `[TRP §1.4]` no single AI-assisted figure was published for either story. `[TRP]`'s 192 h trial figure is untouched and needs an explicit in-or-out call on `FW-205`. |
| Aug 14, 2026 | Plan team | **The trial run is now a generated workbook — `Development/FlatWire_TrialRunPlan.xlsx`, ten sheets, 61 work items, 778 hours.** [`Tools/build_trial_run_xlsx.py`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_trial_run_xlsx.py) is the repository's **fifth** script and **third** workbook builder, following the standing rule that each duplicates its helpers rather than importing them so a change to one guard cannot silently change another's. Sheets: *Read Me · Effort Summary · Effort by Phase and Discipline · Platform Detail · Work Items · Sprint Plan · Sprint Allocation · Blockers · Deferred Items · Removal Impact*. **Everything numeric is parsed from `[TRP]`**, titles from `[TB]`, and only the plain-language *what it delivers* per item is authored — in the new [`Tools/TrialRunContent.md`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/TrialRunContent.md). Effort figures reach about twenty files in this repository, so a hand-kept second copy of the trial numbers was the likeliest way they would drift.<br><br>**It is the first generator to write outside `MVP-1/SRS/`, and that placement is the design.** That folder holds **leakage-guarded** client deliverables; this workbook carries story identifiers, hours and gap identifiers on purpose, so it is **internal, shared with the client**, and lives in `Development/`. Putting it in `MVP-1/SRS/` would place an unguarded file among guarded ones. Two further departures from the development-plan workbook are recorded in the script header so none is "fixed" back: **story identifiers are present and there is no leakage guard** (`FW-\d` is in *that* script's fatal `LEAKS` list, and it numbers its rows 1..N instead), and **effort is in hours, not days**.<br><br>**An abbreviation guard takes the leakage guard's place, on client direction that no abbreviation appear in any cell.** The reason it had to be a build-time transformation rather than a style note is that `[TRP]` writes `FE`/`BE`/`DB`/`RT`/`T1`/`1A`/`DB5`/`FL2` freely as prose, **a filtered spreadsheet column has no surrounding sentence, and `DB` is genuinely ambiguous — it is both the Database discipline and the Dashboard prefix.** A `LABELS` map expands every abbreviation at write time; a guard refuses the build if a bare one survives. Two properties keep the expansion safe: **single pass** over one longest-first alternation, so a replacement's output cannot be re-matched; and **no replacement contains its own key**, which is what makes it idempotent and why phrase entries exist. The guard's token set is **deliberately wider** than the expander's — `EF`, `DI`, `PWA` and `JWT` appear only inside phrases, so a bare one in new prose is flagged rather than expanded into something clumsy. `Phase 1A` and `Sprint 1` are exempt as identifiers, and code identifiers are untouched because `\bPLC\b` does not match `PLCTagService`.<br><br>**Five fatal guards, and every one was proved live by breaking it**: reconciliation (*"block table: computed 779, the plan prints 778"*), coverage, title resolution, drift, and abbreviation. Each run named the offending location and left no workbook on disk. Two findings worth keeping: the tables' **footer rows are excluded from their own sums on purpose** — counting them double-counts the total, which is why testing reconciliation means changing a *data* row; and injecting a bare `RT` did **not** trip the guard because the expander correctly handled it, so the real test uses a token the guard covers and the expander deliberately does not. |

Also noted: pre-check-out has **no SRS requirement ID** — §4.17 covers only post-check-in removal, so a new `PCI`-series block is needed. Total questions: 70. |
| Jul 29, 2026 | Analysis team | Added **Q71–Q73** from the Dashboard 2A mockup review: rod diameter tolerance (`CHK007`) has **no column anywhere in the schema** — `AlloyProperty` carries only gauge/width, which are flat-wire *output* dimensions, so the mockup had hard-coded a single 0.005" that is wider than every value in the standards table; whether a failed staging inspection persists a `RodStaging` row at all, given that the UI/API `Blocked` bay state is derivable (`Staged` + any `Fail`) but nothing currently writes it; and which WIP station FL3 pre-check-in posts to, since the schema/DDL/API all scope staging to FL1 **and FL3** while only `FL1PO` is seeded and no `FL3PO` exists. Total questions: 73. |
| Jul 29, 2026 | Client requirement | **Free rod processing order.** Planned rod sequence is explicitly **not enforced** — the operator may process rods in any order; staging validates only current-order membership and availability, and must never block because an earlier-planned rod is unprocessed. Both sequences are retained: planned for planning/reporting, actual in the transaction history for traceability. **Q70 partly resolved** — reframed from "what is `RodSeqno`'s scope" to "which of two sequences is it"; `RodSeqno` is now the *actual* sequence and a new `RodStaging.PlannedSeqno` snapshots the planned one. **Q69 leaning "no"** — validation is scoped to the *current* order, which makes a future-order rod a non-candidate; not closed, because continuous feed then cannot cross an order boundary and that consequence needs confirming. |
| Jul 29, 2026 | Client direction | **Q74 added, part-decided.** Rod→order resolves from **`planning_routings`** at pre-check-in / check-in, so the order is *revealed* by the scan rather than chosen — which makes the first rod on a cold line validatable and removes the need for an order chooser. A rod whose order is scheduled on **another line** is **not refused**: the operator is notified and a **supervisor override** (reason + badge/ID + PIN, remote-approval fallback — the Q66 pattern) authorises it, with the override, supervisor and reason recorded on `RodStaging`. Still open: PIN validation source (shared with Q66), whether the mid-order cross-order case should also be overridable given the weld/genealogy constraint, applying the same panel at Dashboard 2, whether scheduling gets corrected, and whether the other line's operator is notified. |
| Jul 30, 2026 | Analysis team | Added **Q75** — whether multiple rod bundles can be **stacked on a single VPS payoff position**. Every artifact assumes one bundle per bay (`UX_RodStaging_Bay` enforces it as a filtered unique index), but nothing states whether that is the equipment's limit or a modelling assumption, and eye-to-sky is the geometry in which stacking is standard practice. The resolving fact — received bundle gross weight — is stated **two incompatible ways** in the delivered contracts: **8,690–8,840 lb** in `/payoff/status`, `/staging/queue` and `/staging/rod` versus **2,000 lb** in `/rod/{alpha}` and `/checkin/rod`, against a 9,000 lb position rating. That contradiction matters independently of stacking, because the payoff weight bar and the weld alerts (warn 3,000 lb / critical 2,000 lb) are calibrated to it. Recorded the four schema consequences if stacking is real, and flagged **`CK_WeldEvent_PayoffDiff`** as worth relaxing pre-emptively — it rejects an in-stack weld outright, and the real invariant is that the rod *alphas* differ, not the bays. Total questions: 75. |
| Jul 30, 2026 | Analysis team | **Scoped the register to shopfloor.** Added a `Scope` column to the Quick Reference table and a **Shopfloor Scope — Filtered Index** section at the top: **48 of 75** questions relate to Flat Wire Mill shopfloor changes (35 Open/In Progress, 13 Decided); the other 27 belong to adjacent modules and are marked `Other`. **Nothing was deleted** — this Change Log cites question numbers throughout, and `OQ-##` references point inbound from the phase files, `REVIEW.md` and the master specification, so the numbering must stay contiguous. Filter rule: `Shopfloor` if a Dashboard 1–14 screen reads, validates against or writes it — which keeps reference data and equipment limits in scope (Q32, Q36, Q38, Q41) and leaves scheduling representation (Q10), pre-scheduling validation (Q53) and order-entry behaviour (Q9, Q11, Q12) out. Also corrected the **Q4** Quick Reference row, which still read `Open` although its detail entry records it **Decided May 15, 2026**. |
| Jul 31, 2026 | Analysis team | **Q72 items 1–2 decided; Q76–Q77 added** from the Dashboard 2A UX review (`Dashboard2A_UXReview.md`, deleted 1 Aug 2026 — in git history at `2a0426b`). **Q72:** `Blocked` confirmed **derived**, and pre-check-in now **commits the `RodStaging` row before the inspection gate** — `POST /staging/rod` returns `201` with `state: "Blocked"` instead of `422`-and-write-nothing. The deciding argument is physical: a bundle is not unbanded until it is on the payoff, so a rod that fails inspection is already in the bay, and writing no row made `GET /payoff/status` report an occupied position as `NotStaged` and let the next rod be staged into it. `CHK010` unchanged (no bypass). Item 3 — *what releases a blocked row* — is now the blocking residual: `Status` has no value for a WIP-rejection outcome and inventing one would move the row outside `UX_RodStaging_Bay`'s filter, freeing a bay that is not free. **Q76:** `UX_RodStaging_Bay` is keyed `(LineId, PayoffPosition)` while `CK_RodStaging_LineId` admits both `FL1` and `FL3` — so if the two lines share one physical VPS (the assumption everywhere, and why `FL3PO` was never seeded, Q73), **two rods can be staged on one physical bay with every constraint satisfied**. Not covered by G20, which settled payoff-position vocabulary rather than uniqueness scope; logged as **G21**. **Q77:** a welded rod could be un-staged from the queue — welded is `IsWelded` on a `Staged` row, so every "staged" control matched it — despite being physically welded to the rod in the mill; the control is removed, but `WLD011` (supervisor reversal of a weld) remains unspecified. Total questions: 77. |
| Jul 30, 2026 | Client direction | **Q74 extended — out-of-planned-sequence now requires supervisor authorisation.** The operator is notified when the rod is not the one planning expects next, and a supervisor override is required to depart from the planned sequence — same credential block as the off-schedule override (reason + badge/ID + PIN, remote-approval fallback), recorded as `RodStaging.OutOfSequenceOverride` + `ExpectedRodAlpha`. Two deviations can co-occur and share one sign-off. **This supersedes the earlier free-processing-order requirement** (Q70), which had the operator re-sequencing at will with no warning and no override; both sequence columns are retained regardless, so the deviation is now authorised as well as recorded. |
| Jul 30, 2026 | Client direction | **Eleven answers from the 30 Jul call — six questions closed, three reversals.** **Q75 Decided:** rods **cannot be stacked**; only two rods at a time, one per payoff, as on the mills — the weight contradiction inside it is re-homed as **Q81**. **Q74 off-schedule REVERSED:** no blocking message and no supervisor override — the system **auto-selects the correct station** (FL3-planned rod scanned on the FL1 tab switches the tab), at both pre-check-in and check-in. Out-of-sequence override stays, provisionally, with a re-review committed. **Q72 item 3 Decided:** a failed staging inspection is captured as a rejection with a reason on the rejection screen and the rod goes to **`HOLD`** — that is what releases the row and frees the bay. **Q67 Decided (Critical):** `INFLAT` is set **only at check-in**, no intermediate status for welded-but-not-checked-in — reverses the interim SRS-following design and unblocks Phase 4; the reqsum / `wip_coil_orders` residual stays open. **Q71 shape Decided:** min/max tolerances exist for **gauge, width, diameter and ovality**, held in the lookup, applied at both pre-check-in and check-in — values to follow by e-mail, so nothing is to be seeded. **Q69 Decided:** a rod may carry **more than one order**, which inverts the "current order only, else refuse" rule; sequencing deferred to **Q79**, with a possible deferral out of this delivery floated on the call. **Q68 Decided:** pre-check-out needs supervisor approval **only when the rod is welded**, where it is a rejection to `HOLD` — which also **decides Q77** and reverses the Jul 31 removal of the welded-rod Unstage control. **Q65 Decided:** short close is an **unplanned stop** on the mill 10-90 pattern, graded against the **customer min/max weight**, outside-range needing supervisor override + hold or a concession offer, with the spool run off regardless and a mid-run coil break restarting the stop **from zero**. **Q60 basis Decided:** the 2,000 lb default target is withdrawn in favour of the customer weight range. Added **Q78** (rod scheduled on neither FL1 nor FL3 — not covered on the call), **Q79** (multi-order sequencing / MVP scope), **Q80** (panel resolution 1280×1024 vs 1920×1080). Total questions: 81. |
| Aug 1, 2026 | Project decision | **`OffSchedule*` columns dropped.** With Q74's off-schedule case becoming a navigation behaviour, `RodStaging.OffScheduleOverride`, `ScheduledLineId` and `CK_RodStaging_OffSched` are removed and `CK_RodStaging_Override` reverts to keying on `OutOfSequenceOverride` alone. The analysis recommendation had been to retain them unwritten against **Q78**; the project decision is removal, so Q78 re-adds a column group if it ever needs one. **`OverrideBy` / `OverrideAt` / `OverrideReason` survive** — they are shared with the out-of-sequence override, and dropping them would delete the surviving override's audit trail. | Aug 1, 2026 | Project decision | **Weld quality is captured at the weld; `Q24` widened.** Dashboard 2A's Mark as welded dialog now requires a **Pass/Fail** result with a reason mandatory on Fail (`WLD013`), using the same six reasons as Dashboard 4 — so one physical join is documented identically wherever it is captured. Quality was the only NOT NULL `WeldEvent` column that dialog lacked, so **`POST /weldevent` becomes the single weld write** and `POST /staging/rod/mark-welded` is **retired**; the `RodStaging` weld columns are set in the same transaction, **on a Pass only**. This closes `WeldEventPopupPlan` **Q-W1** ("is a quality result at weld time acceptable on the floor?" — yes) and confirms its decision **D-A**; it needs **no schema change**. **A failing weld does not mark the rod welded** — the join did not hold, so the position keeps reading *not yet welded*, the station states the failure and its reason, and the operator remakes the weld. That last rule **widens Q24** rather than adding a question: a remade weld leaves **several `WeldEvent` rows for one physical join**, which is the re-weld-on-certificate question Q24 already asks — now reachable two ways (a weld that breaks mid-run, and a weld that fails its quality check before anything runs through it). Footage attribution across the two boundaries belongs to **Q22**. Total questions: 81. |
| Aug 1, 2026 | Build decision | **DB6, DB8 and DC converted from screens to dialogs.** SPC checkpoint, WIP rejection and die change are now popups (`spc_checkpoint.js`, `wip_rejection.js`, `die_change.js`) raised over the screen the operator is already on, rather than pages navigated to. The reason is context: WIP rejection alone is reached from five places and, as a page, could describe only one — its material banner was hard-coded to *R00042 at 8,220 ft*, and the **Q72 item 3** pre-check-in path (rejection releases the bay, rod to `HOLD`) could not be represented at all. It now is: on that path `runId` and `footagePosition` are `null`, the dialog states on screen that submitting releases the bay, and it reports `releasesBay` back to the staging station. Two hand-offs the spec always described also start working — a gauge-drift/size-change die change opens the SPC checkpoint it mandates (**Q56**), and an out-of-spec checkpoint's *suspend material* opens the rejection with the failing reading carried over. **No dialog scrolls**: an oversized popup is scaled to fit the window, since an operator in gloves cannot drag a scrollbar to reach *Confirm*. Added **Q82** — DB1's "WIP Rejection**s**" nav item reads as a list screen that does not exist. Total questions: 82. |
| Aug 1, 2026 | Build decision | **Dashboard 2A's weld-readiness strip removed; every control moved onto the bay it acts on.** The 96px band between the payoff cards and the traveler queue is gone. Its narrative was already duplicated by the cards in every branch but one — weight and percentage on the payoff bar, all four weld states in the bay alert, cold start in the empty-bay text — and the exception, *induction-weld tail to head*, became the staged card's alert while a rod is running. **Mark as welded** moved to the **staged** card (`PCI008` defaulting; all five disabled-tooltips kept, since they are the only statement of *why* it is unavailable) and **Welds this run · N** to the **active** card. The reclaimed space went to the queue, which is ~108px taller. Added **Q83** (at cold start *Welds this run* is now absent rather than shown-and-greyed — supersedes **TC-068e**) and **Q84** (whether the operator should land on the staging station or the run monitor after check-in). New requirements `FR-050a`, `FR-051a`, `FR-051b`, `FR-079a`; new cases TC-068f/g/h/i and TC-079a. **Note for G27:** the queue heading reuses the name *Rods In Queue* from the retired Dashboard 4 accordion but is **not** that control — it is a read-only table with no drag and no undo, so the re-sequencing capability remains homeless. | Aug 2, 2026 | Client direction | **Q57 part-decided — FL2 has two spool statuses and runs one spool at a time.** FL2 has no space to stage material, so a spool is either waiting for the line or on it: the operator-visible vocabulary is fixed at **`Ready for FL2`** (`RECEIVED`) and **`Checked in`** (`INFLAT`), and **`STAGED` is never set at FL2** — the "At TPO" status is withdrawn from the spool queue, staging remaining the FL1 concept per `PCI002`. **Check-in is exclusive:** while any spool is checked in, **no spool offers a check-in action** — not the others and not the checked-in one — and it returns only on checkout. | Aug 4, 2026 | Analysis team | **Added `Q85`–`Q88` from the PLC tag-surface consolidation.** The surface existed in six partial, mutually contradictory copies; reading them as one document surfaced four questions no single copy could raise. **`Q85`** — speed is pushed as a **target** in four sources and a **limit** in two, including inside `02-SRS.md` itself (§9.1 vs `FR-073`); a setpoint and a safety clamp are different tags with opposite failure modes, so `FR-073`'s wording is deliberately left unfixed until this closes. **`Q86`** — edge type is in the push payload in four sources and absent from a fifth, and separately **no edger tag path exists anywhere in the repo**: the only edger-adjacent tag is `FL1.EdgeSet.Status.Active`, on the one line that has no edger. **`Q87`** — every map addresses FM2 as `FL2.FM2.*` even inside the FL1 map, so it is undetermined whether FL3's single-batch push crosses a controller boundary; commissioning test **C5** passes either way and cannot distinguish them. **`Q88`** — units are in the path for `Speed.FPM` and `Weight.Lb` but not for gauge, width, roll gap or die diameter, where inches are assumed and never stated; a controller reporting gauge in mils would pass every structural check and produce out-of-spec wire. It is **Critical**: every published path follows a proposed convention, none has been read off a controller, and a wrong path fails silently. **`Q89`** surfaced a contradiction the consolidation exposed — assumption A2 (rescued from the deleted file, its only home) says take-up load cells are fitted, while [SpoolCompletionNotification.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/Screens/SpoolCompletionNotification.md) says the completion weight is *derived* from footage × cross-section, and **the tag map publishes no take-up weight path at all** — so the machine-stop prompt, the completion transaction and the printed label all read a value with no published source. **`Q91`** (ordinal convention: `DB1`/`Payoff2` vs `FM2.Stand6S1` — R7 and R8 contradict each other) and **`Q92`** (`ITInhibit` is unprefixed in all six sources while every other tag is line-scoped; if it is genuinely plant-level, one line’s unmet prerequisite blocks all three) complete the set. | Aug 4, 2026 | Client direction | **`Q93` decided on arrival: FM2 has three stands — `S1` 8″, `S2` 6″, `S3` 6″.** The client corrected FL2's roller sizes. This is **not a digit swap**: the repo had modelled a separate 8″ roller upstream of three 6″ stands — **four components** — because the May 21 note was recorded as *"FM2 has three 6″ stands"* in `00-foundations.md` §0.3 and read that way. **The 8″ roller is S1**, and the fourth stand never existed. The misreading had propagated into ~50 files, the `Stand` seed data, two SQL `CHECK` constraints, the `ComponentName` enum, the PLC tag grammar and eight mockups. Three things pin the correction: the client's **published PLC map has exactly three FM2 stations**, all `[CONFIRMED]` by observation; **every seeded pass schedule has exactly three FM2 component rows** with a descending gap chain; and **`FM2_6inS3` never had a tag path or a seed row** — its absence was itself logged as `OI-36`/`G29`. **Two open items close, neither having been a real defect. `OI-04`** — *"is the mandatory stand `FM2_6inS2` or `6″ S3`?"* — both named **the same physical stand**; the phantom made one answer look like two. **`OI-36`** — *"the final stand has no tag path"* — the map was complete. **Component names become position-only** (`FM2_S1`/`FM2_S2`/`FM2_S3`) with roll diameter moved into a new `Stand.RollDiameterIn` column, because diameter inside an identifier is what let this survive ten weeks. **No gap or gauge value is recomputed** — the seeded chains were always right, and `FR-387`'s multipliers just move from diameter labels to positions, which fixes a defect where the final stand had no gap formula. **One real physics consequence:** roll radius feeds the generation engine, so S1 admits **~1.33×** the draft of a 6″ stand and develops **~1.16×** the force at equal draft — `F_max` and mill modulus become per-stand (`PSG-D10`, `PSG-D12`), and §3.3.5's allocation illustration is recomputed at `k` = 3 (**5.4% / 9.8% / 13.5%**). **Added `Q94`** (`PLC-Q04`, Critical) — the PLC station rename departs from observed `[CONFIRMED]` strings and needs the controls engineer's sign-off; gap **G32**. Total questions: 94. |
| Aug 4, 2026 | Client direction | **`Q92` decided: `ITInhibit` is line-scoped — `FL1.ITInhibit` and `FL2.ITInhibit`.** One tag per line, so a line blocked from running blocks **only itself**. The plant-level reading — in which an idle FL1 with no rod checked in would have stopped a scheduled FL2 — is excluded. All six pre-consolidation sources had recorded the tag bare, without its line prefix. **The answer reaches further than one tag.** Rule **R2** of the naming convention (*"the first segment is always the line; there is no plant-level tag"*) was `[PROPOSED]` **only because of this single counterexample** and is now `[CONFIRMED]` — which matters because the specification asks the client to confirm **the grammar** rather than ~60 individual strings, and every `[PROPOSED]` path derived from that grammar now rests on a convention with no exception in it (**Q90**). Commissioning test **C7** also becomes a per-line test: set each of the five conditions on one line, assert the other two still run. **One residual, on FL3 only** — whether the hybrid line carries `FL3.ITInhibit` or asserts both controllers' interlocks follows from **Q87** / `PLC-Q08`, the same question that decides whether FL3's single-batch push crosses a controller boundary; FL3 is the one line where a block legitimately implicates a second, the two being one physical thread of material. Applied to [PLCTagSpecification.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/PLCTagSpecification.md) v1.3 as decision **D15** (§2.2 R2, §2.3, §2.4, the three §3.2 tag maps, §3.4 item 4, **§5.1 — a new per-line tag table**, §12, §13.1, and Part A/Part B of the sign-off sheet), and re-rendered to `MVP-1/SRS/PLCTagSpecification.docx`. `PLC-Q18` closed. **Register index also reconciled:** `Q93` (decided Aug 4) and `Q94` (Critical, open) had been added without updating the filtered index — `Q93` is now listed under Decided and `Q94` under Critical/Open. Counts: **46 Open/In Progress unchanged** — less `Q92`, plus `Q94`, so 8 Critical · 24 High · 13 Medium · 1 Low — and **20 Decided** *(from 18, adding `Q92` and `Q93`)* · 1 Superseded. Total questions: 94. |

Added `Q95` (Critical).** Six corrections were applied and generalised across all three lines: `Speed.FPM`→`Speed`, `Payoff{n}.Weight.Lb`→`Weight`, `Die.ActiveDiameter`→`Diameter`, `Status.Active`→`Status.IsActive`, `Status.Fault`→`Status.IsFaulted`, and `.Current` dropped from roll gap, gauge, width and footage — **41 paths, FL1 15 · FL2 14 · FL3 23**. | Aug 4, 2026 | Analysis team | **Tag specification reissued as v1.0 — a first issue, not a consolidation. `[CONFIRMED]` retired; both registers renumbered.** The document had accumulated three tells that it was a consolidation of six sources revised four times, and all three are gone. **(1) `[CONFIRMED]` is withdrawn entirely** — the legend row is deleted and the reading convention is now two tags. Tracing the status is what forced this: **no client artifact contains a single tag path** (all 14 `BaseDocuments/` files searched for every tag token returned one hit, *"~1800–2000 FPM"*, a speed figure), the strings trace to our own `HMIAndSCADALayout.md` §6.2 — deleted 4 Aug, which itself said *"they follow a proposed naming convention, not a verified map"* — and `[CONFIRMED]` was manufactured by the convention's own second limb, *"or stated consistently across the source specifications."* Eight items claimed it; the count is now **zero** (**Q90**). | Aug 6, 2026 | Client direction | **Five outcomes from the 6 Aug client call: `Q79` decided, `Q96`–`Q99` added.** **`Q79` DECIDED** — the rod consumption sequence is a three-tier rule: **full coils → partials → multi-order coils last** when welding is involved, operators working a **pick list in planned order** as on the mills, and an operator **may not jump a multi-order coil to the head of the line**. Full coils of the same order in the middle may be taken in any order. **The validation applies at pre-check-in *and* at check-in**, and it is **in MVP-1** — the deferral floated on 30 Jul was not taken. **The rule was stated three ways before it settled** — Srikanth gave full→partial→multi-order, Tim said partials first and elaborated on it, then corrected himself to Shray's reading at 12:30 — so the exchange is recorded in the entry to stop the wrong half being quoted. This **resolves `G22`**, which has stood since 1 Aug as a knowingly-wrong staging rule waiting on exactly this answer. **`OI-13`'s flow was decided in the same call** (recorded against the master spec, not here): a wire break that is welded keeps the **same alpha** — Bob: *"if it's not actually splitting and you're welding back together, it's the same alpha, no new alpha"* — with the weld recorded at its footage position and the material flagged in SCADA; only the persistence target is still missing, raised as **`G34`**. Added **`Q96`** (High) — the no-weld-customer break, decided in principle as **Z-mill coil-break logic** with a supervisor disposition on both pieces, open on the vocabulary, the gate and the persistence. Added **`Q97`** (High) — **FM2 carries two dancers with two modes** (compensating speed control and tension), which nothing in the repository models and whose tension mode **contradicts `PSM012`**, *"tension is derived from speed, never entered manually"*; gap **`G35`**. Added **`Q98`** (High) — **edging is vertical profiling roll-forming, not a knifing cut**, so it needs its own formulas treated as their own operation; the coefficients land as `PSG-D24`–`D26` in the generation spec and this row is the pointer. Added **`Q99`** (Medium) — does multi-order-last survive **without** welding? Both readings are on the recording and they differ by one validation branch. Also corroborated without change: **`Q93`** (FM2 `S1` = 8″, `S2`/`S3` = 6″, re-stated by Tim from the generation spec) and **`Q60`** (FL1 output in ~1,800 lb multiples, FL2 in 800/900). Full record in [ClientCall_2026-08-06_SyncPlan.md](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-06_SyncPlan.md). Counts: **50 Open/In Progress** *(9 Critical · 27 High · 13 Medium · 1 Low)* · 21 Decided · 1 Superseded. Total questions: 99. |
| Aug 6, 2026 | Analysis team | **Gap audit of `PassScheduleGenerationSpec.md` — twelve defects fixed, five algorithmic; specification at v1.4.** No `Q##` opens or closes, but **`Q98` is widened** and five new `PSG-` items are owed by the client. The five that mattered: **(1)** FL2 standalone had **no path through the engine** — §6.2 began at *“area reduction, rod to flattening entry”* and every branch exited via FL1, FL3 or FL1→anneal→FL2, while the section's own note claimed a standalone FL2 schedule was producible; the tree now branches on **input form** and a flat spool enters at Step 9. **(2)** §4.2 had **no incoming flat-section inputs**, and `Prior cold work` — optional — is now **mandatory on a spool input**, since the anneal gate otherwise starts from zero on already-worked material. **(3)** `no_draw_threshold` gated the whole drawing sequence but was **never defined** anywhere — now **`PSG-D28`**, with `FR-383`'s 2% explicitly *not* adopted pending review. **(4)** **Die snapping could violate the bound it snapped from**: §3.3.3 calls the zero-elongation entry diameter a *lower bound* whose use as an answer produces under-width wire, yet Step 6 snapped to nearest and the worked example snapped **down**. The final die now snaps **up** from a bound — and the down-snap had passed every reduction check, because those test reduction, not width. **(5)** Step 9A's partition function was **undefined** (`Δt = φ · f(…)`), so **`PSG-D25` asked for a coefficient to a function that had not been stated**; it is now volume-constant strain decomposition tagged `[RECOMMENDED DEFAULT]`, with the shape itself raised as `PSG-Q31`. Also closed two **dangling validations** — `V33` had no calculation behind it (new Step 12A; over-capacity **splits the order**, not fails it) and `V16` had no method (**`PSG-D29`**) — and corrected §7's rule tally, which claimed 12/14/4 against a table holding 16/13/1. New client items: **`PSG-D28`**, **`PSG-D29`**, **`PSG-D30`**, **`PSG-Q30`**, **`PSG-Q31`**. **`PSG-D30` overlaps `Q36`** (footage-to-weight, Critical) and must resolve to one factor shared with spool completion and the coil label. Re-rendered to `PassScheduleGenerationSpec.docx`. Totals unchanged: 99 · 50 Open/In Progress · 21 Decided · 1 Superseded. |
| Aug 6, 2026 | Analysis team | **Second gap audit of `PassScheduleGenerationSpec.md` — eighteen defects, seven algorithmic; specification at v1.5.** Again no `Q##` opens or closes, but **`Q97` and `Q98` are both widened** and **eight more `PSG-` items** are owed. The recurring cause was a change made in one section between 1 and 6 Aug and never carried into the sections that consume it. **The seven that mattered: (1) width was designed at one point on the line and nowhere else** — the entry diameter was solved from target width, then nothing computed width through FM2, although two later steps required it. New §3.3.12 and Step 9B do, and they surface a consequence no artifact had stated: **there is no edger at the mill exit, so final width leaves S3, which spreads** — the last edger must be set *narrow* of target or every FL2/FL3 coil runs over-width. **`PSG-D08` is therefore not an FL1-only item**, and S3's exit must be instrumented in the same trial campaign as FM1's. **(2)** Steps 9/9A/9B are **mutually dependent** and were written sequentially — now one iterative solve. **(3)** Step 9 had **no form for the FL2 path v1.4 had just added**, solving for an FM1 exit gauge that does not exist on FL2; split into rod and spool directions, with `k_bulk = 0` handled and a **pre-anneal** branch added, since *"intermediate anneal"* is meaningless with no upstream stage. **(4) An undersize rod passed silently** — a rod smaller than the required entry gives a *negative* area reduction, which satisfied the no-draw branch, so both draw boxes bypassed and a warning-free schedule issued for a product that cannot be made. **(5) The bite condition was the wrong relation on the wrong coefficient** — `PSG-D04` asked one number for die/wire *and* roll/material contact, and the limit is **quadratic in μ**; split, with rolling friction as **`PSG-D31`** (High). The flat-entry relation also **rejects the spec's own nominal example** when applied to FM1's round entry (it would need μ ≥ 0.176), so it is excluded and the real criterion asked as **`PSG-Q32`** (High). **(6)** Drive power, tension and `Q_p` were **required but not calculable** — no torque relation existed behind an Error-severity check, `R34` was tagged `[INDUSTRY STANDARD]` with no relation named, and `Q_p` had no source and no id. **(7) Cold work under-reported** — the area ratio equals the *length* strain alone, so v1.3's edger term was understated ~2×; equivalent strain now used for rolling and edging, and `ε_prior` added, which the formula had omitted although §4.2 makes it mandatory. **Also:** Step 12A split orders against take-up **ratings** rather than the **planned output multiple** this same call corroborated (`D8` — ~1,800 lb FL1, 800/900 FL2), roughly halving the reported unit count — now **`PSG-D32`**; §8 gained a third outcome (*not evaluated*), since a strict Error/Warning rule means no schedule could be approved at go-live; §8.7 held **fifteen rows against forty validations** and is completed, including `V27`, an **Error** blocked on `PSG-D26` and previously unlisted. New client items: **`PSG-D31`**–**`PSG-D35`**, **`PSG-Q32`**–**`PSG-Q34`**. **Separately, the audit found `FR-381`/`384`/`385`/`386`/`387` contradict this specification on five counts of physics** — recorded as `FlatWire_MasterSpecification.md` **§10.5** and a `REVIEW.md` Tier 1 callout, **not** in the client deliverable, which carries no requirement IDs. Re-rendered to `PassScheduleGenerationSpec.docx`. Totals unchanged: 99 · 50 Open/In Progress · 21 Decided · 1 Superseded. |

| Aug 11, 2026 | Recommendations added | **All 73 open questions now carry a `Recommendation:`** at the foot of their body — **our proposed answer, explicitly not a decision and carrying no client authority.** The purpose is to make a client review a confirm-or-correct exercise rather than a blank page, and to give the build a defensible default while an answer is outstanding. **Where a body already held a proposal, leaning or preferred option, the recommendation crystallises that rather than inventing a new one** — `Q30` Option 2, `Q49`'s proposed checkout behaviour, `Q63`'s 5-second dwell, `Q66`'s ±2 % and scale-wins, `Q72`'s reuse-`Unstaged` note, `Q88` part 1, `Q91`'s two-rule resolution and `Q84`'s third option are all restatements, not new positions. **Confirming a recommendation closes the question** and moves it to [FlatWireDecidedQuestions.md](Analysis/FlatWireDecidedQuestions.md) with the date and the confirming name. **Nine recommendations deliberately converge rather than standing alone:** the seven PLC items (`Q85`, `Q86`, `Q87`, `Q88`, `Q91`, `Q94`, `Q95`) all recommend closing inside the single controls-engineer session `Q90` asks for — they are one conversation blocked on one person; `Q73` and `Q76` recommend one answer between them, because the station-versus-`LineId` index fix follows directly from the one-VPS reading; and `Q97` is to be closed from `PSG-Q29` rather than asked twice. **Three recommendations are deliberately "do less than asked":** `Q19` (no location tracking), `Q43` (no roll lifecycle in MVP-1) and `Q82` (rename the nav item rather than build the register) — each says so with its reason, because an unstated descope reads as an oversight. **Two flag a wrong-in-the-unsafe-direction risk rather than a preference:** `Q85` recommends implementing against a clamp because the wrong guess then fails loudly instead of starting a threading line at full speed, and `Q88` recommends declaring the unit in configuration because with `.Lb`/`.FPM` gone nothing in an address can catch a 1000× mils error. Header note added above the filtered index. No question opened, closed or changed status. |

| Aug 11, 2026 | Scope division | **Divided by MVP scope — tagged in place, not split, and one table rescued first.** The inventory gained an **`MVP`** column and all fifteen dashboard sections gained a scope badge: **DB9, DB9A and DB10 are MVP-2**; everything else is MVP-1, **including DB7 and DB7B**, which returned with Phase 9 on the same day. **The alloy lookup table was lifted out of the Dashboard 9 section first** and promoted to the top-level **Alloy Reference Data** — it is MVP-1 reference data (cited by the master specification and by `phase-13`, whose alloy-lookup admin is the non-deferrable half of that phase) that had been buried inside an MVP-2 screen; tagging DB9 without moving it would have carried MVP-1 data out of scope, the same seam class as `CoilCompletionService`. Its `Spring-back factor` column is flagged, not removed — master spec **§10.5** arbitrates springback as a machine stiffness misfiled as a material property, but `AlloyProperty` carries the column and the generation that consumes it is MVP-2. **Two things stayed whole deliberately:** the **Screen Navigation Map**, which spans both scopes and gained an MVP legend instead of being halved (*"a half-ER is worse than none"*), and this **Change Log**. **A note was added under DB7 recording that DB7B has no section here and never had one** — which is precisely how it reached 11 Aug 2026 with no owning specification. |
| Aug 11, 2026 | Consolidation | **This document ceased to be a requirements source.** The last five screens without an owning requirement document — **DB3** (Active Run Monitor), **DB7** (Output Coil Completion & Label), **DB8** (WIP Rejection), **DB10** (Supervisor Shift Summary) and **DB11** (Roll Adjust) — were consolidated into new client-facing specifications in `../MVP-1/RequirementDocuments/`, completing the migration begun on 1 Aug 2026. An authority banner was added at the head, the **Dashboard Inventory gained an "Owning specification" column** (and rows for DB5A, the PLC tag surface and the generation engine, which it had never listed), and each of the fifteen dashboard sections now names its owning document. Bodies are **retained deliberately** as the internal UX and wireframe record — several sections are cited by name from the mockups and the schema seed-data notes — but they are no longer citable as requirements, and the banner states that the owning document wins on any disagreement. Corrected the stale register count in Related Documents (59 → 99) and removed the pointer to the withdrawn consolidated SRS `.docx`. **Two defects were recorded rather than propagated:** the DB11 roll-gap table still shows five rows for three FM2 stands with duplicated position labels, a residue of the 4 Aug correction; and the roll-adjust line scope is stated three different ways across this file (inventory *FL3 Operator*, DB11 *FL1/FL2 Operator*, DB3 action table *FL3 only*), **all of them wrong or incomplete** — `FR-107`–`FR-109` and all three active-run mockups agree on **FL2 and FL3, not FL1**, and the inventory row has been corrected accordingly. |

Added an **order context header** (line · order no · material spec · `n staged / n available / n on order`); progress counts are required because free ordering removes the implicit "what's left" cue a fixed sequence gave. **Alloy and Temper columns removed** — identical on every row of an order, now stated once in that header; Diameter stays per row per `TRV004`. **Location column removed** — not a `TRV004` field, dependent on the open OI-85, and "bay" already means *payoff position* on this screen. |

Both bays now use one state machine and one renderer: all four states apply to either payoff, the wizard disables a bay for being *occupied* rather than for being bay 1, Payoff 2 can be the running bay after a transition, and weld readiness plus Mark-as-welded handle the no-material case. Empty bays state both cold-start routes — stage here, or go straight to rod check-in. |

Supersedes the free-processing-order behaviour recorded the previous day. |

The FL1/FL3 toggle therefore stops being an operator-only control that merely relabels the chrome — it must **reload the bays and the queue**, and what happens to a part-completed wizard needs specifying (**Q39**/**Q37**, **F13**). Wizard step 1 now validates diameter against a **min/max** tolerance, one of **four** pairs (gauge, width, diameter, ovality) whose values are owed by e-mail, so the alloy map stays mock (**Q35**). Not yet applied here: the order-membership rule is knowingly wrong for a **multi-order rod** (**Q70**/**G22**), pending the sequencing answer (**Q73**). |
| Aug 1, 2026 | Analysis team | **Dashboard 2A — weld-readiness strip removed; every control moves onto the bay it acts on.** The 96px strip between the bays and the queue is **gone**. Its narrative was already duplicated by the cards (weight/percentage on the payoff bar, the four weld states in the bay alert, cold start in the empty-bay text); the one sentence no card carried — *induction-weld tail to head* — became the staged card's alert whenever a rod is running. **Mark as welded** moved to the **staged** card (it *is* the incoming rod — `PCI008` defaulting) with all five disabled-tooltips intact; **Welds this run · N** moved to the **active** card (the run *is* the active bay), which means it is now **absent at cold start rather than disabled** — this supersedes **TC-068e**. **Open active run** was removed from the active card: the run monitor is reachable from the app bar and Line Status, and this station's job is staging the *next* rod. The reclaimed 96px + 12px gap went to the queue, which is **~108px taller (about four more rows)**; the screen is now **three body regions, not four**, and still measures exactly 1024px. Button rules formalised: **at most one primary per card** (the active card deliberately has none), a leading icon for actions and a trailing chevron for the two navigations, never both. Queue head gained a standing **"Rods In Queue"** title — it had been an unlabelled empty row since the order-context line and scan box were removed. **Dashboard 2:** *Acknowledge & Begin Check-in* now returns to **Dashboard 2A**, not Dashboard 3, closing the stage → check-in → stage-next loop. Verified in headless Chrome across six viewport heights and all six station states: 1024px exactly, no overflow, no console errors, and the payoff-transition rule (**TC-068**) confirmed live with Payoff 2 running and Payoff 1 staged. |
| Aug 2, 2026 | Analysis team | **New — Dashboard 5A, the FL2 Spool Queue.** FL2 had no view of its own waiting material: FL1 operators have Dashboard 2A's staging queue, and FL2 has no equivalent because `PCI002` excludes it from staging. Separately, `FR-090` has the operator *scan the FL1-printed label* while **Q27** records the client saying they *"select it by spool number"* — both stand, and only the scan had a screen. And "the spool queue", the phrase `FR-326`, `TC-389`, `RodCheckout.md` and phase 7 all use for where an accepted partial spool goes, had **no table, endpoint, screen or status of that name anywhere**. DB5A is all three. **Two modes over one table:** on opening it lists every spool available for processing irrespective of order; entering a spool number resolves that spool's order **server-side in a single call** and narrows the list to that order's spools, marking the scanned one. The **column set never changes between modes**, and a failed scan **leaves the list unchanged** — losing your place to a mistyped digit is the worst thing a screen like this can do. An **unallocated spool is a valid single-row result, not an error** (planning remainders and supervisor-accepted partials legitimately have no order). Deliberately absent: spool age (`Spool` has no creation timestamp, so it is not queryable), location (`Spool.Location` has no writer), and filter/sort furniture. Read-only — it writes nothing. Reached from DB1's nav strip, More Options, the shift-summary spool tile (whose arrow had gone nowhere since it was drawn) and DB5. Backed by a **new** `GET /spools[?spoolAlpha=]`; before it, `POST /checkin/spool` was the only spool endpoint in the contract, so even DB5's scan validated against nothing. Verified in headless Chrome at five viewport sizes: exactly 1024px, scale 1:1 on the panel, no overflow, no console errors, 44 assertions. **Blocked on OQ-27** (what "available" means), **OI-06** (two unmapped status vocabularies), **OQ-76/OI-02** (identifier and format), and — critically — confirmation that **`Spool.OrderNo` is populated from planning**, without which nothing can be resolved. |
| Aug 4, 2026 | Client direction | **FM2 roller sizes corrected — three stands, `S1` 8″, `S2` 6″, `S3` 6″.** Every FM2 component table, roll-adjust panel and generator formula in this document listed **four** FM2 rows: a separate `8" Roller` above `6" Roller S1 / S2 / S3`. That came from this document's own May 21 2026 change-log entry — *"FM2 now has three 6" stands (S1, S2, S3)"* — which was a **misreading** of the client note. **The 8″ roller is S1**, and there is no fourth stand. Corrected here: DB5's pass-schedule table, the FL2/FL3 component strips, the DB1 line-status component list, the DB11 roll-adjust panel and its override history, and the DB9 generator pseudocode (`FM2 S1 gap = gauge × 1.06`, `S2 = × 1.02`, `S3 = × springback` — the multipliers are unchanged; they move from diameter labels to positions, which fixes the earlier state where three formulas covered four stands and the final stand had none). The mandatory stand is stated as **`S3`**, closing **OI-04** — its two "rival" names, `FM2_6inS2` and `6" S3`, were the same physical stand. Edger placement (S2 and S3) and the stand count are unchanged from May 21. Authoritative source remains [`00-foundations.md`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/BusinessRules.md) §0.3; decision **D-26**. |

The packaging rule itself is unchanged; only the undefined attribution is gone. See `Q4` / `OI-67`, which now ask which existing convention is being inherited. |

## [BaseDocuments/FL Alphas Plus - Analysis.md](BaseDocuments/FL%20Alphas%20Plus%20-%20Analysis.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 21, 2026 | Initial document, with the **recovered VBA source** filed beside it as `FL Alphas Plus - Module1.bas` (561 lines, verbatim) and the extractor committed as [`Tools/extract_vba.py`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/extract_vba.py) — verified to reproduce the `.bas` byte-for-byte. `FL Alphas Plus.xlsm` (Tim O'Brien, 25 May 2026) is the tool referenced on the 20 Aug call, and it turns out to be **the only end-to-end implementation of the rod → spool → coil chain that exists anywhere**, in this repository or the delivered contracts.<br><br>⚠ **Recovering it is not a one-liner and the failure mode is silent.** The module text sits at **offset 22177** behind a ~22 KB performance cache, and a scan for the `0x01` container signature finds a **false start at offset 61** that decompresses without error and looks like valid source for several hundred bytes before degrading. The offset must come from the `dir` stream's `MODULEOFFSET` record, which is itself compressed. That is written into both the analysis doc and the tool's docstring, because anyone re-deriving this will hit the same trap.<br><br>**The alpha scheme is derivational and recursive, and it is a third candidate the alpha-format table does not record.** `R00001` → spool `R00001A` → stop `R00001AA`, each generation appending `AlphaLetter(n)` (Excel-column base-26, so 27 → `AA`). **A welded spool carries one alpha per segment in a single cell**, joined by `" - "` — `R00001C - R00002A` — while `" / "` joins consumption facts. ⚠ **The concatenated form is ambiguous by construction**: `R00001AA` is *either* "rod 1 → spool A → stop A" *or* "rod 1 → 27th segment", and it collides outright past 26 segments. `BusinessRules.md` §3.3 records a two-way `SP-#####` / `TS######` conflict; it is three-way, and the third option differs in **cardinality**, not just format.<br><br>**`SegmentType`'s four fields — `Rod`, `Alpha`, `Weight`, `SpoolID` — are confirmed by the client (21 Aug 2026) as their working data structure.** ⚠ **There is not one footage variable in 561 lines**: the client's model is **weight-primary**, which inverts any design that treats footage as the backbone and weight as deferred.<br><br>**Two rules the code states that no repository artifact does.** `' A STOP MAY USE MULTIPLE ALPHAS BUT ONLY FROM THE SAME SPOOL` — enforced by the loop, so **a coil never spans two spools** and one spool reference per output coil is correct *by design*. And `' FL1 … 2. Ensure FL2 can ALWAYS create valid stops` — implemented as `VALIDATE FINAL STOP POSSIBILITY` and `PREVENT INVALID FINAL REMAINDER`, i.e. **FL1 sizing is already constrained by FL2's ability to make shippable coils**, which is the inversion proposed on the 20 Aug call and built three months earlier.<br><br>**Findings that settle open questions.** The stop suffix is a **per-spool stop index shared by every alpha part in that stop** — which explains `R00004AB` existing with no `R00004AA` as *correct*, and means alpha + suffix is **not a unique key**. **LIFO is deliberate code** (`For revIndex = alphaPartCount To 1 Step -1`), so the tool treats *last on, first off* as fact — evidence for `Q45`, though a tool is not a client statement. `efficiency = totalOutput / (rodsUsed × rodWeight)` is a **working definition of metallic yield**, which `OI-60` / `Q11` lack. And the **400 lb overproduction discussed on the call is the anti-remainder rule working as intended** — 40,000 ÷ 1,800 leaves 400 lb, `400 < stopMin`, so the last spool is raised to 800 and the run totals 40,400.<br><br>⚠ **Three defects, two of them latent — do not port them.** (1) `PREVENT INVALID FINAL REMAINDER` **can emit a coil above the customer maximum** to avoid an unshippable tail; latent only because 1,800 divides cleanly by 900. (2) The FL1 sizing loop subtracts `targetSpoolWeight` rather than the `proposedSpool` it just allocated, so its accounting drifts whenever the validate step raises a spool; masked for the same reason. (3) `rodsUsed` is recomputed from output weight instead of counted from the segments the loop already built — benign here, two sources of truth in general.<br><br>⚠ **The workbook's rod weight is 4,000 lb** (`INPUT!C3`) — a **third** figure beside the transcript's ~5,500 lb and the contracts' 8,690–8,840 lb (**`OI-97`**, open). An input cell, so a data variance rather than a defect, but every count in the run depends on it. **Nothing in this document is citable as a requirement.** |

## [LatestDocument/FlatWire_MasterSpecification.md](LatestDocument/FlatWire_MasterSpecification.md)

> This is the one document that kept **two** change logs — an `Appendix B` (`Date | Author | Change`) and a
> later `## Change Log` (`Date | Change`) appended after it. Both are reproduced below, in that order and with
> their own column shapes, because neither superseded the other; entries interleave by date across the two.
> **New entries go in the second table.** The `Appendix B` table-of-contents entry was removed from the
> document in the same pass.

### Appendix B — Change Log

| Date | Author | Change |
|---|---|---|
| Aug 24, 2026 | Client direction | **`OI-121` updated — the alpha-creation *moment* is now agreed by all three UA voices, the *content* still is not.** Asked at the planning end whether alphas are generated at runtime as spools are actually created, Srikanth: *“dynamically is fine … at the **transaction time, not at the check-in time**”*; Bob: *“you have to wait till the transaction”*; Tim: *“I also agree.”* On 20 Aug this was Srikanth's position alone and **Tim's own *pounds-per-alpha* alternative was unadopted**, so this is a genuine change of standing — and it newly rules out **check-in** as the moment. **It settles when, not what**: this item's weight-per-alpha column is untouched, it still inherits **`Q10`** wholesale, and action `A1` (the Excel walkthrough) remains the only thing scheduled against the replacement design. The recording renders “alphas” as *“L pass”* / *“L plus”* — a transcription artefact, **confirmed with Yash on 24 Aug 2026** as *“alphas are generated dynamically at the actual transaction”*. **No other `OI-##` touched and none added.** Full record in [ClientCall_2026-08-24_SyncPlan.md](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-24_SyncPlan.md) §3.1. |
| August 22, 2026 | **Synced with the rod ↔ order design: §5.6 and §5.7 gain the two tables, `CoilOutput.SharedCoilNo` becomes `CoilNo`, and §4 gains a drift finding.** §5.6 records why the split is held in **pounds** — weight is conserved through drawing and rolling, footage is not, and it buys an enforceable single-row `CHECK` the footage version could not express. §5.7 records that `Station`, not `LineId`, is the exclusivity key (FL1 and FL3 share one VPS — the `G21` correction) and that `FlatWireRun.OrderId` now narrows to *“the order at check-in”*.<br><br>⚠ **`FR-541`–`FR-560` were deliberately NOT transcribed into §4, and the reason is a pre-existing defect.** `BusinessRequirements.md` self-declares as *“the `FR-###` register”*; §4 here is a grouped narrative over it — and it **stops at `FR-508` while the register runs to `FR-560`**. Copying one block into a drifted restatement would duplicate the register and deepen the inconsistency, so §4 now states the drift and names `[REQ]` as authoritative on membership. **Reconciling the two is a separate pass, done in one sweep.** Also corrected: a stale *“Foreign keys (all 41)”* heading |
