It now gates **FL1 as well as Phase 9**, because FL1 segment alphas are minted through the same function (`Q57`). A second instance of the same class was found the same day — the scrap-weight path is a scripted wrapper onto an unscripted `united_db` procedure (`Q56`). **`OI-123`** and **`OI-124`** are `High`: `FlatWireRun.OrderId` narrows to *“the order at check-in”* once one run spans two orders, so every reader treating it as *“the order this run produced”* is silently wrong at a boundary; and the spool↔order association has **no positional column**, so a spool crossing a boundary cannot say where the cut goes |
| Aug 20, 2026 | Client direction | **§11 gains seven open items; two are the `G46` backfill and five are new from the 20 Aug call.** Backfilled into §11.2: **`OI-115`** — the shared write set for FL2 spool check-in is undefined and **blocks building, not merely deploying** (`[API §4.6a]` claims *"the same shape as §4.6"* then lists only `FlatWireDB` writes; a spool has no `proddb..coils` row) — and **`OI-116`** (`coil_mill_processing`). ⚠ **`OI-115` is narrowed by the call**: FL2 scans **a coil code off the spool**, not the spool alpha (*"they're going to scan in the coil code that's on the spool"* — furnace-plate behaviour), so the natural `WIPStations.CoilNo` value is the **lead rod alpha**, which every legacy reader would read correctly rather than `SP-00021`, which none would. New: **`OI-118`** — `RodStaging` cannot host an FL2 pre-check-in, because its columns are rod-shaped throughout (inspection columns `RocCheckin.md` §4.3 says are *not performed* on a spool, `IsWelded`, `UnstageKind`, two-bay states, `PayoffPosition NOT NULL`, `CK_RodStaging_LineId IN ('FL1','FL3')`), so the reversal of `FR-031` **adds a table and moves a published count 28 → 31** once `OI-119`/`OI-121`'s associations land — re-derive in **one** sweep against a fresh teardown-and-deploy, never incrementally. **`OI-119`** — `Spool.OrderNo` and `SpoolCheckin.OrderId` are scalar against a confirmed many-order spool, and the `NOT NULL` **already contradicts `SpoolQueue.md` SQ-6**, a pre-existing defect this decision exposes. **`OI-120`** — no physical spool-carrier entity exists anywhere in the 28-table schema; `SpoolConfiguration` is a **size class**, so under the client's *"all one standard size"* it holds exactly one row while the carriers number 30–45, and nothing links a material `Spool` row to the carrier it is wound on. **`OI-121`** — weight per source alpha has no home, and converting `G42`'s footage to weight is **`Q10`**, which carries no recommendation by design. **`OI-122`** — the call is entirely about the welded multi-rod spool while weld capture was descoped 14 Aug, so `FR-172` goes to UAT unexercised and **`G42`'s free window closes at `S1`, 24 Aug**. ⚠ **`FR-031` is reversed but not yet rewritten** — §142, §543, §1566, §2016 and §2290 all still state the superseded rule; they are wave W3 of the ledger. |
| Aug 18, 2026 | §5.13's touchpoint table gains the **eight run-end writes** at FL2/FL3 coil completion (§5.13a / `[INT §8.1]`). **`OI-104` closed** with its original text retained as the audit trail; **`OI-112`**, **`OI-113`**, **`OI-114`** added to §11. |
| 30 Jul 2026 | Consolidation pass | **Footage-to-weight resolved as far as the sources allow, and a new duplication found.** §5.4 gains the weight-derivation subsection: the formula `lb/ft = A × 12ρ` with the round-edge area correction `A = t·w − 0.2146·t²`, the per-alloy `k = 12ρ` constants, reference lb/ft for the line's real cross-sections, and the ruling that **a scalar `LbPerFtFactor` is the wrong shape** and must stay NULL. Density is re-sourced to **`united_db..alloys.alloy_density`**, with its unit **verified as lb/in³** from the PIW formula in `Planning_GetorderminPIW`. §5.12 adds the `alloys` touchpoints plus the **`FlatWireDB..Alloys` view** access pattern — the convention already used by six other databases — and the three type/nullability/join-width mismatches it must absorb. `FR-137`, `FR-332` and `FR-383` re-sourced accordingly; `FR-332a` added to stop Dashboard 7's incorrect `0.069 lb/ft` reaching code. **New issue OI-93:** `AlloyProperty` duplicates four `united_db..alloys` columns, one of which (`Draw_max_reduction`) is the generator's core input held locally only as a provisional seed — while story FW-054 pushes more flat-wire alloy data upstream. **OI-45 rescoped** from "the factor is undefined" to the four sign-offs that actually remain, and a second finding recorded: the ±2 % variance threshold in `FR-153` is unreachable from target dimensions, whose tolerance stack is ±2.6 %. Two malformed table rows in §11.2 (OI-67, OI-69) repaired. Appendix A.10 records the read-only excursion into `ual-database` and what it could not establish. |
| 30 Jul 2026 | Consolidation pass | **Initial creation.** Reconciled every artifact in `Flatwire-planning/` — 19 analysis notes, 18 roadmap/phase files, 8 development-plan documents, 6 schema design docs, 15 DDL/seed scripts, 1 CommonDB script, 27 mockups and 8 shared UI assets, 15 business source documents and 3 SRS revisions — into one self-contained master specification. **Resolutions applied:** the July 26 roadmap and `ShopfloorPlan/*` take precedence over the four April 29–30 documents; the four `REVIEW.md` Tier-1 correctness bugs in `APIContracts.md` are corrected in place (the `/passschedule/generate` worked example recomputed, `RollAdjustTrigger` added to `CheckpointType`, the edge-type vocabulary reduced to one domain set with a display mapping, and the `POST /checkin/rod` body extended with the three `NOT NULL` fields it never sent); the timeline is stated only as 17 Aug → 30 Sep 2026 with the 14 Aug Phase-1 gate; the `Rod`-table divergence is resolved in favour of the DDL and ER document, giving **28 tables** with enforced rod-alpha FKs, and the superseded 21/22-table variant is recorded in §10; the `--fw-*` token prefix is confirmed stale in favour of the `--color-*` semantic system; and Dashboard 2 is fixed as the `- New.html` 6-step wizard with the two earlier layouts listed once as retired. **11 contradictions not present in any existing register were found and recorded** in §11.5. Existing files were not modified. |
| 1 Aug 2026 | Client sync (30 Jul call) | **§11 updated with eleven client answers; body sections deliberately untouched.** Seven open items move — **OI-01** `INFLAT` is set only at check-in (the SRS §4.2 `PCI` data note is superseded; the reqsum / `wip_coil_orders` residual stays open); **OI-07** respecified as **four min/max tolerance pairs** (gauge, width, diameter, ovality) with the values owed by e-mail and nothing to be seeded; **OI-44** pre-check-out needs supervisor approval **only when the rod is welded**, where it is a rejection to `HOLD` — which also settles `WLD011` for the un-staging direction and reverses the 31 Jul removal of that control; **OI-70** WIP rejection releases the blocked staging row; **OI-71** *inverted* — a rod may carry more than one order, so "current order only, else refuse" is wrong for the same-rod successor; **OI-73** *reversed* — off-schedule is no longer an override but an **auto-switch to the correct station**, and the `OffSchedule*` columns are dropped; **OI-74/OI-75** the completion basis is the **customer min/max weight** (the 2,000 lb default is withdrawn) and a short close is an **unplanned stop** on the mill 10-90 pattern, with the mid-run coil break restarting the stop from zero. Four added: **OI-94** (rod scheduled on neither rod line), **OI-95** (multi-order sequencing / MVP scope), **OI-96** (panel resolution — gates Phase 1A against the 14 Aug gate), **OI-97** (bundle gross weight, re-homed from OQ-71). **The body of this document still states the superseded rules** — §4 pre-check-in note, §5 tolerance and staging columns, §6 staging contracts, §7 Dashboard 2A — and is scheduled for wave W6 in [`../BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-07-30_SyncPlan.md`](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-07-30_SyncPlan.md). |
| 1 Aug 2026 | Build decision | **DB6, DB8 and DC are dialogs, not screens.** SPC checkpoint, WIP rejection and die change moved out of `dashboard_6_spc_checkpoint.html` / `dashboard_8_wip_rejection.html` / `dashboard_die_change.html` into `spc_checkpoint.js` / `wip_rejection.js` / `die_change.js`, opened as popups over the screen the operator is already on; the three `.html` files remain as launchers so every reference to them still resolves. **No requirement text changed** — the requirements are unaffected by the container. What changed is that each dialog now receives its material context from the caller instead of hard-coding one run, which is what finally lets the pre-check-in rejection path (Q36 item 3) be represented: no run, no footage position, and submitting releases the bay. Two hand-offs the spec already described also become real — a gauge-drift or size-change die change opens the SPC checkpoint it mandates, and an out-of-spec checkpoint's *suspend material* opens the WIP rejection with the failing reading carried over. DB6 gains a **read-only mode** for DB1's "SPC · Last check … · View →", which reviews a recorded checkpoint rather than opening a blank form. New open item **Q43**: DB1's "WIP Rejection**s**" nav item reads as a list screen that has never been specified. |
| 1 Aug 2026 | Build decision | **Pause/Resume redesigned; DB12 becomes a dialog; OI-14 closed at four outcomes.** The fifteen pause reasons were a flat radio list ~1100px tall — the last screen in the suite on radio buttons, and tall enough that the dialog was scaled on every window, taking its 14px labels under the shopfloor floor. They are now glove-sized cards in one column per category and the dialog fits the 1280×1024 panel at 1:1. **Rod Checkout is no longer a pause reason** — it was the only one of fifteen that did not pause, rendered identically to the fourteen that did — and is now the `CheckOutRod` **resume outcome**, which `POST /run/{runId}/resume` and `CK_RunPauseEvent_Outcome` already accepted. That closes **OI-14** and supersedes **FR-262**. **Rod Checkout itself (DB12) moved into `rod_checkout.js`**, so Mode B opens over the pause that raised it with the frozen footage carried over rather than navigating away and losing the pause. Four correctness fixes went with the redesign, all of which the mockup could previously get wrong: the payload now carries **`ReasonCode` + `ReasonCategory`** rather than a display label (`Other` used to overwrite its own code with the note text); **notes are mandatory on `Other`**, matching `CK_RunPauseEvent_NotesOther`; footage comes from the caller rather than a `#footage-val` element that **does not exist on the FL1 monitor**, where the dialog had always shown “Footage —”; and duration reads **h:mm:ss** past an hour instead of reporting a 90-minute stop as “90:00”. New requirements `FR-261a`–`FR-261b` and `FR-266a`–`FR-266b` record the rules; no existing number was reused. |

### Change Log

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| Aug 6, 2026 | **`CoilTraceability.SpoolAlpha` — the coil → spool edge, which existed nowhere.** `VARCHAR(20)` NULL, `FK_CoilTraceability_Spool` → `Spool.Alpha`, filtered index `IX_CoilTraceability_SpoolAlpha` on `IS NOT NULL`. **`FR-333` requires the chain `rod → spool → coil` and the schema could not produce it:** `CoilOutput` has no spool column, and `RunId` cannot substitute because `SpoolCheckin.RunId` is non-unique and `CoilOutput.RunId` is many-per-run, so the join yields a set of spools rather than the spool. Placed on the range-grained traceability row rather than on `CoilOutput`, since a spool running out mid-coil makes a header column wrong; a separate `SpoolCoilMapping` junction table (the client's May 2026 design) was **rejected** as a footage-less duplicate of an edge this table already owns. `NULL` is correct and common — FL1 standalone and rod-fed FL3 runs have no input spool — so `NOT NULL` was rejected too. Verified on a clean rebuild: spool-fed row accepted, one spool feeding **two** coils accepted (the 1,800 lb → 2 × 900 lb case), one coil spanning **two** spools accepted, a bogus spool rejected by the FK, and `trg_CoilTraceability_NoOverlap` still fires. **Table count unchanged at 27; FKs 42 → 43.** Closes neither `FR-172` (`Spool.ParentRodAlpha` is still singular, so a multi-rod welded spool loses parents) nor `OI-25` (run-cumulative vs coil-local footage frames), on which the attribution's accuracy depends. |
| Aug 6, 2026 | **Die-life columns on `Drawer` (§5 lookup schema).** `Drawer` gains **`LastGrindingFeet`** (`DECIMAL(10,2)` NOT NULL DEFAULT 0) and **`TotalFeetAllowed`** (`DECIMAL(10,2)` NULL), with `CK_Drawer_LastGrindingFeet` (≥ 0) and `CK_Drawer_TotalFeetAllowed` (> 0 when set). Sourced from the `Drawer` sheet of `BaseDocuments/flatwire tables.xlsx` — a **May 2026 proposed design that was never built** — with semantics taken from `DieChangeAndManagement.md` §4.2/§4.4 rather than invented: `LastGrindingFeet` is feet run **since** the last grind (a resettable counter, **not** the reading *at* it, which is what the name suggests), and `TotalFeetAllowed` is the scheduled-life threshold, set lower on a reconditioned die. **No `LastGrindingFeet ≤ TotalFeetAllowed` constraint** — *overdue* is a real state §5.10 displays, not a data error. `TotalFeetAllowed` seeds **NULL**, not an invented limit: **OI-77 / OQ-83** has die-life tracking decided but the thresholds TBD, the same discipline already applied to `AlloyProperty`'s rod-diameter tolerances. **`OI-41` is narrowed, not closed** — `Drawer` is a die-**size** catalogue (13 rows, one per hole diameter), so the counter accumulates against a size rather than a physical tool; registration, condition, status and disposition history are still missing and **Phase 6 still depends on Phase 13**. Nothing maintains the columns automatically either: `DieChangeEvent` identifies its dies by `OldDieSizeIn`/`NewDieSizeIn` decimals with **no `DrawerId` FK**, so run footage cannot be attributed to a `Drawer` row — Maintenance-maintained until that FK or the PLC die counter lands. **Table count unchanged at 27; no new FKs or indexes.** |
| Aug 4, 2026 | **FM2 roller-size correction (client).** FM2 has **three** stands — **S1 = 8", S2 = 6", S3 = 6"** — with edgers at S2 and S3 and S3 final. This is not a digit swap: the repo had modelled a **separate 8" roller upstream of three 6" stands (four components)**, a misreading of the May 21 note recorded in `00-foundations.md` §0.3 as *"three 6-inch stands"*. The 8" roller **is S1**, and `FM2_6inS3` never corresponded to real equipment. New decision **D-26** supersedes **D-21**. Component vocabulary becomes **position-only** — `FM2_S1` / `FM2_S2` / `FM2_S3` — and roll diameter moves into a new **`Stand.RollDiameterIn`** column (12.000 / 8.000 / 6.000 / 6.000), because diameter-in-the-name is what let the error hide for ten weeks. Mapping: `FM2_8in`→`FM2_S1`, `FM2_6inS1`→`FM2_S2`, `FM2_6inS2`→`FM2_S3`; `Stand.Id` 1–4 keep their meaning and Id 5 is removed. `FR-094`, `FR-096`, `FR-297`, `FR-386` and `FR-387` reworded — **`FR-387`'s multipliers are unchanged** (1.06 / 1.02 / springback), they simply move from diameter labels to positions, which incidentally fixes a pre-existing defect where FM2's final stand had no gap formula. `CK_PSC_ComponentName` and `CK_RollOverride_Component` updated; the `Stand` seed drops 5 rows → 4. **Two open items close. `OI-04`** (mandatory stand: `FM2_6inS2` or `6" S3`?) was never a real contradiction — both named the same physical stand, now `FM2_S3`. **`OI-36`** (the final stand has no tag path) is void: the client's published map carries exactly **three** FM2 stations, all `[CONFIRMED]` by observation, so it was complete — the stand with no path was the phantom. PLC stations are renamed to bare position (`FL2.FM2.S1/S2/S3`) pending controls-engineer sign-off as **`PLC-Q04`** / gap **G32**, with the as-published mapping preserved in `[PLC §4.3]`. `PassScheduleGenerationSpec.md` §3.3.5's worked illustration is recomputed at **k = 3**, and §3.3.2's bite condition now states that **S1's larger radius admits ~1.33× the draft** of a 6" stand. |
| Aug 4, 2026 | **HMI/SCADA descoped.** Dashboard 13 (HMI Line Schematic), Dashboard 14 (SCADA Trends) and the Machine View tab on the active run monitor are **withdrawn at client request**. `FR-111`, `FR-112`, `FR-114`, `FR-425`, `FR-440`–`FR-451` and `FR-460`–`FR-470` are marked **withdrawn** — numbers retained, never renumbered — and `FR-113` **reworded**, since it asserted a rule about "the active tab" that outlives the tabs. Both mockups and `HMIAndSCADALayout.md` are deleted. **Descope-ladder rung 7 is removed entirely:** its 67 h stops being *recoverable* effort and becomes *never-planned*, so Phase 5 drops 221 → ~154 h and the programme 3,727 → ~3,660 h, but the ladder loses its largest optional rung and Phase 5 is no longer deferrable. **Nothing structural was removed:** all six run event markers still land on the DB3 traces and no hub event, endpoint, table or column is deleted — every DB13/DB14 reference in the real-time and tag tables was a *consumer* entry, not a row. `Q51`/`OQ-51` is **superseded**, because Dashboard 14 was its answer. §4.21 and §4.22 replaced with withdrawal notices; the approved-screen count drops 22 → **20**. **PLC tag surface consolidated.** The surface existed in six partial, mutually contradictory copies; it now has one home in [`PLCTagSpecification.md`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/PLCTagSpecification.md) (client-facing, `[PLC]`, with its own `PLC-Q##` register) plus `MVP-1/DevelopmentPlan/PLCTagImplementation.md` (internal). This document’s tag-surface section is reduced to a pointer, keeping only what is genuinely its own job. §6.8 keeps only the `INT007` *no new integration layer* sentence. **`OI-36` escalated Medium → Critical** and moved to §11.1: the *write* side already pushes a roll gap and an edge configuration to FM2 6″ S3, for which **no read path exists in any document**, so it blocks the FL2/FL3 push rather than a schematic node — and with DB13 descoped its old *"schematic node"* impact clause is void. §11.5 gains four contradictions surfaced only by reading the six copies as one: speed **target vs limit**, the **edger paths that exist nowhere**, **FM2’s namespace on FL3**, and **units in the path**. |
| Aug 6, 2026 | **Three corrections from the 6 Aug client call.** **(1) `OI-13` is half-closed** — the wire-break *flow* is decided and only the persistence target is missing (gap **G34**). The machine stops, a physical component trips a tag, the system prompts for the stop reason, and on *wire break* it offers **WIP reject** or **weld and continue**. **Weld-and-continue keeps the same alpha** — the wire is still one piece — with the weld recorded at its **footage position** and the material flagged in SCADA; consistent with **Q61** Case 1. Applied to the §3.5 event table, the §4.13 gap note, the §6 traceability table and the **OI-13** register row. The customer-accepts-no-welds branch is new **Q55**. **(2) `D-27`** — the FM2 edgers are **inter-stand** (between S1/S2 and between S2/S3) with **no exit-side edger**, and edging is **vertical profiling roll-forming, not a knifing cut**: nothing is trimmed, width is pushed into length and a slight centre bulge, the first edger converts round to square, and **S3 is a skim pass with no edging**. This gives the generation engine a formula subset it does not have (`PSG-D24`–`D26`, **Q57**) and confirms *“edgers at S2 and S3”* as **entry-side**, leaving the **G29** tag proposals unchanged. **(3) `D-28`** — **FM2 carries two dancers, each with dancer mode and tension mode**, which nothing in the design models and which qualifies `PSM012` (§3.4 glossary amended: the derived-from-speed rule holds **in dancer mode**); open as **Q56**, gap **G35**. Also corroborated without change: **D-26**'s stand sizes, re-stated by the client from the generation spec. §10.2 retitled, since it now carries corrections from three dates. Full record in [`../BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-06_SyncPlan.md`](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-06_SyncPlan.md). |
| Aug 6, 2026 | **`FR-380`–`FR-391` reconciled against the generation specification — new §10.5.** A gap audit of [`PassScheduleGenerationSpec.md`](MVP-2/RequirementDocuments/PassScheduleGenerationSpec.md) (issued as **v1.5**) found that the authority split recorded in `CLAUDE.md` — that document winning on *physics*, this one on *contract shape and arithmetic* — **no longer holds**, because four revisions between 4 and 6 Aug changed the arithmetic itself. **Five contradictions, all in the unsafe direction. `FR-381`:** `D_pre = sqrt(4·t·w/π)` is the **zero-elongation lower bound used as the answer** — `[PSG §3.3.3]` says doing so "produces flat wire that is **under-width**" — and it omits the **round-edge correction**, worth **0.0057″** on the published case, "larger than a typical die increment". **`FR-384`:** snapping to *nearest* violates `R36`/`V39`, which forbid snapping the **final** die below a bounded entry — and since `FR-381` always yields a bounded entry, it violates it on **every** schedule, silently, because the reduction checks test reduction rather than width. **`FR-385`/`FR-387`:** `gauge × springback factor` and the `1.06`/`1.02` multipliers set the gap **above** target where `h₁ = S₀ + F/K` makes the compensation **negative** and **load-dependent**; they also conflate *springback* (a material property) with *mill spring* (a machine stiffness), which `[PSG §3.3.7]` names explicitly as the error that hides the need for mill calibration, carry **no edger term**, and treat `S3` as the gauge stand where `D-27` makes it a **skim**. **`FR-386`:** setting `routeMode = Hybrid` from the aspect-ratio test conflates a **geometric** question with a **metallurgical** one and can select the route with **no intermediate anneal** for material that needs one; its non-hybrid default also bypasses `FM2_S1` and `FM2_S2`, leaving the **skim stand as the only active stand**. **A worked case that fails both documents:** this section's own corrected example — 0.375″ rod to 0.125″ × 0.875″ — has **0.98% of area margin** where the flattening pass needs ~**11% elongation**, so it needs a *larger* rod; the generation spec now rejects it at Step 3A, `FR-383` reads it as a no-draw case. **Contract shape is unaffected** — the envelope, `FR-389`, `FR-390` and `FR-391` all stand — and **the FRs are not rewritten here**; §10.5 records the conflict, adds the row to §10.4, and warns at the head of the FR table that the five formulas must be **rebuilt to the generation spec before `FW-013`**. The 2% no-draw threshold in `FR-383` is a separate case: the generation spec has **not adopted** it (`PSG-D28`) rather than contradicted it. §3.4's **Dancer** entry gains a pointer that **`Q56` and `PSG-Q29` are the same question** — one answer closes both — and that tension **reduces roll separating force**, so it moves the gap as well as the force check. |
| Aug 11, 2026 | **Two mockup filenames normalised — no design change.** `dashboard_3_active_run_v2.html` → **`dashboard_3_active_run.html`**, and `dashboard_2_rod_checkin - New.html` → **`dashboard_2_rod_checkin.html`**. Both suffixes had become misnomers: each marked a revision of a rival layout that no longer exists (the left-rail DB3 screen was withdrawn 1 Aug 2026; the DB2 interim and `- Old.html` layouts were deleted 11 Aug 2026, all recoverable at `2a0426b` / `d79ce78`). **Every reference across the repo was repointed**, and the prose that described the deleted files by these now-reused filenames was rewritten to name the *layout* rather than the *filename* — read "the interim single-page layout" and "the earlier left-rail layout" wherever a retirement is recorded, because the plain filenames now denote the **approved** screens. `- Old.html` remains the only DB2 variant suffix still in use, and it names a deleted file. |
| Aug 11, 2026 | Terminology | **"Transformer line" replaced with "UA's existing coil packaging".** The term was the stated precedent for the 2-coils-per-skid rule but is **defined nowhere in this repository or the wider UAL document tree**. The inheritance claim is kept because it is load-bearing here; the undefined line name is not. The rule is unchanged. `Q4` / `OI-67` now ask which existing convention is actually being inherited. |
| August 15, 2026 | **`OI-37` / `G6` largely closed — the six roles are confirmed as JWT claims.** §8's *"Unverified assumption"* callout is restated as a confirmation: all six exist in `Login` on the standard `ClaimTypes.Role`, **no provisioning story is needed**, and the *"it could block the build"* reading is spent. The §11 `OI-37` row is restated the same way, with its Impact cell struck through. ⚠ **Two things deliberately survive.** The six claim **values** are abbreviated or coded rather than the matrix's labels and the mapping is unsupplied — that gates verification, not construction. And `Engineering/Maintenance` and `QA` remain **inferred** role definitions: confirming a claim exists is not confirming that the capabilities attributed to it are right, so the `[REQ §2.8]` open item is untouched. |

## [LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation.md](LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 24, 2026 | **Worked-examples companions linked from the header and the Related table**, and `Last Updated` bumped. No change to the design: §2.8's two scenarios stand exactly as written and are reproduced verbatim as the new document's §4 and §7. |
| August 22, 2026 | **First issue.** The rod ↔ order many-to-many, which **nothing in the repository persisted** — the pairing existed only implicitly in `united_db..planning_routings`, with `[INT §8]` recording the flat wire side as read-only. Built to [`RodOrderAllocation_DesignPlan.md`](LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation_DesignPlan.md) as the design counterpart to action **`A1`** of the [20 Aug call](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-20_SyncPlan.md), whose `D8` had a rejected design and no adopted replacement.<br><br>**Proposes two tables with full DDL, FKs and indexes** — `RodOrderAllocation` (the plan; superseded and never mutated, so re-planning cannot retro-change what the floor was told) and `RodOrderConsumption` (the actual; immutable once closed) — plus `ORD003`–`ORD017`, a four-tier sequence validator combining rule 5 with **`Q73`**, the handoff state machine, the fulfilment rollup and `OQ-A`–`OQ-N`. **Nothing is applied: no DDL executed, no register id minted.**<br><br>**Three decisions worth knowing before reading any of it.** **(1) One alpha namespace.** FL1 segment alphas and FL2 coil identities are the same strings off the same six-character root, and `CommonDB.dbo.GenerateCoilAlpha` cannot see `FlatWireDB` — so a local counter would hand the same `R00001A` to a spool segment and to a finished coil. Both are now minted through that one function, with **every** prior segment alpha for the rod passed in `@CoilNoToIgnore` (read from `SpoolTraceability`; the shared schema the sweep finds unaided). This decides `SpoolTraceability.ChildAlpha`, deliberately absent pending exactly this cardinality answer, and **replaces its holding comment** rather than annotating it. **(2) `SharedCoilNo` is renamed `CoilNo`, and `CoilAlpha` is retained — so `D5` stands.** An earlier draft withdrew `CoilAlpha` on the grounds that `FW-#####-C##` is **not client-specified** (`FR-330`'s source reads *“Analysis”*, `FR-509` is `[PROPOSED]`, and every `FW-` string in `BaseDocuments/` is a story id) and carries no information `CoilNo` lacks — `D5`'s own example pairs `FW-00421-C01` with `R00421A`, both derived from rod `R00421`. Both observations hold, but the consequence does not: **`CoilNo` is nullable by design** (the value does not exist until the cross-database mint returns, which is why its index is filtered and why it is the retry contract), so as the sole identity it would have forced a `CoilOutput` row to wait on `CommonDB.dbo.GenerateCoilAlpha` — turning a reconciliation problem into a coil-completion outage, given that function's unresolved `coils` reference. **Keeping `CoilAlpha` as the locally-minted `NOT NULL` identity removes the coupling outright**, leaves the `CoilTraceability` FK in place, and closes **`OQ-N`** without the surrogate-key fallback that item proposed. `FR-330`, `FR-509`, `FR-230`, `BusinessRules.md` §3.3 and `APIs.md`'s `"coilAlpha"` payloads are all untouched; the propagation is a rename only. **(3) The split point is held in pounds, not feet** — weight is conserved through drawing and rolling and footage is not (the same 900 lb is ≈ 11,100 ft at FL1 gauge and ≈ 76,300 ft at FL2), which also makes `RodWeightTo − RodWeightFrom = AllocatedWeightLb` a single-row `CHECK` the footage version could not express.<br><br>**Findings on delivered artifacts, recorded because they change what a reader should trust.** `CommonDB.dbo.GenerateCoilAlpha` is **one of seven objects** sharing that job — two implementations and five wrappers, `SlitterDB`→`united_db`→`CommonDB` being a double indirection and `PlanningDB` carrying a divergent copy that sweeps the *modern* planning tables where CommonDB sweeps the *legacy* ones; **neither covers both**. Its `coils` reference **does not resolve in CommonDB in the scripted schema** — a deployment-verification item now gating FL1 as well as Phase 9. Its no-locks design is **structural**: twelve objects across four databases cannot be locked without MSDTC. Suffix exhaustion is a **silent infinite loop**. The caller's *“sixteen tables”* is 14 selects over 12 objects, stated twice. Separately, **`CoilTraceability`'s header justifies its design on a case that cannot happen** — a coil spanning two spools, when welding is FL1-only and `Q17` made FL2 check-in exclusive; the design is right for a different reason (many *rods*, one spool) and the rationale needs correcting. And **`SpoolOrder` loses the order boundary** (no positional columns) while its stated derivation documents a workaround written *because this document's table did not exist* — both free to fix only while nothing writes it. |

## [LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation_DesignPlan.md](LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation_DesignPlan.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 22, 2026 | **First issue.** Design plan for the rod ↔ order many-to-many, which **nothing in the repository persists** — the pairing exists only implicitly in `united_db..planning_routings` and `[INT §8]` records the flat wire side as read-only. Written as the design counterpart to action **`A1`** of the [20 Aug call](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-20_SyncPlan.md), whose `D8` has a rejected design and no adopted replacement. **Proposes two tables** — `RodOrderAllocation` (the plan; re-planned, so superseded and never mutated) and `RodOrderConsumption` (the actual; immutable once closed) — plus `ORD003`–`ORD017`, a four-tier sequence validator combining rule 5 with **`Q73`**, the handoff state machine, and `OQ-A`–`OQ-M`. **Nothing is applied: no DDL, no register entry.**<br><br>**Findings that bear on delivered artifacts.** **(1)** `CommonDB.dbo.GenerateCoilAlpha` is **one of seven objects** sharing that job — two implementations and five wrappers, `SlitterDB`→`united_db`→`CommonDB` being a double indirection and `PlanningDB` carrying a divergent copy that sweeps the *modern* PascalCase planning tables where CommonDB sweeps the *legacy* snake_case ones. **Neither covers both.** Its `coils` reference **does not resolve in CommonDB in the scripted schema** — a deployment-verification item for FL1 and Phase 9. Its no-locks design is **structural**: twelve objects across four databases cannot be locked without MSDTC, which `[ARC §10]` forbids. The caller's *“sixteen tables”* is **14 selects over 12 objects**, stated twice. **(2)** FL1 segment alphas and FL2 shared coil alphas draw on **one namespace**, so both are minted through `GenerateCoilAlpha` with prior alphas passed in `@CoilNoToIgnore` — read from `SpoolTraceability`, because `FlatWireDB` is outside the sweep. This decides `SpoolTraceability.ChildAlpha`, deliberately absent pending exactly this cardinality answer, and **replaces its holding comment** rather than annotating it. **(3)** `CoilTraceability`'s header justifies its design on a case that cannot happen — a coil spanning two spools — since welding is an FL1 operation and `Q17` made FL2 check-in exclusive; the design is right for a different reason (many *rods*, one spool) and the rationale needs correcting. **(4)** The workbook **consumes FIFO and names LIFO**, so which coil the weld lands in is open (`OQ-M`). **(5)** `SpoolOrder` loses the order boundary — no positional columns — and its stated derivation documents a workaround written *because this plan's table did not exist*; both are free to fix only while nothing writes it.<br><br>**Departures from the brief, stated rather than assumed:** the split point is held in **pounds**, not feet, because footage is not conserved through drawing and rolling (the same 900 lb is ≈ 11,100 ft at FL1 gauge and ≈ 76,300 ft at FL2) and weight is the client's own unit — their planner has no footage variable in 561 lines. |

## [LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation_WorkedExamples.md](LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation_WorkedExamples.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 24, 2026 | **Extended to seven scenarios — §10 is the same cardinality as §9 with the order boundary placed *on* a coil cut**, and it is the fix for it. Held constant: the same three rods, the same two welds, the same seven spools, the same 156.25 ft operator delay and therefore the same **12.64 lb** overrun. **Changed: one number in the allocation, `1500` → `1900`.** Because `R00003`'s first 1,000 lb goes onto `SP-00005`, that lands the boundary **900 lb into `SP-00006`** — exactly one coil — and the spool yields **two shippable coils instead of one good and two below the 800 lb minimum**. Material at risk on that spool falls from **900 lb to 12.64 lb**, and `00431` becomes the **only order in the seven traces to reach `Complete`**. The rule generalises: *place an order boundary at a whole multiple of the coil weight, measured from the start of the spool it will fall in.*<br><br>⚠ **Two arithmetic errors in §9 were found by building §10 and are corrected.** Its fulfilment table had `00422` producing **900 lb / one coil** where it produces **1,800 lb / two** — one coil off `SP-00006` and one off `SP-00007` — so total stranded was understated as **812 lb** against the true **1,200 lb**, and the comparison matrix's coil count was **13** against the true **14**. The 900 lb figure quoted in §9's prose is correct and refers to `SP-00006` alone. Separately, `ThresholdFootageFt` is now quoted to **two decimal places** throughout (`18,543.75` and `49,606.25` in §9, `23,488.75` in §10) rather than rounded to 10 ft: the column is `DECIMAL(10,2)` and is computed from pounds, so rounding it made the stated threshold disagree with the stated overrun by 3.75 ft. Container footages stay rounded to 10 ft and still sum exactly within each container; the basis note now states both conventions.<br><br>**Two findings added, no register id minted.** **(1)** ⚠ **The acknowledgement overrun can push a coil past the customer maximum and nothing says what to do** — even with the boundary aligned, the 12.64 lb run between the crossing and the confirmation lands on a coil already at 900 lb, giving **912.64 lb**. Cut at the boundary (over maximum — and the client's own planner has precedent, its anti-remainder rule already exceeds `stopMax`), cut at 900 (**breaks order attribution on the certificate**, the one option to avoid for a welding-wire customer), or keep a remnant. Filed to **`OQ-C`** and **`Q47`**. **(2)** **Boundary alignment is a planning input, not an execution path** — nothing in the build branches on it, §9 and §10 run the identical state machine — so it is mitigation rather than a guarantee: it degrades silently when a rod arrives short or a weld is remade, which is why **`OQ-I`** (close the spool at the acknowledgement) remains the stronger answer and the two are complementary.<br><br>Sections renumbered: the matrix, the findings and the Related table move to §11/§12/§13. The client page gains an Example 7 card with a 900/900 band diagram and a three-option decision table, and its closing question list gains the overrun item; re-verified clean on leakage, self-containment, SVG well-formedness (8 blocks, unique marker ids), the 14 px floor and figure-for-figure parity with the markdown. |
| August 24, 2026 | **First issue — two files, one subject, two audiences.** The `.md` is internal (row-level traces, table and constraint names, register ids); [`RodOrderAllocation_WorkedExamples.html`](LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation_WorkedExamples.html) is a **self-contained client page** — the same six scenarios as proportional rod → spool → coil band diagrams in shop-floor language, inline SVG and inline CSS, no external request of any kind, print-clean. **Neither is citable as a requirement**; the requirements remain `[REQ]` §5.28, `FR-541`–`FR-560` / `ORD003`–`ORD017`.<br><br>**Why it was written.** The mechanics were right and scattered: [`RodOrderAllocation.md`](LatestDocument/RodOrderAllocation.md) §2.8 carried **two** traces, both single-order; `WeldEvent.md` §5 one chain with **no order dimension**; `SpoolCompletionNotification.md` §4.7 the creation of **one** spool. Answering *“two orders share a rod and the spool spans the boundary”* meant assembling five documents, and the client's own planner cannot help — it has **no order entity at all**, one scalar in cell `C4`. Six traces now cover {1 order, 1 rod} × {1 order, *n* rods} × {*n* orders, *n* rods}, welded and not. **§2.8's Scenario A and Scenario B are reproduced exactly as §4 and §7**, including the `R00001D`/`R00001E` and `R00002D`/`R00002E` mints, and given their missing order dimension.<br><br>**Three findings the traces produced, none of them minting a new id.** **(1)** ⚠ **`RodOrderAllocation.md` §2.8 numbers `CoilAlpha` per *spool*** — `FW-00001-C01` for spool 1, `FW-00003-C01` for spool 3 — where `[REQ]`'s alpha table and the master specification both state that ***`FW-#####` is the order*** and `C##` the sequence within it. Not cosmetic: on the 40,000 lb run the sequence is a single `C01`…`C45` across twenty-three spools, against twenty-three separate `C01`s. The new document uses the authoritative form and says so in its §2.1; the `CoilNo` mints, which are the load-bearing half, are unchanged. **(2)** ⚠ **`PinRole='Sole'` is in the `CHECK` list but in none of §3's four tiers** — `partition(order.rods)` covers `PinnedFirst`, `Free`, `Free`+`Partial` and `PinnedLast`, so a `Sole` row matches nothing and `minTier` is undefined over it. Harmless while a `Sole` order has one rod; the validator must special-case it. **(3)** **The recorded weld footage and the material boundary are captured at different moments** — the encoder is read when the operator makes the join at the payoff, but the join passes the counter later, after the outgoing rod's remainder has run through. The traces bound segments at the **material** boundary; nothing states whether the two values coincide. Same class as `OI-25`.<br><br>**What §9 demonstrates, and it is the reason the document earns its place.** A rod split 1,500 / 2,500 lb across two orders, welded on both sides, produces a spool (`SP-00006`) that is **one rod, one segment, two orders, no weld** — and because `CoilOutput.OrderId` is a scalar `NOT NULL`, FL2 must cut at the boundary and yields **two coils below the 800 lb customer minimum from a good 1,800 lb spool**. `SpoolOrder` cannot even tell FL2 where the boundary falls, which is gap **`G48`** made concrete; **`OQ-I`** (close the spool at the acknowledgement?) would remove the failure outright. The same trace shows the **overrun on the outgoing order equalling the shortfall on the incoming one to the pound** — 12.64 lb — which is why two weight latches exist rather than one.<br><br>**Numbers are the shipped run's own** (4,000 lb rod, 1,800 lb spool, 800–900 lb coil, 0.0809 and 0.0118 lb/ft), so the traces reconcile with §2.8 line for line rather than introducing a fourth set. Footages are rounded to 10 ft and **made to sum exactly within every container**; verified by recomputation, along with half-open range contiguity at all three hops and every enumerated value against its live `CHECK` list. ⚠ The 4,000 lb rod is one of three figures in circulation (`OI-97` / `OQ-J`) and every count scales with it. The client file was additionally checked for leakage (no table, column, file, requirement, gap or test identifier reaches it — the only `FW-` strings are coil alphas), self-containment, SVG well-formedness and the 14 px text floor. |

## MVP-1/DBChanges/GapAnalysis.md — RETIRED

*No link: the file was deleted on 23 August 2026 and has no successor document. Recoverable at `ebd0834`. Its findings live as `G49`–`G51` in [GapsRegister.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Development/GapsRegister.md) and `OI-126` in master spec §11.*

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 23, 2026 | **Retired and deleted; recoverable at `ebd0834`.** It carried a competing authority claim — *"the 25 / 33 / 41 / 1 / 1 figures above supersede every other count in the repository"* — which was wrong on three of five figures and did not match its own baseline table two rows above it. It had no shortcode, no owner and no entry in `ProjectPlan/README.md`, so by the repo's own citation rule it was uncitable except by filename and finding letter, which is exactly how its citations broke. **Findings dispositioned:** `A1`/`C5`/`E1`/`E2`/`E4`/`E6` resolved or moot; `A2` resolved (⚠ its two `wip_skids` constraints were **not** unique to it — they are in `OI-104`, `FW-219`'s task plan, `TaskBreakdown.md`, `TrialRunPlan.md` and as named constraints `C7`/`C8` in the implementing procedure); `A3`/`A4`/`C3` already `OI-24`/`OI-99`, `OI-22`/`OI-100`, `OI-41`; `A6`/`D2` duplicates; `E3`/`E5` fixed in the SQL headers; `E7` fixed as the four broken `cd` paths. **`B1`–`B9` → `G49`** (one gap, not nine, so they cannot be triaged apart and half-forgotten), **`C1`/`C2`/`C4` → `G50`**, **`D1` → `G51`**, **`A5` → `OI-126`**. Its *checked-and-found-sound* table migrated into `[DBD §6.9]` |
| August 12, 2026 | **First issue.** Gap analysis of every artifact in `MVP-1/DBChanges` — the five schema documents, the fifteen SQL scripts, the ER documentation and the WIP-stations script — against the SRS, the HLD, all sixteen requirement specifications, `phase-01c`, `PLCTagImplementation.md` §3 and the `OI-##`/`G##` registers. **Establishes the as-built baseline as 25 tables · 33 FKs · 41 indexes (39 non-clustered + 2 filtered-unique) · 1 procedure · 1 trigger**, counted from the scripts, superseding the four different table counts stated across `phase-01c` (24, 25 and 28 in one document), the root `CLAUDE.md` (24) and the ER documentation footer (27). Findings are classified A–E. **Fifteen are new and unregistered**, the most consequential being: **A1** the FW-001/FW-002 shared-schema migration (8 renames, 2 columns, the `INFLAT` status) has **no script at all** against a 14 Aug Phase-1 gate, while every FlatWireDB table has executable DDL; **D1** the 30 Jul asymmetric-tolerance decision reached `AlloyProperty` only, so `SpcMeasurement.InSpec` — `ABS(actual−target) <= tolerance`, **PERSISTED** — silently stores the symmetrised answer, and `SpcCheckpoint.AllInSpec`, `CoilOutput.GaugeInSpec`/`WidthInSpec` and the print-target-or-measured rule all descend from it; **A5** `FR-075`'s per-write tag audit log is the only persistence sink in `PLCTagImplementation.md` §3 with no DDL reference; **B3**/**B4** the `CoilOutput` weight-basis and approval columns and the `RollOverride` revert columns, both decided requirements (`S-18`–`S-24`, `FR-212`) with nowhere to be recorded — the same class of defect as `G24`; and **C1** `AlloyProperty` is orphaned now that its only child left with MVP-2, so the `CHK007`/`FR-065` tolerance lookup can silently miss. Proposes `OI-107`, `OI-108` and `G37`; **creates no register numbers**. Closes with a *checked and found sound* list — MVP split mechanics, idempotency, seed coverage, the FM2 three-stand model, the `RodStaging` invariants, `CoilTraceability.SpoolAlpha` and the closed `G24` — so verified areas are not rediscovered. |

Counts corrected from *"Total tables needed: 22"* to the verified **25 MVP-1 / 28 full** from the clean rebuild of 11 Aug 2026, with a table of what the design gained since April — `PayoffPosition` (**G20**), `RodStaging` (**G19**), `RunReading` (**G3**), the `FlatWireRun` hub of the header/detail split, `Dancer` (**G35**), `AlloyProperty`, and the three MVP-2 `PassSchedule*` tables. **`Rod` is explicitly retained** per `D-04` — `REVIEW.md`'s worklist row telling the reader to drop it is superseded and struck, `G12` having closed on exactly that point. `SpoolCheckin`'s missing owner recorded (created Phase 1C, populated Phase 8). **`RodCheckin`'s NOT NULL conflict verified against the DDL and found narrower than `REVIEW.md` Tier 1 #5 describes**: `InspectionConnectorTag`, `SpcM1In` and `SpcM2In` are genuinely required and unsent, but `SpcOvalityIn` is a **PERSISTED computed column** and can never be supplied — it must not be added to the contract. `united_db` was already absent. |

Replaced by the FK **policy** plus the `sys.foreign_keys` query. The `New Tables` inventory heading corrected to **(24)**; the `Total:` line replaced by a pointer to `[DBD §6.2]`; the enumeration reference banner-ed **derived and non-authoritative — the `CHECK` constraint wins**; and the `Document Type` line restated, because the filename now overstates the scope. **Not renamed** — 17 files cite it. Put on the range-grained row, not `CoilOutput`, so a coil spanning two spools is expressible; a separate `SpoolCoilMapping` junction table was rejected as a weaker duplicate carrying no footage. **Table count unchanged at 27; FKs 42 → 43.** The stated FK total is corrected to the as-built count. |

Added component `FM2_6inS3` (May-21-2026 revision). Documented MMS ID / spool-numbering resolutions. See per-domain docs for column-level changes; schema validated on SQL Server 2019. |

Equipment capability only: `Position`, `Ordinal` (1 = upstream), `SupportsTensionMode`, `DefaultMode`. **No pass schedule column** — whether dancer mode is scheduled or machine-side is unresolved (`OQ-56`), and `PassScheduleComponent` is MVP-2 regardless. Schema is now **28 tables**, 25 of them MVP-1. |

|---|---|

The coil → spool edge did not exist anywhere in the schema, so `FR-333`'s `rod → spool → coil` chain was not satisfiable: `CoilOutput` has no spool column, and `RunId` cannot substitute because `SpoolCheckin.RunId` is non-unique and `CoilOutput.RunId` is many-per-run, so the join returns a set. Placed on the range-grained row rather than on `CoilOutput` so a coil spanning two spools is expressible, and preferred over a separate `SpoolCoilMapping` junction table (the client's May 2026 proposal), which carries no footage. **Table count unchanged at 27; FK count 42 → 43.** Does not close `FR-172` (singular `Spool.ParentRodAlpha`) or `OI-25` (run-cumulative vs coil-local footage frames). |

Added `CK_RodCheckout_ModeP` and `CK_RodCheckout_ModeB` so the per-mode field rules are enforced in the database rather than only stated in prose (`REVIEW.md` #20). Added a modes-compared table. |

Retargeted to `FlatWireDB`. |

Added `CK_RodCheckout_WasWelded` (welded removals are Mode P only), `CK_RodCheckout_Approval` (all-or-nothing stamp), `CK_RodCheckout_ModePWelded` (a welded pre-check-out needs supervisor approval, a documented reason and `NewRodStatus='HOLD'` — it is a rejection, not a return) and `CK_RodCheckout_ModeBApproved` (retro-enforcing OQ-74). Sample data gains a Mode P welded row and an approver on the existing Mode B row, which failed the rebuild without one. DDL rebuild clean, 15 constraint tests pass. |

## [MVP-1/DBChanges/Schema/FlatWireSchema_Runs.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Database/Schema/FlatWireSchema_Runs.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 23, 2026 | **Two tables documented for the first time** — `SpoolStaging` (the FL2 pre-check-in queue; `QueuePosition` is `DECIMAL(9,3)` and **deliberately not UNIQUE**, because a drag-and-drop swap creates a transient duplicate a UNIQUE index rejects and the failure does not surface until the *second* reorder) and `RodOrderConsumption` (**one check-in, N consumption rows** — the client's rule 7 — with **two weight latches** and the overrun between them captured rather than discarded). Header fields standardised |
| July 31, 2026 | **`Blocked` is now a reachable state — staging commits the `RodStaging` row before the inspection gate.** Q72 items 1–2 decided: `Blocked` stays **derived** (`Status='Staged'` + any inspection column `='Fail'`), and `POST /staging/rod` returns `201` with `state:"Blocked"` instead of `422`-and-write-nothing. A failed bundle is already on the payoff, so writing no row reported an occupied bay as free. **Q72 item 3 — what *releases* a blocked row — is the blocking residual**: `Status` has no value for a WIP-rejection outcome, and a fourth value would move the row outside `UX_RodStaging_Bay`'s filter and free a bay that is not free. From the Dashboard 2A UX review finding F2 (`Analysis/Dashboard2A_UXReview.md`, deleted 1 Aug 2026 — in git history at `2a0426b`). |
| July 29, 2026 | **Free rod processing order.** Planned sequence is not enforced — staging validates only current-order membership and availability. Added **`RodStaging.PlannedSeqno` (int NULL)** and redefined `RodSeqno` as the **actual** processing sequence assigned at pre-check-in, monotonic per line; `PlannedSeqno` snapshots the planned position (same snapshot pattern as pass schedule id/version on the run record). Added `CK_RodStaging_SeqPos` / `CK_RodStaging_PlannedSeqPos`; deliberately **no** constraint relating the two, since a difference is the normal case. Also annotated that `PCI008`'s "enforce sequencing" means *physical weld* sequencing, not planned order (**Q70** partly resolved). |
| July 29, 2026 | Documented that the Dashboard 2A / `GET /payoff/status` **`Blocked`** bay state is **derived** (`Status = 'Staged'` + any inspection column `= 'Fail'`), not a fourth `Status` value — adding one would fall outside the `UX_RodStaging_Bay` filter and free a bay that is still physically occupied. Flagged **Q72**: nothing currently writes such a row, because a failed inspection routes straight to WIP Rejection without committing, so `Blocked` is unreachable in practice; also asks whether `InspectionNotes` should be constraint-enforced when any item fails. No DDL change. |
| July 29, 2026 | Added **`RodStaging`** (pre-check-in / payoff staging) implementing SRS §4.2 `PCI001`–`PCI008`, `WLD010` and `TRV004`; two filtered unique indexes enforce one-rod-per-bay and one-bay-per-rod. `FlatWireRunDetail.PayoffPositionId` now has a real FK parent (`PayoffPosition`, REVIEW.md #15). `WeldEvent`: added `OutgoingPayoffPosition`/`IncomingPayoffPosition` + `CK_WeldEvent_PayoffDiff` so the payoff handover is queryable. `RodCheckin` documented as *consuming* the staged row. |
| July 26, 2026 | Added `RunReading` (sampled gauge profile, G3). `FlatWireRun.FootageFt` → `decimal(10,2)`; added audit + `RowVersion` on `FlatWireRun`. `RodCheckin`: computed `SpcOvalityIn`, added `MmsId`/`MmsStatus`/`ScrapBoxRef`. `SpoolCheckin`: added `MmsId`/`MmsStatus`. `RunPauseEvent`: added `OperatorId`/`ResumedBy`, computed `PauseDurationSeconds`, Notes-when-Other CHECK. `WeldEvent`: fail-reason-required CHECK. `RollOverride`: computed `Delta`, ReasonCode CHECK. `DieChangeEvent`: ReasonCode CHECK. Retargeted to `FlatWireDB`. |
| August 1, 2026 | **`RodStaging` — off-schedule columns dropped, blocked-row release added (client 30 Jul 2026).** `OffScheduleOverride`, `ScheduledLineId`, `CK_RodStaging_OffSched` and `CK_RodStaging_OffSchedLine` are **removed**: a rod booked on the other rod line now triggers an **automatic station switch**, not an override (**Q74**). `CK_RodStaging_Override` reverts to keying on `OutOfSequenceOverride` alone; the shared `OverrideBy`/`OverrideAt`/`OverrideReason` trio is retained for it. Added `UnstageKind` (`PreCheckOut` | `WipRejection`) and `WipRejectionId` so a **WIP rejection can release a blocked bay** — the Q72 item-3 residual, decided 30 Jul: the rejection captures the reason, the rod goes to `HOLD`, and the row leaves `Status='Staged'`. Chosen over a fourth `Rejected` status. `CK_RodStaging_RejectLink` uses `ISNULL(...)` after a rebuild showed the bare comparison admitted a rejection link on a still-staged row. Sample data gains a **blocked bay** (R00047, Staged + Fail). DDL rebuild clean, 15 constraint tests pass, 28 tables. |
| Aug 1, 2026 | **Weld quality now gates the `RodStaging` weld columns. No column changes.** `POST /weldevent` becomes the single weld write (`POST /staging/rod/mark-welded` retired) and sets `IsWelded`/`WeldedAt`/`WeldedBy` in the same transaction as the `WeldEvent` row — **on `WeldQuality = 'Pass'` only**. A failed weld writes the event and leaves the staging row un-welded, because the join did not hold and the line cannot transition through it. `CK_RodStaging_Welded` is **unaffected** — a failure sets none of the three, so the all-or-nothing group still holds. Quality is deliberately **not** mirrored onto `RodStaging`: one join, one quality answer, held on `WeldEvent`. Added a note to `WeldEvent` that a fail-then-remake writes **several rows for one physical join**, that no uniqueness constraint exists or should, and that `CoilTraceability`'s per-weld-boundary footage attribution has no rule for a superseded attempt — **Q24** (certificate) and **Q22** (footage). |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/SignalR.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/SignalR.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 22, 2026 | **First entry for this document. Events 13 and 14 added — `OrderAllocationReached` and `OrderAllocationResolved` — and `PP-04` rewritten because it was stale on two counts.** The two events follow event 11's durable contract, and the reason is sharper than the spool case: **the line is still running** between the crossing and the operator's acknowledgement, and the material produced there is the recorded overrun. `OrderAllocationResolved` does something event 12 does not — it reveals the **next order on the same rod**, because the acknowledgement is what starts it and nothing is scanned.<br><br>⚠ **`PP-04` claimed three places carry the event count; checked, only two do.** The master specification has **no event table** — it names events inside requirement text, which states no total — and `BusinessRules.md` §3's list **is gone**, having moved in the ProjectPlan restructure. It also quoted the summary as saying *“9”* when it said **10**, because the 14 Aug promotion to 12 never reached it. The rule is now: **this table and the master spec's summary, together.** Individual event *names* appear in roughly 25 files but none states a total — the distinction `PP-04` was missing |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/Integration.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/Integration.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 22, 2026 | **§8.0a added — FL1 spool completion becomes a cross-database caller.** FL1 segment alphas are minted through `CommonDB.dbo.GenerateCoilAlpha`, the same function FL2 uses, because that function cannot see `FlatWireDB` and a local counter would issue one string for both a spool segment and a finished coil (`Q57`). A read-only scalar call, same instance, **local transaction manager, no MSDTC**. ⚠ **Consequence: FL1 spool completion can no longer be tested on LocalDB**, which has no `CommonDB` — a developer-environment consequence, not a design one. Records what the sweep does and does not cover: twelve objects across four databases, including — verified 22 Aug — `CommonDB..coils`, which **`proddb..coils` is a synonym for**, so it is the same table `FlatWire_CompleteCoilOnSkid` writes to. It does **not** cover `FlatWireDB`, which is why the ignore list is load-bearing and why a third-party caller can still collide (**`Q59`**) |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/Services.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/Services.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 22, 2026 | **First entry for this document. §3.2d `IFootageWeightConverter` and §3.2e the allocation services added.** The line measures feet and planning allocates pounds; exactly one component converts between them. **The formula is not a variable** — `FR-137` and `[DBD §6.6]` specify it and `FR-332a` bans a wrong variant — so what configuration selects is the **dimensional basis** (`Q10` / `OI-45`), and `Describe()` exists so every consumption row persists the basis and factor it used. That is the whole reason this is a service and not an extension method: a later answer to `Q10` must not retro-change a record whose weight a customer relies on. ⚠ Registering two implementations would let two answers to `Q10` coexist — the failure it exists to prevent. §3.2e names the three cross-row invariants that live in the domain because SQL Server has no exclusion constraint, and records that the sequence validator **must not enumerate** — `|freeFull|! × |freePartial|!` explodes |

## [04-APIContract.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/APIs.md)

> ⚠ **This document has TWO sections in this file** — here and at the other heading that links to [`MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/APIs.md`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/APIs.md) `[API]`. Both are history and both are kept, because the headings name the documents as they stood. **Append new rows to the LATER of the two**, which is the one whose heading carries the current path. *(Flagged 25 Aug 2026: the convention says “append to that doc’s section”, which was ambiguous for five documents.)*

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 22, 2026 | **§4.20 `GET /rod/{alpha}/orders` and §4.21 `POST /order/{orderNo}/complete` added**, for the rod ↔ order allocation. §4.20 returns the rod's **active allocation set** with each pairing's weight and tier — which is what makes the sequence validation and the order-set membership check possible; a consumer reading a scalar order from `GET /rod/{alpha}` is reading a rod that happens to have one. §4.21 is **the only thing that closes an order**, and it is not a confirmation of a system decision: the hub raises `OrderAllocationReached`, the operator decides. It writes the acknowledgement stamps, the **second weight latch**, the overrun between the two, and the conversion basis and factor used — then opens the next pairing on the same rod, **atomically**, or the station exclusivity index rejects the handover. `422` where the incoming order's pass schedule differs (`ORD015`), which refuses the *mounted handoff* rather than the order. `PP-04` updated: the hub event count is **14** |
| Aug 22, 2026 | **The `POST /coil/complete` worked example was losing a foot, and it is the example a developer copies.** Two `CoilTraceability` segments were shown as `1–1850` and `1851–…`, but the ranges are **half-open `[From, To)`** — as `trg_CoilTraceability_NoOverlap` enforces by joining `a.From < b.To AND b.From < a.To`. Written that way, foot **1850** belongs to neither segment and the payload fails `TC-617`'s coverage assertion. Corrected to `footageFrom: 1850`. The column comments in `FlatWire_DDL_05_QualityOutput.sql` said *“(inclusive)”* against the same half-open trigger and were corrected with it. |
| Aug 18, 2026 | `POST /coil/complete`'s side effects gain **the shared half** — eight objects written through `FlatWire_CompleteCoilOnSkid`, and the three rules the handler must implement: persist `SharedCoilNo` immediately, never swallow the procedure's error, one call per coil (`coils_iud_tg` is single-row only). **The response is unchanged** — `SharedCoilNo` is an internal mapping, not a contract field. |
| August 2, 2026 | Added **`GET /spools[?spoolAlpha=]`** for Dashboard 5A (FL2 Spool Queue). One endpoint, two modes, identical `{ order, spools[] }` shape: without the parameter it lists everything available for processing with a null order; with it, **the backend resolves that spool's order** and returns the order plus that order's spools in one response. Before this, **`POST /checkin/spool` was the only spool endpoint in the contract** — there was no read at all, not even a single-spool lookup, so Dashboard 5's scan field validated against nothing. Four contract points are easy to get wrong and are called out inline: `404` is for an unknown alpha only and an **unallocated spool is a `200` with a null order**; `gaugeIn`/`widthIn` must come from the source FL1 run because `Spool.GaugeIn`/`WidthIn` are set at check-in and are null for every row returned; `sourceRodAlphas` is a list from `CoilTraceability`/`WeldEvent` because `Spool` holds only two single-valued rod FKs; and the `order` block is a cross-database read. **Also recommends an index on `Spool.OrderNo`**, which is unindexed today. The availability rule is `RECEIVED` + `STAGED` as a **proposal** — OQ-27 leaves it undefined. |
| July 31, 2026 | **Failed staging inspection now returns `201 Created` with `state: "Blocked"`, not `422`.** The old behaviour wrote no row at all, so a bundle that failed inspection — already physically on the payoff, since bundles are not unbanded until positioned there — left `GET /payoff/status` reporting the occupied bay as `NotStaged`, Dashboard 2A offering it as "Empty — available", and the next rod stageable into it. It also made the `Blocked` state unreachable despite being implemented across the API enum, the schema and the whole of Dashboard 2A. Staging now commits the row before the inspection gate. `CHK010` is unchanged — no bypass, WIP Rejection is still the only forward path. Closes **Q36** items 1–2; item 3 (*what releases a blocked row*) is the blocking residual, and two consequences are recorded but untraced (`TRV009` traveler class, `Available` projection exclusion). From the Dashboard 2A UX review finding F2 (`Analysis/Dashboard2A_UXReview.md`, deleted 1 Aug 2026 — in git history at `2a0426b`). |
| July 29, 2026 | Added the **Pre-Check-in / Payoff Staging** section: `GET /payoff/status`, `POST /staging/rod`, `POST /staging/rod/unstage`, `POST /staging/rod/mark-welded`, `GET /staging/queue`, plus the `PayoffStateChanged` hub event and `PayoffStagingController`. Extended `GET /rod/{alpha}` with `footageRunToDate`, `remainingWeightEstimateLb`, `stagedPayoffPosition`, `isWelded` — without them the `PRC007` carry-forward gate cannot be enforced. Documented check-in *consuming* the staged row. |
| April 30, 2026 | Original contract set. **Known correctness bugs catalogued in `REVIEW.md` Tier 1** — notably #37 (`RodCheckin` NOT NULL columns the check-in command never sends), the `/passschedule/generate` worked example, a missing `CheckpointType` value, and three edge-type vocabularies. Cross-check before implementing. |
| August 1, 2026 | **30 Jul client decisions applied.** **Wrong station is corrected, not authorised:** the `offSchedule` override object is **removed** from `POST /staging/rod` — the client resolves `scheduledLineId` from `GET /rod/{alpha}` and **switches station**, and a mismatched POST returns `409` with `correctLineId` for the client to switch and re-post (Q38). Out-of-sequence remains the one supervisor-authorised deviation. **`POST /staging/rod` no longer sets `coils.coil_status`** — `INFLAT` is set at check-in (Q68); the reqsum / `wip_coil_orders` half of that side effect is still open. **The blocked-row release is specified:** `POST /wipreject` releases the staging row (`Status → 'Unstaged'`, `UnstageKind = 'WipRejection'`, `WipRejectionId`) and broadcasts `PayoffStateChanged` — the Q36 item-3 residual, and the only thing that clears a `Blocked` bay. **`POST /staging/rod/unstage` gains a weld-dependent approval:** unwelded is operator-only, **welded requires a supervisor override and puts the rod on `HOLD`** because removal means cutting the material (Q69/Q72) — restoring, behind that gate, the control removed on 31 Jul. **`CHK007` becomes a min/max band** read from `AlloyProperty`, currently unseeded pending the values owed by e-mail (Q35). Flagged **G22**: the order-membership `409` is knowingly wrong for a **multi-order rod** (Q70) pending the Q73 sequencing answer, and **Q40** (order scheduled on neither rod line) has no station to switch to and no specified path. |
| August 1, 2026 | **New read endpoint `GET /run/{runId}/weldevents`** — every weld recorded against one run, backing the read-only **Welds this run** dialog on Dashboard 2A (`PCI021`). It replaces a mislabelled "Weld event log" link that navigated to Dashboard 4, which is the weld *capture form* and not a log of anything. Deliberately **not** served by widening `GET /run/active.weldEvents`: that array is a trimmed marker list feeding Dashboard 3's gauge trace, carries no quality/operator/weld type, and Dashboard 2A never calls it. **Read-only by design** — no PUT/PATCH/DELETE counterpart, because reversing a recorded weld in place is `WLD011` and is unspecified. Empty array (not `404`) for a run with no welds yet. Placed in **S3** with Dashboard 2A even though `POST /weldevent` writes its rows in **S4**, so the screen is complete at the phase-4 gate; the S3 stub must return non-empty sample data. Closes `WeldEventPopupPlan.md` **Q-W3** and **withdraws the proposed gap G25**. |
| August 1, 2026 | **Weld quality is captured at the weld, and `POST /staging/rod/mark-welded` is retired.** Dashboard 2A's Mark as welded dialog now captures Pass/Fail with a mandatory reason on Fail (`WLD013`, the same six reasons as Dashboard 4). Quality was the **only** NOT NULL `WeldEvent` column that dialog was missing — it already captured both rod alphas, weld type and footage — so the two screens now compose the same row and **`POST /weldevent` becomes the single weld write**, setting `RodStaging.IsWelded`/`WeldedAt`/`WeldedBy` in the same transaction. This is decision **D-A** in `WeldEventPopupPlan.md` and it closes **Q-W1**; it needs **no schema change**. **The `RodStaging` write is conditional:** a `Pass` sets the weld columns, a **`Fail` writes the `WeldEvent` row and leaves the rod staged and un-welded** — the join did not hold, so flagging it welded would assert a continuous feed that does not exist; the operator remakes the weld. Added the `422` for a missing fail reason and an `isWelded` field on the response so the client never infers bay state from quality. **Consequence — widens `Q13` rather than adding a question:** a fail-then-remake produces several `WeldEvent` rows for one physical join, which is the re-weld-on-certificate question `Q13` already asks; footage attribution across the two boundaries is `Q11`. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | **Demoted below `ProjectPlan/04-APIContract.md` and reconciled to the phase model.** Two API contract documents had coexisted since 4 Aug with no statement of which governed; **the 4 Aug document is now the contract of record** and this one is an elaboration that is not citable — retained because it is 2.6× longer and carries request/response detail, worked examples and project structure the owner does not, all of which should be **migrated into the owner rather than cited from here**. The `S1`–`S5` delivery model is replaced throughout by the roadmap's **14 phases**, sourced from `back-matter.md` Appendix A; the five sprint sections became phase-labelled groups. `/passschedule/**` and `/shiftsummary` are **marked MVP-2 in place**. Correctness fixes: **`RollAdjustTrigger` added to `CheckpointType`** (`POST /rolloverride` already wrote it); the six-member `LineStatus` enum renamed **`LineOperatingState`** and the hub event **`LineStateChanged`** per `[PLC §6]`, with a standing note **not to add a seventh member** since the absence of `Stopped` is deliberate and the mapping is configuration (`PLC-Q01`); `PushPassSchedule`'s first parameter is **`scheduleId`**; `ComponentState` is the three-value enum; `EdgeType` is `Round`/`Square` with display labels mapped at the UI. The hub event list was verified complete at nine events. **Three missing endpoint families — alloy-lookup CRUD, roll-override revert (`OI-32`) and the alert lifecycle (`OI-28`) — are recorded as gaps rather than drafted**, because inventing a shape here would create exactly the second unowned contract this document was demoted for. **`POST /passschedule/generate`'s worked example is deliberately left uncorrected** and is marked as such. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/BusinessRules.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/BusinessRules.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 22, 2026 | **First entry for this document, and §3.3a added — rod ↔ order cardinality and the four-tier sequence it forces.** States the full cardinality (rod→many orders, spool→many rods, spool→many orders, FL2 output exactly one order at a time), that orders are consumed one at a time per **rod payoff station** — FL1/FL3, FL2's equivalent being a separate rule — and that a shared rod is **position-locked**: last of the outgoing order, first of the incoming one. The partition is **pinned-first → free full → free partial → pinned-last**, so the legal sequence count is `|free full|! × |free partial|!`. **Two traps are called out:** a rod may be pinned at **both** ends — an order lying wholly inside one rod — so the end tiers are not necessarily different rods; and `Q73`'s *“multi-order coils last”* and the *“last rod of the outgoing order”* rule are **the same statement** read from opposite sides, so reading `Q73` alone makes the pinned-**first** case look like a violation of it |

---

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/ProcessFlows.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/ProcessFlows.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 22, 2026 | **First entry for this document, and §4.4a added — the order boundary as a handoff inside one mount.** The one flow in which an operator action closes one order and starts the next **with no material movement at all**: rod checked in once → allocation reached, detected **server-side** on the footage stream → durable notification → **the line keeps running** → operator marks complete → next order on the same rod, same run. Carries the trigger-and-writes table, including that the weight is **latched twice** — at the crossing and at the acknowledgement — because the difference is the overrun and it must be recorded rather than discarded. **Three consequences that surprise people are stated outright:** one run spans both orders (the schema forces it — a run is one row per check-in event and a check-in points at exactly one run), the PLC tags are pushed **once**, and **FL1 does not wait for FL2** — an order is *consumed* at FL1 and *produced* at FL2, and is complete only when both are true |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-080-FlatWireHub.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-080-FlatWireHub.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-080`, the one RT-stream story in this set.** Records two decisions. **`P-21`:** the MessagePack package is **named in no specification** — `[SIG §4.1]` prescribes `AddSignalR().AddMessagePackProtocol()` without saying what supplies it, and `Microsoft.AspNetCore.SignalR.Protocols.MessagePack` is absent from `API/Directory.Packages.props`, where central package management requires it to be added. Treated as **measure-first** per `[SIG §4.1]` and `G10`: build batching and decimation first — *"the real win"* by `[SIG]`'s own judgement — and put MessagePack behind the `FW-144` switch, because it is a new dependency on **both** ends (`@microsoft/signalr-protocol-msgpack` is otherwise unused in the repository). **`P-22`:** `Hub<IFlatWireClient>` cannot compile without the interface, yet `IFlatWireClient` is `FW-149`'s deliverable and `FW-149` lists `FW-080` as *its* dependency — read literally, neither can start. Resolved by defining the interface in `FlatWire.Domain` in this story's first commit with the **full** published set (twelve events + six run-event markers), leaving `FW-149` the payload types and the match against `FW-136`; a partial interface widened later would be a breaking change under `[API §8]`. Carries the four traps that each produce a working-looking but wrong hub: **no existing hub is a template** (`D-05` — `CoilDataHub`/`OPCManagerHub`/`supervisor-monitor-hub` were reviewed and rejected); **reconnect is a deliverable, not a test** (backoff **plus line-group re-join**, cached state behind a banner, never a blank screen); **`SpoolCompletionPromptDue` is the only non-fire-and-forget event**, persisted to `FlatWireRun`'s five prompt columns — which only landed 15 Aug 2026 as `G38`, before which it was unbuildable — and re-delivered on re-join per `TC-173`; and **`PayoffStateChanged` must never enter the ~100 ms batch**. Notes that `enum LineState` has **no `Stopped` member** deliberately, so the `RUNNING → STOPPED` edge resolves through the configurable `LineStateMap` (`[PLC §6]`) and never by adding a value (`PLC-Q01`). Flags two stale citations: AC 4 cites *"Foundations §0.4, decision 4"* against `00-foundations.md`, **dissolved 13 Aug 2026** — the rule is now `[SIG §4]`/`[SIG §5]` and `[ARC §13.1]` `D-05` — and the *"9 hub events"* figure, which predates `PayoffStateChanged` (ten by `[API §10.3]`, twelve on the interface by `[SIG §5.2]`). Both blockers restated: `G10` (IIS WebSockets) and **`G9`/`OI-34`**, where *"the channel cannot be sized and the load test cannot fail"* — build to the stated cadence, record what it achieves, invent no target. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-082-PLC-Tag-Push-On-Acknowledgement.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-082-PLC-Tag-Push-On-Acknowledgement.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the trial-path plan for the moment software configures a machine.** Frames the story by its three rules of **restraint**: the push writes **six value groups and no other tag**; it is triggered **only by the step-2 acknowledgement**, never at schedule generate or apply time and never at pre-check-in; and on failure it issues **compensating re-clears and emits no `LineStatus` broadcast** — a broadcast after a failed push tells every screen the line is running a schedule it never received. **`P-41`** resolves `G29`, which leaves the payload carrying a value with nowhere to write it — **no edger tag path exists on any line, yet edge type is one of the six groups**: do not drop it (it ships a machine configured without it), do not guess a path (`[PLC]` owns every tag string and **`G33` means a wrong path fails silently**, reporting success while the line keeps its previous settings), but **carry it, resolve the path from configuration, and fail the push if unresolved** — converting a silent omission into a visible one. ⚠ Notes that **`SimulatePLCTagPush` masks this until commissioning**, since a simulated write cannot tell that a path is fictional, so **`C1`/`C11` is the first real test**. Also records why the card's **`FW-010` dependency reaches into MVP-2 and is not a blocker**: `D-31` moved the schedule tables into MVP-1 with a real FK and the seed carries three active schedules, so the story can **read** one today — but **`OI-110` stands, nothing in MVP-1 populates them in production**. |
