## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-138-Fifteen-Thin-Controllers.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-138-Fifteen-Thin-Controllers.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-138`, the second document in `Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/`.** **The headline finding is `P-06`: the framework cannot produce the contracted response envelope, and no specification records it.** `[API §1.2]` requires `{data, success, errors[]}` with `errors` *"always an array, never a bare string"*, `[API §1.8]` requires a **string** machine-readable code alongside it, and `[API §1.3]` requires real status codes with the 409/422 split called load-bearing. Verified against `c:\UAL\ual-api` on 15 Aug 2026, **all three framework paths fail that contract**: `ActionResultBase<T>` (`UA.Framework.Common.Model`) carries `Data`/`Success`/`ErrorCode`/`ErrorDescription` with **no `errors` array** and an **`int`** code where the catalogue is strings; `UAController.Error(HttpStatusCode, string)` returns the right status but a `{ErrorMessage, RequestUri}` body with no `success` and no `errors[]`; and **`CoilCheckin`'s controller pattern returns `Ok(actionResultBase)` from its catch block — HTTP 200 on failure** — which contradicts `[API §1.3]` outright and destroys the 409/422 split. **Resolved toward the contract**: define `FlatWireResult<T>` in `FlatWire.Domain` (`Data` · `Success` · `Errors` `string[]` · `ErrorCode` `string`) returned with the correct status, because the wire shape is what every 1A screen is coded against and `[API §8]` makes changing it a breaking change; `[ARC §2.2]`'s binding of `CoilCheckin` for *controller shape* is kept in full and diverged from only where the template contradicts `[API §1.3]`. **`FW-146` inherits it** — that story owns the global middleware and must neither redefine the envelope nor re-map the `201`/`Blocked` response. If ratification prefers `ActionResultBase<T>` instead, `[API §1.2]` must change, which is a contract change across 1A and not a backend detail. **Second finding — the story is smaller than its title, and the arithmetic appears in no specification.** `FW-N04` already delivered the fifteen shells, so acceptance criterion 1 is satisfied before this story starts; of `[API §3.2]`'s **32 live rows**, seven are outside MVP-1 (the six pass-schedule endpoints and `GET /shiftsummary`) and `GET /health` has **no controller** (`FW-148`), leaving **24 stub endpoints across 13 controllers** — `PassScheduleController` and `ShiftSummaryController` get classes and **no actions** (`P-07`, deliberately absent rather than stubbed, since a stub in a scope MVP-1 does not own is a de-stub liability). `P-08` keeps fixtures per controller so the de-stub pass has one seam each. Records the three behaviours a naive stub gets wrong — **`POST /staging/rod` returning `201 Created` with `state:"Blocked"`** on inspection failure (committed before the inspection gate, `Blocked` derived and never stored, `POST /wipreject` the only thing that clears it), **FL2's `null` live gauge and width**, and a `Draft` schedule refused with `SCHEDULE_NOT_ACTIVE`/`422` — and warns that **`PS-1100-FL1-003` is the *negative* fixture**, the FL1 happy path being `PS-1100-FL1-001`. Notes that `[API §7]` reads as though there is one stub when there are two: §7.1's `useMockData` mechanism is Angular, while §7.2's obligations bind both, of which **four are this story's and the mock SignalR stream is `FW-080`'s**. **§8.1 flags four stale citations** *(seven after the 15 Aug fixture correction below)*, one of them internal to the story card: `[API]`'s front matter still says *"31 live rows, of which MVP-1 implements 24"* against §3.2's 32/25; `[SSP]`'s *"thirteen thin controllers"* at 35 h; the card's own **rate-card basis line saying "14 controllers … → 42 h" against its 45 h header** (15 × 3 h), which was not restated when the count went to fifteen; and anything routing spool completion through `CoilController` rather than `SpoolController` endpoint 16b. **No hour cell was restated.** The `P-##` decision series is declared **continuous across the folder**, and `FW-N04` §5 and §7 were updated to say so and to carry the `P-06` warning. |
| August 15, 2026 | **⚠ The FL2 happy-path fixture was wrong and is corrected — `G40`.** §4 step 4 reproduced `[API §7.2]`'s callout naming **`PS-1100-FL2-001`** as the FL2 happy path, `Active`. **The seeded row is `Hybrid`, `Inactive`**: `G40` demoted it on 15 Aug so **`PS-1100-FL2-002`** (Standalone, Active) could exist, because `UX_PassSchedule_OneActivePerLineAlloy` permits exactly one Active schedule per line + alloy and the two cannot coexist. `-001` is kept, not deleted — it is the Hybrid-FL2 coverage case and `RUN-0004` still references it. The fixture note is replaced by a **six-row table** naming three happy paths and three negatives, and §8.1 gains three rows: `[API §7.2]`'s callout, **`[API §4.6a]`'s `POST /checkin/spool` worked example** — which shows a check-in against `-001` returning `"success": true` and would be refused **twice over**, by `SCHEDULE_NOT_ACTIVE`/`422` and by `FR-091`'s route-mode validation — and the seed file's own §8 banner comment, which reads *"Hybrid · Active"* above a row that says `Inactive`. **Also distributed in: the four cross-cutting conventions no other 1B story owns** (`FR-174` server-side timestamps, pagination's 50/200 defaults and the two non-paginated endpoints, the unit-suffix rule, and `[API §8]`'s breaking-change split) · **§3.0a, the trial's eight-controller subset**, of which only **six serve a screen** — `Lines` lost `/lines/status` with DB1 and `Coil` is Phase 9, both kept as scaffolds on purpose · and **§6.1, the QA0 walkthrough checklist** with its seven items mapped to owning plans and **`reviewer: TBD`**, since *"a layer with no automated tests and no named reviewer has no verification at all."* |
| August 15, 2026 | **`G6` answered — no effect here, recorded so.** This story's 24 actions carry bare `[Authorize]`, which was unaffected whichever way the question fell; role policies arrive in `FW-145`. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-139-MediatR-Registration-And-Pipeline-Behaviours.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-139-MediatR-Registration-And-Pipeline-Behaviours.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-139`.** Records that **UAL does not call `AddMediatR`**: registration is a **Scrutor assembly scan** (`services.Scan(...).RegisterHandlers(...)` with `RegistrationStrategy.Append`), kept as `P-09` because `[ARC §2.2]` binds `CoilCheckin` for MediatR wiring and consistency with eleven sibling services outweighs `AddMediatR`'s ergonomics. **The Append strategy makes duplicate handlers a live hazard and the template has one** — `CoilCheckin` declares `GetCoilsQueryHandler` twice, nested and standalone, in two namespaces, both handling the same request, and Append registers both; hence the rule to nest every handler inside its command/query and never create a standalone handler file. **`P-10`:** `TransactionBehaviour` **cannot be inherited** — the `UATemplate` and `OPCConnection` copies both take the **concrete** `DbContext` in their primary constructor — so it lands with `FW-142` rather than here, even though this story's `Dependencies` field reads `FW-N04` only; the other two behaviours (`LoggingBehavior`, `ValidatorBehavior`) ship in `UA.Framework.API` and must not be rewritten. Records that `[SVC §3.2]`'s bar on SignalR types in Application is **what forces domain events** (`[SVC §3.2c]`) rather than an `IHubContext` call in a handler. Adds one verification step worth doing deliberately: resolve `IEnumerable<IRequestHandler<,>>` at startup and assert exactly one registration per request type, since a duplicate is silent until dispatch. Flags that the AC cites **`04-APIContract.md`**, absorbed into `Backend/APIs.md` on 13 Aug 2026, and that copying `CoilCheckin.API/Program.cs` wholesale gets a file registering **no** `IPipelineBehavior` at all. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-140-DI-Registration-And-Stub-Swap.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-140-DI-Registration-And-Stub-Swap.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-140`.** **The card names the flag `useStub` twice and it is `useMockData`** — `phase-01b` L84 says so in as many words, and L171's exit criterion uses it too; a backend flag with a different name means the two halves of the stub contract are configured by two switches that can disagree. Recorded as `P-11`, which also fixes the mechanism: **branch at registration time in `Program.cs`**, not behind a per-request factory, because the choice is environmental, cannot change within a process, and a factory would thread through every handler — the coupling the interface exists to avoid. The consequence is accepted deliberately: stub implementations ship in the production binary, as they already do in `CoilCheckin`'s pattern, because conditional compilation would make the de-stub pass (`[API §7.3]`) invisible to a code search. **The document's main contribution is §2.2: three independent simulation switches that are routinely collapsed into one** — `useMockData` (stub vs real *services*, this story), **`SimulatePLCTagPush`** (whether a PLC *write* reaches a controller; **`true` in every environment until commissioning**, selected *by configuration, not by call site* — **not a dev-only mode**), and **`IReadingSource`** (which *telemetry feed* is live, real ingest vs simulator, two implementations of one interface neither aware of the other). A build that ties any of the three to `IsDevelopment()` has merged switches that must stay separate. Verification is pinned on the one criterion that matters to 1A: all 24 `FW-138` endpoints must return contracted shapes **with no database reachable**. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-141-Repository-Layer.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-141-Repository-Layer.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-141`, whose acceptance criteria name the wrong repositories.** The card asks for `PassScheduleRepository`, `RodRepository`, `RunRepository`, `CoilRepository` *"and siblings"*; `phase-01b` L85 and `[SVC §3.2a]` specify **seven, one per aggregate root** — `FlatWireRun`, `RodStaging`, `WeldEvent`, `Spool`, `CoilOutput`, `RodCheckout`, `WipRejection` — and **`RunReading`, `Rod` and `PassSchedule` get none at all**. **Two of the four named repositories must not be built** (`P-12`): a `PassScheduleRepository` would create a write path into a table MVP-1 must never author (`OI-110` already records that nothing in MVP-1 populates it in production), and a `RodRepository` as an aggregate repository would imply `Rod` is a root, which `D-04` and `[SVC §3.2a]` both deny. **Rod work is relocated, not cancelled** — it is the most externally-coupled data access in the service, reading `united_db..planning_routings` (genuinely cross-database, **columns unmapped**, `OI-33`) and `coils`, and **writing `actual_start_date` back to `planning_routings` *and* `routings`** per `FR-077`, all via Dapper. Records that repositories are **keyed on the alpha value object** — `GetByAlpha(RunAlpha)`, not `GetById(int)` — because the surrogate is not the identity, and the trap inside that trap: **`Entity.Equals()` and `IsTransient()` operate on `Id`, so equality is surrogate-based** and two instances with the same alpha do not compare equal before both are persisted. Notes that *"the pass schedule read is cross-database"* is stale — it became a **local** query under `D-31` — and that only `planning_routings` remains genuinely cross-DB. Four stale citations flagged, including the withdrawn unit-test criterion and a rate-card basis pricing **five** repositories against a 28 h header for what is now seven. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-142-Dapper-EF-And-FlatWireDbContext.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-142-Dapper-EF-And-FlatWireDbContext.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-142`, two of whose four acceptance criteria were reversed by `D-31` on the day they were written.** AC 2's *"all **25 MVP-1 tables** … 28 in the full design"* is now **28, one number** (`phase-01b` L47, verified on a live deploy alongside 43 FKs · 47 index statements · 1 procedure · 1 trigger); AC 4's *"the three `PassSchedule*` tables are not mapped — owned outside MVP-1"* is reversed outright, the tables being MVP-1 with `PassScheduleId` a **real, enforced and trusted FK** on four tables. **But the reversal is not "map them after all"**, which is `P-13`: `D-31` and `[SVC §3.2a]` are both correct and pull opposite ways, since MVP-1 owns the tables and must still **never author a schedule**. Resolved by making them reachable **read-only** — **no `DbSet<PassSchedule>` on the context**, the read going through **Dapper** into the immutable `PassScheduleSnapshot` per `phase-01b` L60 — on the reasoning that a mapped tracked `DbSet` is a write path whether or not anyone means to use it, and the rule is better enforced by **absence of the mechanism** than by convention (the same reasoning `phase-01b` L112 applies to `ITInhibit`'s missing operator clear path). The FK is a database constraint and needs no EF mapping to hold. **`P-14`** sets the interim stance on **`D-30`**: do not add `ROWVERSION` to `WeldEvent`/`RodCheckout`/`WipRejection` — a 1C schema change — but write their updates as conditional-on-read rather than blind overwrites so the eventual decision changes the mechanism, not the semantics. Also records that `MediatorExtension.DispatchDomainEventsAsync` is strongly typed to the concrete context and **must be recreated per service**, that `FW-N04` deliberately omitted `AddCustomDbContext` from `Program.cs` and this story adds it, and that **`sp_ShiftSummary` is MVP-2's — do not create, drop or grant it** — where `[SVC §3.3]` names it alongside `sp_GetGaugeTrace`. Four stale citations flagged. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-143-Serilog-And-Audit-Log.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-143-Serilog-And-Audit-Log.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-143`, and it raises an open finding rather than resolving one.** The story is two unlike halves: the Serilog half is configuration, and the audit half is a compliance obligation whose third acceptance criterion — *"audit entries are queryable by run and by operator"* — **has no persistence target.** The verified `FlatWireDB` baseline is 28 tables (`[DBD §6.2]`) and **none of them is an audit log**; Serilog's configured sinks are a rolling JSON file and Grafana Loki, neither queryable by run in the sense a compliance reconstruction needs, and neither inside the database whose backup and retention policy covers the traceability data. **`P-15` presents three options and recommends a new `PlcTagAudit` table — which would be the 29th, so it must go through 1C** and would move `[DBD §6.2]` and every count citing it; the interim is to build behind an `IAuditLog` interface so the choice is one implementation rather than a refactor. **Registered as the same defect class as `G34`** (wire break — *"a decided flow with no persistence target"*). Records `FR-072`: **the audit record is written *before* the push**, not after and not in a `finally`, because a push that fails must still leave evidence it was attempted; and that OPC writes are **not transactional**, so the trail is expected to show push → failure → compensating clear as three records rather than one rolled back (`G2`). Notes that the two overrides already have homes (`PassScheduleChangeLog`, `RollOverride`) and **PLC pushes have neither** — that being the gap. Also records the sharpest consequence of `G33`/`PLC-Q05`: a wrong tag path **fails silently**, the write reporting success while the line keeps its previous settings, so **a green audit trail is not proof the line was configured**. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-144-Configuration-Binding.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-144-Configuration-Binding.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-144`.** Records four decided, non-obvious rules that each produce a plausible-looking but wrong configuration. **(1) Two distinct cadence knobs, both owned by this layer** — the OPC **publish interval** (`NFR005`: 1 s default, configurable 5/10/30 s, `[PLCC]`'s `PublishIntervalMs`) and the hub **drain cadence** (~100 ms); they sit on opposite sides of the bounded channel, which is what decouples them, so they are bound as two settings with two names and neither is derived from the other. **(2) `P-16`, the fail-fast/warn-once split**: structural problems (missing connection string, absent JWT variable, a line with no `Tags` block, an out-of-range cadence) **fail at boot**; *confirmation status* does not — under `[PLC]` v1.0 **no tag path is confirmed**, a path becoming confirmed only when commissioning `C1`/`C11` says the controller accepted it, so **one warning naming the count**, not 41 lines at every boot in every environment for weeks. Unconfirmed is explicitly **not** a startup failure, since the service could then not boot until commissioning, which is what `SimulatePLCTagPush = true` exists to avoid. **(3)** The `ITInhibit` key lives **inside each line's `Tags` block, never at the root** — a root-level key would imply one interlock across three lines, the opposite of the line-scoped behaviour `TC-017a` tests. **(4)** The anti-drift rule: this story **binds** the tag map, `[PLC]` **owns** it, and writing a tag path into a `.cs` file, a comment or a second `appsettings` fragment creates the seventh copy of a surface deliberately reduced to one. Verification is pinned on a `grep` for tag-path strings returning zero hits outside configuration. Seven open items recorded, including `G29` — **no edger tag path exists on any line, yet edge type is in the push payload**. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-145-JWT-And-Role-Policies.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-145-JWT-And-Role-Policies.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-145`, and the only one of the twelve marked blocked.** **The card lists five roles and there are six** — QA is missing, and QA has real capability (`/wipreject` dispose, SPC-HOLD release) — and it points at `04-APIContract.md`'s matrix where **`[SEC §8]` is the matrix of record**; `[API §9.2]` is *"only a role→endpoint summary"* and its own table omits Admin. Recorded as `P-17`, which also sets the practical hedge against **`G6`/`OI-37`** — *"whether any of the six exists as a JWT claim is unconfirmed … can block the build outright"* — by routing every policy through **one claim-type constant** defined once, so the eventual answer is a one-line change rather than a sweep. **This bounds the cost of the answer; it does not unblock the gap.** Records the two strings that appear on endpoints and in no matrix and must be **mapped to a column, not made into policies**: **`Receiving`** on `POST /rod` is upstream and not this service's to police, and **`Process Engineering / System Admin`** resolves as Eng/Maint ✓ + Admin ✓. Documents what is **inherited and must not be rebuilt** — `AddCustomAuthentication`'s double-indirect secret lookup and, worth knowing before someone reports it as a defect against this story, its `ValidateLifetime = false` against a 40-minute configured expiry, which is existing platform behaviour and out of scope here. Notes that **there is no `app.UseAuthorization()`** in the UAL pipeline (a global `AuthorizeFilter` from `AddCustomMvc()` does the work, so adding it changes behaviour), that `GET /health` is the **single anonymous** endpoint and `[DEP]`'s smoke test depends on it, and that the supervisor PIN **authenticates only** and is neither a role nor a claim (`OI-38`). Four stale citations flagged, including the withdrawn authorization-test criterion, now `TC-655` manually. |
| August 15, 2026 | **`G6`/`OI-37` answered — status ⚠ Blocked → ⚠ Buildable, one fact outstanding.** The six roles exist as JWT claims on the standard `ClaimTypes.Role`, so **no framework change and no `RoleClaimType` configuration** are needed. **`P-17` restated:** its original hedge — one claim-type constant in `Domain/Constants/Constants.cs` — is superseded, because it bounded the unknown that evaporated. The surviving unknown is the six claim **values**, which are abbreviated or coded rather than `[SEC §8]`'s labels, so the hedge becomes a six-constant `FlatWireRoles` class with the mapping to be recorded in §5 when supplied. Build-order step 2 becomes a five-minute check against a real token rather than a blocker, and steps 3–6 explicitly do not wait on it. **New finding — the failure mode is quiet and `TC-655` does not catch it:** a wrong value fails *closed*, presenting as a uniform and plausible `403`, while `TC-655` tests only that every endpoint requires authentication. §6's cell-by-cell matrix walk is the only check that distinguishes *correctly denied* from *denied because the string is wrong*, which makes it load-bearing rather than diligent. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-146-Exception-Middleware-And-Envelope.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-146-Exception-Middleware-And-Envelope.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-146`, eight hours with two failure modes that are invisible until a screen misbehaves.** **The first:** `POST /staging/rod` returns **`201 Created` with `state:"Blocked"`** on inspection failure — the row committed *before* the inspection gate, because writing nothing reported an occupied bay as free and a bundle is already physically on the payoff when inspected. `phase-01b` L93 names it *"exactly what a global exception middleware gets wrong."* It is **not an exception path at all** — the action returns it deliberately — and the middleware's job is to leave it alone; a rule mapping inspection failure to `422` is right everywhere except here. **The second, `P-18`:** the framework already installs `HttpGlobalExceptionFilter` via `AddCustomMvc()`, so adding middleware yields **two handlers for one exception** with an undocumented precedence. A filter sees only exceptions inside the MVC pipeline while middleware sees those plus everything outside, and `[API §1.2]`'s envelope must cover both — a client cannot branch on a contract that holds for only some failures. Resolved toward **middleware as the single mapper**, positioned before `UseRouting()`, with the inherited filter explicitly accounted for rather than ignored. **The envelope is inherited from `FW-138` `P-06` and must not be redefined here**, which is the trap in AC 2's `{Data, Success, Errors}` — no framework type carries an `errors` array. Records the `409`/`422` split as load-bearing and the most likely thing to be collapsed by a middleware written from habit, the 18-code catalogue travelling with the status, `INSPECTION_FAILED` carrying a `{route, rodAlpha}` payload rather than just a code, and that a `PLC_PUSH_FAILED` `500` means *the transaction aborted and compensating writes were issued* — **not** a rollback, since OPC writes are not transactional (`G2`). Also warns against `ErrorDescription = ex.Message`, `CoilCheckin`'s habit, which puts exception text on the wire. |
| August 15, 2026 | **§7 audit — two open items added, `P-18` corrected against the framework source, and the code-is-a-label rule written into §2.1.** **(1) `INSPECTION_FAILED` carries two statuses** and §7 did not say so: **`422` at `POST /checkin/rod`** (`[API §4.6]`) and **`201 Created` + `state:"Blocked"` at `POST /staging/rod`** (`[API §4.5]`) — the same code with the same `{route, rodAlpha}` payload. §2.2 covered the `201` case but never that the code is shared, so the natural implementation — a `Dictionary<string, HttpStatusCode>` — gets one of them wrong, and the one it breaks is the `201` regression this plan exists to protect. **§2.1 now states the rule: status comes from the exception type or from the action, and the code is never an input to choosing it** — derived from the type the two never collide, because one path throws and one does not. ⚠ Also records that **`[API §1.8]` states `INSPECTION_FAILED | 422` unqualified**, which is wrong at `/staging/rod`; raised with the contract owner rather than edited from here. **(2) `OI-46` added** — `SCHEDULE_NO_MATCH` has **no defined client action** (*"Path undefined — `OI-46`. Interim: block and alert Operations"*) — **and deliberately scoped out**: this story emits the code and the `422` and nothing more, because *"block and alert Operations"* is a **side effect** belonging to the check-in path (`FW-157` → `FW-208` → `AlertRaised`), and exception middleware must not start carrying business behaviour on a path that is already failing. **(3) `P-18` was wrong on a point of fact.** It offered *"not calling `AddCustomMvc()`'s filter-adding overload"* — **no such overload exists**: `AddCustomMvc()` and `AddCustomMvcWithoutAuthentication()` both add `HttpGlobalExceptionFilter`, only the `AuthorizeFilter` differing. ⚠ **And the filter is *inner* to the middleware**, so leaving it in place does not create two competing handlers — it makes **the middleware dead code for all 24 endpoints**, which is worse than the problem `P-18` was written to avoid. Resolved by calling `AddCustomMvc()` as `[ARC §2.2]` binds and then `o.Filters.RemoveType<HttpGlobalExceptionFilter>()`, since skipping it entirely would forfeit the global `AuthorizeFilter` — **the only thing enforcing `[Authorize]`, this pipeline having no `UseAuthorization()`** — plus the `CorsPolicy` and `AddControllersAsServices()`. A fallback is recorded, the divergence from every sibling service is flagged for ratification, and §5 gains checks that the two inspection paths hold **together** and that the filter is absent from `MvcOptions.Filters` at runtime. The `G2` row is trimmed to a pointer at §2.3. |
| August 15, 2026 | **Deep close-out — §2.4 added, `P-18` settled, and four of five open items closed.** Reading `UA.Framework.API/Infrastructure/Filters/HttpGlobalExceptionFilter.cs` removed the choice: `OnException` has **two branches and sets `ExceptionHandled = true` unconditionally** — `CustomException` becomes **`ValidationProblemDetails`** (`problem+json`, 400, carrying only `context.Exception.Message`, which for `CustomException` is the fixed string *"Command Validation Errors for type …"*, so **the `failures` collection is never read**), and **everything else becomes a flat `500`**, with the full exception and stack in Development. ⚠ **`BusinessRuleValidationException` therefore returns `500` not `422`, a `ROWVERSION` conflict `500` not `409`, and `[API §1.3]`'s load-bearing 409/422 split does not survive in any form.** Recorded as **§2.4**. **`P-18` is settled rather than ratified — the removal is forced, not chosen** — since keeping the filter fails `[API §1.2]`, `[API §1.3]`, `[API §1.8]` and `phase-01b` exit criterion 2 at once. ⚠ **This plan's own text is corrected twice**: the *"keep the filter … worse, survivable"* fallback is **withdrawn as not survivable** (it forfeits the contract rather than degrading it), and shadowing the filter by ordering is rejected against explicit `RemoveType`. The divergence framing is corrected too — **this is the only UAL service whose contract specifies a status per failure kind**, so the divergence is *in the requirement, not the taste*. §5 gains **four positive checks**, each expected value being exactly what the filter returns if the removal was missed. **§7 restructured into 7.1 open / 7.2 closed / 7.3 stale**, with the three closure routes stated — **(a) resolved · (b) scoped out · (c) reclassified** — because most of the section took the second or third rather than the first: `P-18` closes by **(a)**; `INSPECTION_FAILED` by **(a+c)**, its residual `[API §1.8]` unqualified-`422` row moving to stale for the contract owner; **`OI-46`** and **`G2`/`OI-39`** by **(b)** — *the item is open, but this story's exposure to it is not*, both named to `FW-157` (and `FW-151`) where the behaviour is actually built. **`P-06` alone remains open** and is `FW-138`'s to ratify; evidence added that should make it a formality — the framework sets `ActionResultBase<T>.ErrorCode` to an HTTP status integer, making it **incompatible with `[API §1.8]`'s string catalogue in meaning, not merely in type**. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-147-FluentValidation-Value-Objects-And-Enums.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-147-FluentValidation-Value-Objects-And-Enums.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-147`, whose first acceptance criterion files two rules in the wrong layer.** The card asks FluentValidation to enforce *"`FM2_S3` must be Active; FL3 requires Hybrid"*; **`[SVC §3.4]` and `phase-01b` L94 both name those two in the *aggregate* list**, and `phase-01b` L183 adds that the `FM2_S3` rule has **no DB constraint anywhere** — the only non-bypassable `CHECK` is `CK_PSC_FM1NotBypassable`, which is for FM1 — making it *necessarily application-only*. Recorded as `P-19`, and it is not a filing preference: a validator returns **`400`** — *"your request is malformed"* — where these two are **`422`** — *"this will never succeed as submitted"* — and `[API §1.3]`'s distinctions are what every screen branches on, so the wrong home returns the wrong contract for the life of the system. Only the card's third sample rule, `State ∈ {Active, Bypass, Skip}`, is genuinely a validator rule (enum membership). Records the three-way mirror — fourteen enums as a C# enum, a TS union and a DB `CHECK`, *"a change to any one is a change to all three"* — with the traps that carry a stated rule: `ComponentState` **never a boolean**, `EdgeType` nullable and one vocabulary not three, `CheckpointType` **five-valued** (the `RollAdjustTrigger` omission fails Phase 8's FL2 roll-adjust button at write time), `PayoffPosition` ids **pinned** with `TraversingTakeup = 3`, and `ComponentName` **three** FM2 stands. Notes that edge type's operator-facing labels — **"Round Edge"/"Flat Edge"** — are mapped by a single Angular pipe and **must not be added to the C# enum**. Warns that **`TC-020`, the manual three-way diff that replaced the withdrawn suite, is itself defective**: it cites `FM2_6inS3`, **withdrawn as never-existent** by `D-26`. *"Fix the test case; do not build to it."* Also flags that the card's title says *"value objects"* while none of its five criteria mentions them — the thirteen belong to `FW-207`. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-148-Health-Checks.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-148-Health-Checks.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-148`, which `phase-01b` L95 assigns a routing decision rather than resolving one.** `[API §3.2]` row 30 and the card say `/health` **base-relative**; `[DEP]` calls the absolute `/api/v1/flatwire/health`; and with `PATH_BASE = /API.FlatWire` a base-relative route actually serves at `/API.FlatWire/health`, which is **neither**. **`P-20` maps the endpoint at both paths and makes the absolute one canonical**, because `[DEP]`'s smoke suite and `[MON]`'s alerting are what break if the path is wrong and both use the absolute form, while the base-relative form costs one extra `UseHealthChecks` line and keeps every document's literal text true — with the chosen canonical path to be **recorded back into `[DEP]` and `[API §4.19]`** rather than left as analysis. Records that this is the **only anonymous endpoint** in the service and the only one with **no controller**, which matters twice: `AddCustomMvc()` installs a global `AuthorizeFilter`, so an MVC-routed health endpoint would be protected by default, and mapping it outside MVC before `UseAuthentication()` sidesteps that. Notes that the required body — `{status, database:{reachable,latencyMs}, opc:{reachable,latencyMs}, version, environment}` — is **neither the ASP.NET Core default nor `UIResponseWriter.WriteHealthCheckUIResponse`**, which is what `CoilCheckin` uses, so a custom `ResponseWriter` is required; and that `CoilCheckin`'s actual health surface is a single `AddCheck("self", …Healthy())` on routes `/liveliness` and `/hc`, checking nothing real. Adds the **third consumer the card omits**: `[MON]` instruments **hub connection count and broadcast-cadence deviation** off this surface — hub code this layer writes — exposed as instrumentation rather than gating checks, since a hub with zero connections at 3 a.m. is not unhealthy, and with **no threshold to alert against** while `G9`/`OI-34` leaves the real-time NFRs undefined. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-150-Broadcast-Loop.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-150-Broadcast-Loop.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Frames the loop as **four traffic classes**, since putting a channel in the wrong one is the defect this story ships or avoids: batched-and-decimated · on-change-only · immediate-unbatched · and **`PayoffStateChanged`, which must never enter the ~100 ms batch** because *"a bay changing hands is an operator-visible state transition, not a sampled reading."* Records that **FL2 suppresses two channels, not the line** — `SpeedFPM`, `FootageCounter`, `ComponentStatus` and `LineStatus` still flow, `FR-120` makes live gauge/width **`null`**, and the rule is conditioned on FL2 **in standalone mode**, not on FL2 as a line, so an FL3 hybrid run is not suppressed. Records that the **`ITInhibit` gate stops persistence, not broadcast** — it sits immediately before the `RunReading` write inside this loop. **`P-30`**: build a **fixed** configured cadence, not an adaptive one — `G9`/`OI-34` leaves the decimation ratio unvalidatable and the QA2 load test unable to fail, and an adaptive loop tunes itself against a target nobody has specified while making `TC-601`–`TC-613` non-deterministic; record the achieved cadence at QA0 so `G9` can close against a measurement. **`P-31`**: the loop is **unreduced for the trial** and must contain **no simulator-aware branch** — if it can tell the difference, `FW-203`'s substitution has failed. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-151-PLCTagService.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-151-PLCTagService.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Records that **the card names two operations and `phase-01b` L108 requires all six built here** — `PushPassSchedule`, `ClearPayoffTags`, the per-component write, hold/idle-and-restore, `SetITInhibit` and batch write — *"even though later phases call them"*, so this story ships code nothing calls yet and that is correct (`[PLCC §4]`). Treats the **banned word as a design rule**: OPC writes are not transactional, recovery is **compensating re-clears**, and `rollback` must appear nowhere in code, comments or log messages — a developer writing `RollbackAsync` has described a guarantee the hardware does not give. **`P-27`** splits the blocker: the **write surface is not blocked** and all six are built now, while the **compensation design** waits on `G2`/`OI-39` (saga/outbox vs `INFLAT` mirror, carrying the 24–64 h Phase-4 reserve) — implement compensating clears for the single-line push, document the saga boundary **in the service** per AC 4, and leave the cross-DB saga to Phase 4; `phase-01b` also says settle **`G30`** first, because one failure domain or two changes what a clear must cover. Records `FR-072` (**audit before the push**, leaving an incomplete-push marker), that `SimulatePLCTagPush` is `true` in **every** environment until commissioning and is selected by configuration not call site, and that this story owns **Polly** on outbound OPC. ⚠ Flags the sharpest risk: **`G33`/`PLC-Q05` — a wrong path fails silently**, the write reporting success while the line keeps its previous settings, which Polly cannot see and the audit will record as success. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-157-CheckIn-Rod-And-CheckInService.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-157-CheckIn-Rod-And-CheckInService.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the largest backend story in the trial (36 h), and the one where the system's hardest architectural fact bites.** Check-in spans `FlatWireDB` + `coils` + `wip_coil_orders` + the PLC and **is not one ACID transaction**, so two rules shape every line: **records are written *before* the PLC push** (`FR-072` — inspection result, pre-run SPC checkpoint, run record, acknowledgement audit entry, *then* `PushPassSchedule`), leaving an incomplete-push marker rather than a silent gap; and recovery is **compensating writes, never "atomic rollback"** (`G2`, `G16` closed 4 Aug 2026). **`P-40`** splits the blocker: the ordered path, the status codes and the snapshot are **specified and unblocked**, while `G2`/`OI-39` has only left the **recovery strategy** undecided — saga/outbox versus `INFLAT` mirror — so build the ordered writes and single-line compensating clears now, mark the run so a half-configured line is **detectable rather than silently `Running`** (the story's own "so that"), and leave the cross-database strategy a documented seam. ⚠ **Do not implement a two-phase commit or anything named for one.** Records that **the trial runs this story without `RodStaging`** — `[TRP §4]` prices Phase 4's write path as *"no `RodStaging`"* since DB2A left scope — so AC 5's staged-row match and consumption is **out of trial scope while remaining an MVP-1 obligation**, and its absence must not become an assumption that check-in never has a predecessor row. Also records that the **snapshot rule survives `D-31`**: `PassScheduleId` is now a real FK, but MVP-1 still persists its own copy of what it pushed, because a certificate must stay reproducible **after the owning system later edits the schedule**, which a local FK does nothing to prevent. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-164-Run-Queries-And-RunQueryService.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-164-Run-Queries-And-RunQueryService.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Records that **`GET /run/active` is the trial's landing route** — DB1 left trial scope on 14 Aug, so `[TRP §1.4]` names this endpoint as what the app opens on instead of `/lines/status`, making it load-bearing in a way its 12 h does not suggest. Its DTO **reaches across databases**: the Order Information block — customer, due date, tolerances, setup width/gauge, finish, OD min–max, weights — lives in the shared order/scheduling schema and is served **in one round trip**, because a second call doubles latency on the screen an operator opens first. **`P-42`** handles the fact that those columns are **unmapped** (`OI-33`): surface the read as a **view in `FlatWireDB`** per `[DBD §6.6]`'s established convention — *"one place to absorb mismatches rather than repeating them at every call site"* — and make **every order field nullable**, rendering as *not available*, so a missing field degrades the landing screen rather than failing the request; `Q18` (which order field carries coil min–max weight) is a second independent unknown in the same block and `[TRP §6]` lists it as second-tier, stopping no build. Also records that `RunReading` has **no repository and is in no aggregate** — Dapper via `sp_GetGaugeTrace` — that **weld markers come from `WeldEvent`** and the layer must be built **though it renders empty in the trial** (*"a legitimate state, not a defect"*), and that **Profile must not be re-sampled by the live tick**. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-168-Spc-And-SpcService.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-168-Spc-And-SpcService.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Leads on the story's own "so that" — **an in-spec verdict cannot be produced by the client**; a screen that decides pass/fail and posts the answer has inverted the point. Records that **`CheckpointType` has five members and `RollAdjustTrigger` is the one that was missing**, with `[TB §7]`'s warning that *"Phase 8 wires the FL2 roll-adjust button against it; if 1C ships a four-value `CHECK`, that button fails at write time"* — so all three layers must be verified **before S2** — and that **`PostDb1` must not be accepted** (`OI-10`, applied to the UI and never to the data model). **`P-43`** addresses `[TRP §6]` blocker 2: the min/max tolerance band `SpcService` computes against is read from `AlloyProperty` and **the values are owed by the client by e-mail with nothing seeded**, needed before T2 — so **do not compile a default band**, since a hard-coded tolerance produces a confident verdict against a number nobody agreed, and the verdict is exactly what this story exists to make authoritative; **read from `AlloyProperty` and fail the checkpoint on a null band**, following the precedent `[TRP §9]` already sets for `LbPerFtFactor`. ⚠ Records that this story can set an **`SPC-HOLD` with no supported path out of it** — `[API §9.2]` marks QA SPC-HOLD release as *"endpoint missing — `OI-32`"* and it is **MVP-1** — and that **`OI-18` leaves an SPC checkpoint unable to join to its trigger**, so the die-change auto-link is required with its mechanism open. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-170-Pause-Resume-And-RunControlService.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-170-Pause-Resume-And-RunControlService.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Eight hours with three deciding details. **Pause drives the PLC, not just the clock** — `RunControlService` calls the hold/idle and restore operations `phase-01b` L108 lists among the six built in Phase 1 and *"first exercised"* later, which is why the story is Phase 6; and ⚠ **this story's idle is not a stop** — `FR-302`'s never-send-a-stop invariant is Phase 7's and must not be pre-empted. **Resume has four outcomes, `OI-14` closed on four, and Rod Checkout is the fourth** — which carries a consequence that reads like a bug: **Rod Checkout is no longer a pause *reason***, superseding **`FR-262`**, so a reason list still carrying it is stale. The payload carries **`ReasonCode` + `ReasonCategory`, never a label**, with notes mandatory on `Other` per `CK_RunPauseEvent_NotesOther`. **`P-44`**: `FW-072` is deferred from the trial and `[TRP §4]` ships *"three of its four outcomes"*, so **build the enum with all four and let the trial disable the control, not the contract** — a member added later is a three-layer change, and ⚠ **a four-value enum against a three-value DB `CHECK` fails at write time**, the same defect class as `RollAdjustTrigger`; verify the `CHECK` carries four before S2. Also records that the handler raises `RunPaused` as a **domain event** so `FW-172` can broadcast the marker **without this handler touching SignalR**. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-172-Run-Event-Markers.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-172-Run-Event-Markers.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Takes the story's "so that" as the acceptance test — *"the screen and the record never disagree"* — and derives everything from it: **all five broadcasts are rare domain events, immediate and unbatched**, none may enter `FW-150`'s ~100 ms batch, because a marker arriving a tick late while its row is already written **is** that disagreement; and the route from a command handler to the hub is a **domain event dispatched after commit, never before**, since dispatching first broadcasts a marker for a row the database may still reject. **`P-45`** resolves an ownership split stated in neither card: `[SIG §5.4]` publishes **six** run-event markers, this card lists four, and **`FW-177` owns `RodCheckoutEvent` and `AlertEvent`** — its own card claims the first explicitly (*"DB14 was the previously-listed consumer and was descoped; DB3 is now named explicitly so the event is not left without one"*) and grouping the two exception broadcasts keeps one story owning the supervisor path. **Together the two stories cover all six**, which matters because `phase-01b`'s **exit criterion 4 counts twelve events and six markers**. Records that ⚠ **three of the five markers have no writer in the trial** — weld, die change and checkout are all deferred — and that the layers must be built regardless per `[TRP §7]`: *"a legitimate state, not a defect … deleting the layer is rework."* Also carries the two re-broadcasts (`PayoffWeight` after a weld, `ComponentStatus` after a roll override) and the reason they exist: `ComponentStatus` is on-change-only, so a change made through a command rather than a tag read needs an explicit push. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-174-WipRejection-And-Checkout-Services.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-174-WipRejection-And-Checkout-Services.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Two off-ramps in one story, and three rules that will not be obvious from the code being replaced. **`POST /wipreject` is the only thing in the entire system that clears a `Blocked` bay** — not an unstage, not an override, not an admin action — so if the endpoint is wrong a bay stays occupied forever; and ⚠ **`Blocked` is a *derived* state** (`Staged` + any inspection `Fail`), never a stored `Status`, so the release is *"leave `Staged`"* rather than "clear a flag". **The bay is cleared by publishing a domain event, not by reaching into `RodStaging`** — shorter, works, and breaks the aggregate boundary between two roots. **AC 4 is a behaviour change, not a new feature**: Mode B supervisor approval is now enforced **by constraint**, so code that previously wrote a Mode B checkout without a stamp **fails at write time**. ⚠ **And it has nothing to constrain yet — `G24` records that `RodCheckout` has no `ApprovedBy`, `ApprovedAt` or `OverrideReason` columns at all**, so every supervisor-gated checkout is unauditable until `FW-176` adds them; **confirm before building against them**. **`P-46`** keeps PENDING DISPOSITION persisted in the checkout transaction even though `FW-175`'s durable queue is deferred from the trial — it is a **status on the material**, not a notification, so `FW-175`'s absence must mean nobody is reliably *told*, not that the state is not *recorded*; the trial consequence, that a pending disposition may go unseen and the material stay locked, is a known limitation rather than a defect. ⚠ Records that **`OI-32` leaves supervisor disposition of a pending Mode B with no endpoint at all** despite being a decided requirement (`FR-325`/`FR-326`), so this story creates a state with no supported transition out of it. |

---

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-177-Exception-Broadcasts.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-177-Exception-Broadcasts.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Carries the two run-event markers `FW-172` does not (`P-45`), so that between them `[SIG §5.4]`'s six are covered — which matters because exit criterion 4 counts them. Records that **one of its events lost its consumer and was re-homed**: `RodCheckoutEvent` rendered on **DB14**, descoped 4 Aug 2026, and the card names **DB3** explicitly *"so the event is not left without one"* — a marker with nowhere to render being how an event quietly stops being built. ⚠ **`AlertRaised` is now in the same position** — its consumer is DB1, which left trial scope with `FW-060`, and `FW-N06`'s rules engine was deferred *"because its only consumer was DB1's alert bar"* — and the plan holds that it is **built and broadcast anyway**, on the same reasoning as the weld-marker layer. ⚠ **Its durability is deferred**: AC 3 is *"backed by `FW-175`'s durable queue"* and `FW-175` is out of trial scope, so in the trial the notification is **transient** and a supervisor whose terminal was disconnected is not told — though `FW-174` `P-46` still records the state. **`P-47`** broadcasts to a **group** rather than a connection: a supervisor may have several terminals open, `[SIG §4.3]`'s existing pattern is group-based with re-join on reconnect, and it keeps the hub stateless so the Redis backplane stays config-only — ⚠ **but a group send is still transient and does not substitute for `FW-175`**. Notes that role targeting needs **`G6`**, unconfirmed and due before 28 Aug, and should route through `FW-145` `P-17`'s single claim-type constant. |
| August 15, 2026 | **`G6` answered — role targeting unblocked, and §3.1 rewritten around a failure mode `FW-145` does not share.** The Supervisor role exists on `ClaimTypes.Role`, so `[TRP §6]` blocker 4 no longer threatens the story; the value must be read from `FlatWireRoles.Supervisor` rather than a literal, because the claim values are coded and still unmapped. **The finding is an asymmetry:** a wrong role value fails *closed and visible* in `FW-145` — someone gets a `403` and complains within the hour — but **silent** here, because a broadcast to an empty `Supervisors` group raises no error anywhere and the pending disposition simply never reaches a supervisor. §6's notification row is restated to assert **arrival at the terminal**, observed rather than inferred from a sender-side log, and its closing note now separates three states — dispatched, received, guaranteed — of which this story owns only the middle. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-179-CheckIn-Spool-And-Spools-Query.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-179-CheckIn-Spool-And-Spools-Query.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Three traps, the third in the contract itself. **`404` only for an unknown alpha** — an *unallocated* spool is a **`200` with a null order**, which `[TB §7]` calls *"the mistake to avoid"* and which is the natural mistake because both feel like "not found"; **`P-48`** makes it unambiguous by resolving existence first and allocation second, and distinguishes a missing allocation (null order) from an unreachable order schema (`500`), since the order block is a cross-database read on unmapped columns and a failed join is both the expected case and the shape of an infrastructure failure. **FL2 tags only** — `S1`/`S2`/`S3` roll gaps and stand states plus **edgers at S2 and S3**, with **no DB or FM1 tags**, because FL2 is fed an already-flattened spool; ⚠ **`G41` stops being inert here**, since `CK_PSC_FM1NotBypassable` is line-blind and forces every FL2 schedule to mark `FM1` Active, and the constraint only matters *"the moment anything reads component state to decide what to write"*, which is this story. ⚠ **And `[API §4.6a]`'s own worked example is stale** — it checks in against `PS-1100-FL2-001` and returns `"success": true`, where the contract must refuse it **twice over**, by `SCHEDULE_NOT_ACTIVE`/`422` (the row is `Inactive`) and by `FR-091` (it is `Hybrid` against a Standalone-origin spool); **build to `PS-1100-FL2-002`, do not copy the example**. Records that the **hybrid-origin guard is *undefined*, not merely open** — `TC-118` is P1 and reads *"gate fails until specified"* — so build the hook, record observed behaviour, and **do not invent the rule**; the trial does not walk into it but **Phase 8 does**. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-181-FL2-Null-Gauge-Contract.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-181-FL2-Null-Gauge-Contract.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — four hours that `[TRP §7]` singles out as *"the single most likely thing to ship wrong."*** The failure is specific and looks like success: FL2 standalone has no live gauge, so the field is **`null`**, and a chart handed a null that **draws a flat line at target** shows an operator a perfectly in-spec measurement of nothing — *"the mockup animates a simulated trace because a static prototype has no hub; the built screen must not."* Three more that are easy to get backwards: **`null` is not silence** (speed, footage, component and line status keep flowing, which `[TRP §8]` step 9 depends on when it requires Profile static *"across several live ticks"* — there have to be ticks); **Profile is static**, not re-sampled by the live tick; and ⚠ **the suppression is conditioned on FL2 *in standalone mode*, not on FL2 as a line**, which `G40` records as one of the assertions that rested on the earlier fixture error. **`P-49`**: AC 5 binds toggle availability to **line mode**, and a client keying on `lineId === 'FL2'` is wrong on FL3 **and would look right for the whole trial**, which is Standalone throughout — so **the server must publish the run's route mode** on the active-run DTO rather than leaving the client to infer it; that is a contract addition, non-breaking under `[API §8]` but needing the three-layer mirror. Records that the story **straddles two streams** — AC 1 and 5 are server-side, AC 2–4 are Angular's — and reproduces the rendering rules precisely enough that the FE half cannot get them wrong, because the contract is worthless if misread. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-203-OPC-Feed-Simulator.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-203-OPC-Feed-Simulator.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — trial scope, additive to `[CE §3b]`.** Leads on the one mistake that kills two of the six trial screens and is the natural one to make: **FL2 must be driven too.** `[SIG §5.3]` suppresses **only** the batched gauge and width channels, `FR-120` makes live values **`null`** — which is not *absent* — and `[TRP]` states it plainly: *"A simulator that drives FL1 only leaves both FL2 screens dead."* Records that **two of the card's criteria are unreachable in this story**: *"traces can be steered"* and *"drives a `RUNNING → STOPPED` edge on demand"* need a control surface a publisher does not have — `[SIM §1.2]` marks `FW-203` ❌ on it, and configuration-plus-restart **destroys the run being demonstrated**, which is what `[TRP §8]` steps 7 and 10 assert about. That is **`G43`**, resolved by `FW-218`. **`P-36`** splits them: the steerable generator, the stop edge and the drop-readings fault are **internal capabilities built here**, and `FW-218` adds four HTTP endpoints over them and nothing else — the other split would make `FW-218` a rewrite rather than an API and break the *"first increment of `FW-215`"* relationship. **`P-37`** holds the no-new-interface line and extends it downstream: **`FW-150` and `FW-151` must not be able to tell whether the feed is real**, which is the entire reason `[TRP §1.4]` leaves both unreduced. Fixtures pinned to `PS-1100-FL1-001` and **`PS-1100-FL2-002`** per `G40`. ⚠ Carries `G39`: **steering an unverified model does not make it verified** — do not read a green trial as evidence the machine behaves this way. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-205-ITInhibitService.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-205-ITInhibitService.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — and this story alone closes `phase-01b` exit criterion 5**, which had no plan of any kind. Frames the work by what must **not** exist: **there is no operator clear path and there must not be one**, `TC-016` attempts one *via every UI surface*, and the rule is enforced by the **absence** of any endpoint, command or hub method — so a developer who adds a reset button breaks a P1 test **by writing code**. **`P-25`** makes that absence defensible rather than incidental: no controller, no command, no hub method, `SetITInhibit` reachable only through the service's own call path, a comment at the service citing `TC-016`, and a line on `FW-138`'s QA0 checklist — because an absence cannot be unit-tested and backend tests are withdrawn. **`P-26`** resolves a card-vs-phase conflict: the card names **two** tags, `phase-01b` L112 and exit criterion 5 say **three** — build the config surface for three and leave FL3 inert behind the same switch until `G30`/`PLC-Q08` decides whether it carries its own tag or asserts FL1's and FL2's together. Records the two other inversions — the tag is **written, never read**, so it is a `PLCTagService` operation and not a subscription; and the config key sits **inside each line's `Tags` block, never at the root**, because a root key surfaces the first time an idle line blocks a running one — plus that the interlock **gates `FW-150`'s `RunReading` persistence, not its broadcast**. ⚠ Notes that **condition 5 is only exercisable via `FW-218`'s `POST /sim/{lineId}/fault`**, which is the sole fault in that story's scope for exactly this reason, and that `TC-011`/`TC-012` are **not** this story's — conditions 1–2 are `FW-206`, Phase 4. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-207-Domain-Model.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-207-Domain-Model.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-207`, the story that makes `FlatWire` the first UAL service on tactical DDD (`D-29`).** Records that the likely failure mode is **not doing DDD badly but rewriting machinery that already exists**: `UA.Framework.Domain` ships `Entity` and `ValueObject`, `CoilCheckin` ships `IBusinessRule`, `CheckRule` and `DispatchDomainEventsAsync`, and all of it is **dormant** — `CoilCheckin` kept every piece and modelled `DBModels/` as anemic property bags. Also records that `CoilCheckin` **locally duplicates** rule types that ship identically in `UA.Framework.Domain`, and the framework copy is the one to take. **The sharpest finding is §2.2: the alpha is the identity, but `Entity.Equals()` and `IsTransient()` operate on `Id`** — both true at once, so a `HashSet` or `Distinct()` over unsaved aggregates will not behave as the domain reads. **`P-23`** holds the line on the two criteria that became unverifiable when backend tests were withdrawn — nothing exercises `RodAlpha("ROD-00041")` throwing, which is what *"closes `G14`'s format half"*, and nothing runs the bay rule **with the DB index absent**, which is what makes `G21`'s index belt-and-braces rather than the sole defence: **build both, demonstrate the constructor by hand at QA0, and say plainly that the index-absent case is not demonstrable in a deployed environment** rather than claiming coverage. **Do not delete them to make the story pass.** **`P-24`** matches `FW-142` `P-14` on `D-30` — three of the seven roots have no concurrency token though all three are mutated after insert; do not add columns here, but model the aggregates so a token can be added without reshaping them. Two stale citations flagged: the blocker cites **`D1`**, renumbered **`D-30`** on 15 Aug 2026 after colliding with `[PLC]`'s retired log, and AC 3's *"`PassSchedule` is MVP-2-owned"*, superseded by `D-31` — though the **read-model** status, which is the operative half, still holds. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-208-Domain-Events-Post-Commit-Dispatch.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-208-Domain-Events-Post-Commit-Dispatch.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Records that this story is what makes `[SVC §3.2]`'s hardest rule survivable — Application may not reference SignalR types, which leaves a command handler no way to broadcast its own side effect, and **the tempting fix is to inject `IHubContext`**. Domain events are the answer the specification already chose, *"satisfied rather than worked around."* **`P-34`** makes the negative acceptance criterion structural instead of disciplinary: `FlatWire.Application`'s `.csproj` carries **no** SignalR package reference and no reference to the API project, so a handler reaching for `IHubContext` **fails to compile** — a stronger guarantee than a review note, costing nothing, and matching how `FW-205` `P-25` enforces its own absence. **`P-35`** puts the translation handlers in **Infrastructure**, where `[SVC §3.2c]` leaves the choice open: they are dispatched by `FlatWireDbContext`, the API project is already the composition root, and a domain-event-to-hub translation is not a business rule. Records the two rules most likely to be violated quietly: **dispatch after `SaveChangesAsync`, never before** — dispatching first broadcasts a state the database may still reject — and **`WipRejection` clears a blocked bay by publishing, not by reaching into `RodStaging`**, which is shorter and works and breaks the aggregate boundary, `Blocked` being a **derived** condition rather than a field to clear. Also notes the dispatcher is inherited but **not reusable**: `MediatorExtension.DispatchDomainEventsAsync` is strongly typed to the concrete context and must be recreated against `FlatWireDbContext`. |

---

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-218-Sim-Control-Surface.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-218-Sim-Control-Surface.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — trial scope, additive to `[CE §3b]`.** The story exists because **the trial was specified to demonstrate behaviour it had no way to trigger** (`G43`): three of `[TRP §8]`'s ten steps need the feed **steered while a run is live**, and the only lever without a control surface is configuration plus a restart, **which destroys the run being demonstrated** — the very thing steps 7 and 10 assert about. **`P-38`** records why conditional route registration and a policy guard are not equivalent, though they look so in review: a mapped-but-forbidden route **exists**, is discoverable, and is protected only by correct role configuration, whereas an unregistered route cannot be reached by any caller in any role — hence `[SIM §2.4]`'s absolute, **`404`, not `403`**, with `Engineer`/`Admin` layered on top and **never `Operator`**. **`P-39`** requires the four endpoints to carry the **paths, shapes and semantics `[SIM §8.1]` already specifies**, implemented over `FW-203` instead of the line model, so `FW-215` later **extends** rather than replaces and `FW-214`'s greyed controls light up as configuration — *"do not invent a simpler shape because only four endpoints are in scope."* Records that these are **not among `[API]`'s endpoints** and must not be added to the 32-row count. ⚠ **§7.1 reports that `FW-218` appears nowhere in `phase-01b`** — zero occurrences, though it has a full card in `[TB §7]` (Critical, S1, Phase 1B, Stream BE) and a row in `[TRP §1.4]`. **Reported, not fixed**: adding it changes an hours-bearing document, and `[TRP §1.4]`'s arithmetic already carries it (BE 145 → 156, total 220 → 231). |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-N04-FlatWire-Solution-Skeleton.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-N04-FlatWire-Solution-Skeleton.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the build order for `FW-N04`, and the first document in the new `Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/` folder.** Consolidates the skeleton's requirements, which were correct and consistent across `[SVC §3.1]`, `[ARC §2.2]`, `[API §3.1]`, `[SEC §8]` and `phase-01b` but nowhere sat together. **Five decisions are recorded as `P-01`–`P-05`, and two are divergences needing ratification.** **`P-01`:** scaffold from `dotnet new ua-solution-template` (`ual-api`'s `API/UATemplate/`) rather than copying `CoilCheckin`, which the card's third acceptance criterion names — reconcilable because `[ARC §2.2]`'s requirement is that the *controller shape, MediatR command pattern, `Program.cs`, `.csproj` and NuGet set* come from `CoilCheckin`, and all still do; only the physical scaffold changes, and it arrives without `CoilCheckin`'s .NET 6 `Dockerfile`, unregistered `AutoMapper`, doubly-registered `GetCoilsQueryHandler`, `wwwroot/` boilerplate and `<Compile Remove>` blocks. **`P-02` is the one genuine specification-vs-template conflict:** `[SVC §3.1]` and `phase-01b` L81 both specify `Infrastructure → Domain`, but `CoilCheckin.Infrastructure` references **`Application`**, because `CrossCutting.cs` feeds the Scrutor handler scan a `typeof(GetCoilsQuery).Assembly` marker — copying the template literally violates the specified graph. **Resolved in favour of the specification**: the MediatR marker and Scrutor bootstrap move to `FlatWire.API`, which references both, so the dependency rule is not inverted for an indirection that buys nothing given `[SVC §3.2]` already bars `DbContext` types from Application. `FW-139` and `FW-140` inherit this. Also records `P-03` `FlatWire.*` namespaces + `<AssemblyName>` (against `CoilCheckin`'s bare `Api`/`Domain` and its existing drift), `P-04` class-level `[Route("api/v1/flatwire")]` with explicit per-action routes — the `[controller]` token cannot express `PayoffStagingController`'s two prefixes — and `P-05` a fresh `launchSettings.json` port set, since `CoilCheckin` and `UATemplate` ship **identical** ports (7159/5219/47311/44378) and a third copy collides. **Three findings from the `ual-api` side that no planning document carries:** `UATemplate` ships its own 30-line `Directory.Packages.props` and **no domain under `API/Domain/` has one** — leaving the generated copy in place conflicts (`AspNetCore.HealthChecks.UI.Client` 9.0.0 vs 8.0.1, `Scrutor` 5.1.1 vs 5.0.1, legacy `MediatR.Extensions.Microsoft.DependencyInjection` 9.0.0 instead of `MediatR` 12.4.1) and omits Serilog, Dapper, FluentValidation, Polly and `JwtBearer` outright, so **deleting it is mandatory**; `UATemplate.sln` reuses `CoilCheckin.sln`'s four project GUIDs verbatim, so fresh ones must be minted; and the `UA.Framework.*` local feed is **`C:\Nuget\Repo`**, not the `D:\` that `ual-api`'s `CLAUDE.md` documents. **Scope pulls `FW-138`'s fifteen controller shells forward** (classes, `[Authorize]`, `IMediator` ctor — no actions, no stub fixtures) so the Angular stream has a stable base URL set from S0; the endpoints, envelope and fixtures stay with `FW-138`. **No hour cell was restated anywhere** — Phase 1B's 519 h derivation is quoted by `[CE]`, `[DE]`, `[SSP]`, `[TRP]` and `[TB §7]`, and re-deriving in place would desynchronise roughly twenty files; the shift is recorded as a note and re-baselining left as a separate additive exercise. **§8.2 flags four stale backlog citations** a developer working from `[TB]` alone would inherit: `useStub` for **`useMockData`**, the "25 MVP-1 tables / 28 in the full design" split reversed by `D-31`, `FW-141`'s repository list against the seven-aggregate-root rule, and `[SSP]`'s "thirteen thin controllers" at 35 h against fifteen at 45 h. Also records that **no Flat Wire specification states a CORS position at all** — a grep of `MVP-1/ProjectPlan/` returns nothing, while `AddCustomMvc()` applies a permissive default — raised rather than invented. The document declares **no shortcode** and is explicitly not citable as a requirement. |
| August 15, 2026 | **Cross-linked to the new [`FW-138`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-138-Fifteen-Thin-Controllers.md) plan; no decision or step in this document changed.** §5 now states that the **`P-##` series is continuous across the folder** — `P-01`–`P-05` here, `P-06`–`P-08` there — so a number means one thing repository-wide rather than two plans both minting a `P-01`. §1.2's out-of-scope row and §7's handoff link through. §7 also carries a warning that `FW-138` found a conflict this story does not hit but sits beside: the framework cannot produce `[API §1.2]`'s `{data, success, errors[]}` envelope, and **`CoilCheckin`'s controllers return HTTP 200 on failure** — so a developer must not "fix" a `FW-138` controller back toward that catch block on the strength of `[ARC §2.2]`, which binds `CoilCheckin` for controller *shape* and not for its contradiction of `[API §1.3]`. |
| August 15, 2026 | **Out-of-scope table completed to all thirteen dependants and cross-linked.** Every story in `phase-01b`'s Stories trailer now has a plan in this folder — the eight added today are `FW-149`, `FW-150`, `FW-151`, `FW-205`, `FW-208`, `FW-N05` plus trial-scope `FW-203` and `FW-218` — twenty-two documents in all. `FW-206` (Phase 4) and `FW-N11` (uncosted) are named as the nearest neighbours still without one. No decision or step in this document changed. |
| August 15, 2026 | **Three §8.1 open items answered; none changes the build.** **CORS** — the Angular app is same-origin with `FlatWire.API` on IIS, which is the question §8.1 had explicitly asked rather than answered. The row now also warns against the wrong inference: `AddCustomMvc()`'s permissive `CorsPolicy` is inert in production but **load-bearing in development**, where `ng serve` on `localhost:4200` is cross-origin, so tightening it to match the IIS deployment would break every developer's loop while changing nothing in production. **`C:` vs `D:\Nuget\Repo`** — `C:` confirmed, turning this plan's recommendation into a decision; `ual-api`'s `CLAUDE.md` is simply wrong. **`G6`** — the six roles exist on `ClaimTypes.Role`; the skeleton was never blocked, but the row now records that **no `JWTClaims` addition was needed**, which would otherwise have put this story's pack → local-feed → clear-cache → rebuild dance on `FW-145`'s critical path. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-N05-OPC-Ingest-And-Bounded-Channel.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/FW-N05-OPC-Ingest-And-Bounded-Channel.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue.** Records the story's unusual status precisely: **deferred for the trial and uncancelled**, `[TRP §1.4]` calling the deferral *"the single highest-value deferral in this plan"* because it moves 16 h **and** removes the trial's only hardware dependency — while **the contract must still be built**, which is why `FW-150` and `FW-151` are unreduced. **Neither `FW-203` (8 h) nor `FW-218` (18 h) offsets this story's 32 h.** Frames the design as one specific idea: a **bounded** channel with **drop-oldest/coalesce**, so *"backpressure degrades resolution, never memory"* — bounded rather than unbounded because an unbounded channel converts a slow consumer into a memory leak, and drop-oldest rather than block or drop-newest because for 10 Hz telemetry the freshest reading is the useful one. **`P-28`**: `G9`/`OI-34` leaves no defensible arithmetic for the bound, so **size it by configuration with a stated provisional default** — a guess in `appsettings` is revisable at commissioning, a guess in a `const int` is a code change — and **do not let the absence of NFRs become a reason to leave it unbounded**. **`P-29`**: because `FW-203` publishes to this channel and `FW-150` drains it **before** this story is built, the `Reading` type and channel shape must be **defined here and now** even though the hosted service is deferred — *a deferred story whose contract is also deferred cannot be stood in for*. Also records that the **subscription list is `G31`'s decision** — several read rows are published `[NO REMAINING CONSUMER — confirm before subscribing]` — and that this story owns the **OPC publish interval** (`NFR005`, 1 s default) against `FW-150`'s ~100 ms drain, the two sitting on opposite sides of the channel and never derived from one another. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/Orchestration.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Backend/TaskBreakdownPlans/Orchestration.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 15, 2026 | **First issue — the folder's entry point, and the first document to sequence Phase 1B rather than describe it.** The 22 plans each say *how* to build one story; this says **what can start, in what order, and what is stopping it**, holding no build detail and losing to every plan as the plans lose to every specification. **Its headline is a scheduling finding no document previously stated: the critical path is `FW-N04 → FW-145 → FW-080 → FW-N05 → FW-150 → FW-203 → FW-218`, and its second node is the only plan marked `Blocked`.** `G6`/`OI-37` — whether the six roles exist as JWT claims — is unconfirmed, *"can block the build outright"*, and `[TRP §6]` dates it **before T1 closes, 28 Aug 2026**; everything downstream of the hub waits on an answer nobody has. Two consequences follow and are recorded: **the path runs through the 124 h RT stream, not the 249 h of backend bulk**, so staffing BE harder does not shorten the phase — BE is wide and shallow with **twelve stories unlocking together at wave 1**, RT is narrow and deep and `[TRP §1.4]` prices it at 0.75–0.90 retention against FE's 0.62 — and **`FW-N05` sits mid-path while deferred**, which is why `P-29` requires its *contract* now even though the hosted service waits for commissioning. Carries a **mermaid dependency graph** with the two dashed edges that leave the phase (`FW-141`/`FW-142` → 1C's `FW-006`/`FW-007`, the stub path unblocked immediately and only the real repository wiring waiting), a **seven-wave order**, the **six ratification gates** with `P-06` named first because it blocks 53 h and its alternative is a `[API §1.2]` contract change across every 1A screen, a **blocker calendar** with the three dated items, and the **six exit criteria mapped to owning plans** — where **criterion 6 has no reviewer**, and with no automated backend tests the QA0 walkthrough is the entire verification of the layer. §8 records what the folder does not cover (`FW-206`, `FW-N11`, the unscheduled simulator set), the three hours-bearing findings raised and not fixed, and the **three stale FL2-fixture sites outside this folder** — `[API §7.2]`, `[API §4.6a]`'s worked example and the seed banner comment — noting that the plans are correct and the contract a developer copies from is not. §9 states the maintenance rules, including **never add build detail here** — *"two homes is how the six PLC tag copies happened"* — and **never restate an hour figure**: §1 quotes `[TB §7]` and the 134 h is derived for sequencing only, changing no published total. |
| August 15, 2026 | **Audit fixes — the entry point had stopped telling the truth about its own folder.** It still described **22 plans** in two places while **32** existed, its status board carried 22 rows, and the ten trial-path plans appeared in no board, graph, critical path, blocker calendar or exit-criteria mapping — **a reader trusting §1 concluded the trial path was unplanned**. Counts corrected to 32 and **§1.4 added**, listing the ten Phase-4–8 plans with story, hours, stream, phase and status. **§2 now states that the graph is Phase-1B-scoped by design** and points at `TrialOrchestration.md` for the rest, rather than merging a wave axis and a sprint axis into one diagram. **§4a adds the decision register — `P-01` to `P-49`** — because 49 decisions were defined across the plans and only the six unratified ones appeared anywhere, so finding what `P-27` said meant grepping 32 files; §4 is now that table filtered to `⚠ ratify`, and the register notes that **`settled` means decided and recorded, not ratified by a third party**, with new decisions minted at `P-50`+. **§8 gains an explicit scope statement** — this folder plans Phase 1B and the trial's server-side path; MVP-1 Phases 9–14 and the FE/DB streams are not planned here — and **§8.3 names the nine backend stories that had no plan and no other home**: `FW-185`, `FW-187` (⚠ **Phase 9 is wholly MVP-1 — in-scope work, not deferred**), `FW-190` (⚠ overlaps `FW-181` `P-49`), `FW-192`, `FW-090`, `FW-101`, `FW-196`, `FW-198` and `FW-200` (⚠ **already cited by `FW-082`'s plan**). *"Silence is not coverage"* — a boundary that is only implied gets read as completeness. |
| August 15, 2026 | **`P-18` settled — §4 is five gates, not six.** `FW-146`'s reading of `HttpGlobalExceptionFilter` showed the removal is **forced rather than chosen**: the filter collapses every non-validation exception to `500`, destroying `[API §1.3]`'s 409/422 split, so no ratification is available. Struck from §4's table, marked **settled 15 Aug** in the §4a register (which keeps all 49 ids), and §1.1's `FW-146` row no longer cites it. `P-01`, `P-02`, `P-06`, `P-13` and `P-20` remain the open gates. |
| August 15, 2026 | **`G6`/`OI-37` answered — no plan is marked `Blocked` any more.** The §3 heading, the intro's *"the one thing to read first"* callout and §3's consequence 1 all asserted that the critical path's second node was blocked; all three are restated. `FW-145`'s §1.1 row goes 🔴 → ⚠. §5's blocker row is struck through and **replaced by a separate residual row dated *before QA0, not before the build*** — the six claim values gate verification rather than construction. `P-17`'s §4a row restated from one claim-type constant to six role constants. **The 134 h path length is unchanged** — `G6` was a blocker, not a duration. |

W5 is **6.6** (262 ÷ 40 = 6.55) and W6 is **5.9** (234 ÷ 40 = 5.85). No hour figure changed and no conclusion moves. Method is a **retention-factor card** (§1) applied to the capacity model's *published* discipline columns, so every figure is reproducible by one multiplication and that document stays the base of record. Row, column, roll-up and weekly-grid integrity all verified; `1,486 h` is flagged throughout as **not** a programme total. All seven stream columns sum without leakage to **3,292 h**.
