## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/MachineSimulator.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/MachineSimulator.md)

| Date | Changed By | Description |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| Aug 15, 2026 | Plan team | **First issue, `[SIM]`.** Specifies the FL1/FL2/FL3 machine simulator and its engineering console `DB-S1`. Eleven sections: the four rules (§2), the seam and two adapters (§3), the three line models (§4), the kinematic model and its **A1–A10 assumption table** (§5), the closed loop with `SimulatePLCTagPush` (§6), the scenario and fault catalogue (§7), the control surface (§8), the console (§9), what it cannot prove (§10), and open items (§11).<br><br>**Four things in it will look like defects to a reader who does not know they are deliberate.** **(1) It carries no PLC tag path strings and never may** — the same rule `[PLCC]` observes; `[PLC]` is the only tag map in the repository, and writing a path here would create the seventh copy. **(2) `FL2` ticks.** §4.2 states that only the *batched* `GaugeReading`/`WidthReading` are suppressed — `SpeedFPM`, `FootageCounter`, `PayoffWeight`, `ComponentStatus` and `LineStatus` still flow, and gauge is still computed internally for the `RunReading` profile. A simulator treating FL2 as silent leaves both FL2 trial screens dead. **(3) Run status and line state are two vocabularies** (§4.4): `CK_FlatWireRun_Status` is ours and settled; the line-state vocabulary is ours and **unconfirmed** (`OI-35`). A mill can stop with the run still `Running`, and `FR-141`'s `RUNNING → STOPPED` edge is the *line* state. **(4) Mill spring places the roll gap *below* gauge**, load-proportionally (§5.5) — springback-as-an-alloy-multiplier putting the gap above gauge is the error master spec §10.5 exists to correct, a machine stiffness misfiled as a material property.<br><br>**§5.6 is the document's load-bearing table.** Ten assumptions, each naming its basis and the commissioning test that confirms it. **A8 and A9 are permanent simplifications** (lateral spread and rolling force are not modelled, against `PSG` §3.3.3 and §3.3.6); **A1–A7 are provisional and must be reconciled**. It is gap **`G39`**'s only instrument, and the act that closes it — commissioning step **`C13`** — is **not yet written into `[COM]`**.<br><br>Also records, in §1.4, that **there is no prior art in the UAL ecosystem** — `slitter-setup-simulator` is a slitting-pattern optimizer and `OPCConnection` has no simulation support — so the search is not repeated. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Development/StaffedSprintPlans.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Development/StaffedSprintPlans.md)

| Date | Changed By | Description |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **Re-baselined at three developers, with yield/cost/scrap out of plan — and a sequencing defect corrected in the same pass.** The 2- and 4-developer scenarios were withdrawn (recoverable at `1b8814c`); **Phase 12** — coil yield, cost ledger and scrap — is excluded by direction, taking the plan to **103 stories / 1,396 h** from 107 / 1,485 h.<br><br>**The date does not move: 7 sprints, finish Wed 25 Nov 2026, unchanged.** All 89 h came out of `S6`, which was never the binding sprint — the finish is set by `S7`, which exists because **Phase 14 must have a sprint to itself**. What the removal buys is the plan's only real absorption: `S6` falls from **98 % to 60 %**, 95 free hours immediately before commissioning, with every earlier sprint still at 96–102 %. A new §3 states this outright, because the intuition is the opposite.<br><br>**⚠ The allocator had no barrier on Phase 14, and the published plans were right by accident.** `[SP §6.1]` lists `14` last under *Must be sequential* and phase-14's scope call says *"pull this into a dedicated post-feature-complete window regardless of team size"* — the greedy pass enforced neither. The 3-developer plan isolated Phase 14 only because nothing else fitted, and **the withdrawn 4-developer plan had Phase 14 sharing `S5` with Phases 11, 12 and 13, which the rule forbids**. With 89 h removed the greedy pass would have reported a **6-sprint plan finishing 13 Nov** — twelve days early and not deliverable. The barrier is now explicit and the allocation was regenerated under it.<br><br>**One published figure was wrong in consequence and is corrected:** the *add a 4th developer* lever claimed **30 Oct**, which was the withdrawn plan's barrier-violating date. Under the barrier four developers finish **13 Nov** and five finish **30 Oct**, so **no team size on this start date reaches 30 September** — because the last two sprints are dependency-bound, not effort-bound (at four developers `S5` is 31 % and `S6` 22 %). §1 now says so rather than resting on the 4.0-FTE arithmetic floor.<br><br>**Two notes kept deliberately.** The per-stream split (FE 545 · BE 354 · DB 202 · RT 305) sums to **1,406 h against the allocation's 1,396 h**: `[DE]` publishes a per-phase retention factor *and* a per-stream compression rate, and once Phase 12 is removed they disagree by 10 h because its stream mix compresses differently from its phase factor — the per-phase basis is the one every sprint figure reconciles to. And **`OQ-10` (footage→weight) still matters with yield out of plan**: it is a Phase 9 coil-completion question, printed on every customer label, not a Phase 12 one. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **Created** as sprint plans at 2, 3 and 4 developers, with all 107 development stories allocated — two-week sprints from 24 Aug 2026 on the AI-assisted basis. Superseded the same day by the three-developer re-baseline above. |

It exists because a phase total says a block costs 74 h without saying whether it is an Angular or a .NET problem, which is what assigning four or five people actually needs. **Four findings the phase totals hid:** Phase 1 is three unequal problems that genuinely parallelise (1A **139 h pure FE** · 1B **131 BE + 61 RT** · 1C **65 h pure DB**), which is why a fifth developer pays for itself in T1 and nowhere else; **RT is 88 of its 127 h inside Phase 1** and only **39 h across five blocks** thereafter, so the RT owner must take DB or BE work from T2 or idle — `[DE §4]`'s *"RT does not compress"* sharpened to *"and does not spread"*; **`FW-202` is the only BE-heavy block** (29 of 67 h) and is also last and gates Phase 8, so the BE owner's tail is the schedule's tail; and **DB is never the constraint after 1C** — 47 h across seven blocks, none over 15 h. Also records that `FW-081` is dual-tagged **FE/RT** in `[SSP §5]` and is counted wholly as FE, with the alternative split stated so the reading is chosen rather than assumed. **No total changed.** |
| Aug 14, 2026 | Plan team | **Scope reduced on client direction, same day as publication — `DB1` Line Status and `DB2A` Pre-Check-in + weld capture removed. 885 h → 778 h.** New **§1.5** *(published as §1.4 and renumbered when the Phase-1 breakdown took that number)* records what each removal takes with it rather than presenting either as a clean subtraction; the cross-document detail is under [Repository-wide](#repository-wide). In summary: DB1 costs a **minimal landing route** (5 h, since every mockup's back button targeted it), the SPC dialog's **read-only caller**, and **the only consumer of `AlertRaised`/`AlertCleared`**; DB2A + weld **removes two blockers from trial scope** (`G21`, `G26` — both still open for MVP-1) but leaves the trial with **no weld markers, a single-rod spool, and no exercise of the `CoilTraceability` chain behind the welding-wire certificates**, plus **75 test cases out of scope including the contractual weld suite**. Deferred total rose 218 h → **330 h**.<br><br>**Every derived figure was re-derived rather than adjusted:** streams FE 314 · BE 225 · RT 127 · DB 112; sprints T1 395 · T2 240 · T3 143; Phase 1 now **54 %** of the trial. Three developers reach **~1 Oct** on development alone and **a fourth comes within ten hours of closing the whole trial**, so §2.2's recommendation changed from *"staff to five or publish late October"* to **one additional developer, and a second for T1 only**. |
| Aug 14, 2026 | Plan team | **Created** — the client-requested six-screen trial run, shortcode `[TRP]`: four sprints from 17 Aug 2026, **feature-complete Fri 18 Sep, UAT and sign-off 21–30 Sep**. Scope is the six screens (DB2 · DB3-FL1 · DB6 SPC · DB8 WIP · DB5 · DB3-FL2) plus their dependencies, with **every deferral carrying a stated reason** — `FW-001`'s shared-schema renames and `FW-N05`'s real OPC ingest among them. *(Published at 885 h including DB1 and DB2A; reduced to 778 h the same day — see the entry above.)*<br><br>**It is a scoped subset of `[SSP]`, not a replacement** — `[SSP]` remains the staffed plan for all of MVP-1 development at three developers finishing 25 Nov 2026, and this document says so in its own read-first block and in `[SSP]`'s Related Documents. **No figure in `[SSP]`, `[CE]`, `[DE]` or `[RM]` was changed to accommodate it.**<br><br>**§2.2 publishes what each staffing option lands rather than asserting the requested date.** §5 records the two estimate corrections made during drafting, **both upward** — `FW-202` from 52 h to 67 h, and the DB2A block from 24 h to 64 h and then 70 h once it was removed outright, each figure correct against its own baseline and retained so the difference does not read as a discrepancy later. §9 states plainly that the trial proves the screens and not the machine: every line runs `SimulatePLCTagPush` and a simulated OPC feed, so **nothing in it verifies a tag path against a controller** while `G32`/`G33` hold the whole map at `[PROPOSED]`. |
| August 15, 2026 | **Trial blocker 4 (`G6`) closed.** All six roles exist as JWT claims on `ClaimTypes.Role`, so the DB2 supervisor overrides, the SPC skip, the WIP disposition and the spool weight-variance override are all reachable. Its **Stops** and **By** cells are restated rather than merely struck: the residual — the coded claim **values**, still unmapped — stops only the **T1 QA0 walkthrough**, not every role-gated action, and is due **before that walkthrough** rather than before T1 closes on 28 Aug. ⚠ In the supervisor notification a wrong value fails **silent**. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/ShopfloorPlan/back-matter.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Development/GapsRegister.md)

| Date | Changed By | Description |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| August 22, 2026 | **`G47` and `G48` raised from the rod ↔ order design.** **`G47`** — `FlatWireRun.OrderId` is read as *“the order this run produced”* and stops being that at an order boundary, because keeping a rod mounted across the boundary means **one run spans two orders**. Not a design choice: `RodCheckin.RunId` is a single `NOT NULL` column, so closing and reopening the run would need a second check-in, which the client’s rule 7 forbids. **Recommendation: audit the readers, do not widen the column** — widening it to a set breaks every existing reader at once instead of only the boundary cases. **`G48`** — the spool↔order association has **no positional column**, so FL2 cannot place the cut on a two-order spool; and that table’s header documents a **workaround as its design**, reading its order set from shared `planning_routings` *because the rod↔order table did not exist*. Fix is `SpoolWeightFrom`/`To` in **pounds** — weight is conserved through drawing and rolling, footage is not — plus a local derivation, which also removes a shared-schema read from the FL1 path. ⚠ **The `G42` window argument applies literally**: the table was created 22 Aug and nothing writes it yet, so today it is two columns and afterwards a migration plus a backfill against certificate data |
| Aug 20, 2026 | Client direction | **`G42` client-confirmed and its window dated; `G46` raised and closed the same day.** The [20 Aug client call](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-20_SyncPlan.md) settled that a spool carries **many rods and many orders**, and the client's own proposed design — *"the spool coming out of FL1 should have a separate table … that table should contain the one-to-many relationship"* — is `G42`'s recommended `SpoolTraceability` child arrived at independently. So it is now **client direction rather than our proposal**, and it **gains two things** the row never had: a **weight column per source alpha** (Tim: *"how many pounds are on the spool and how many pounds for each alpha"* — `OI-121`, and it rests on `Q10`) and a **spool↔order sibling** (`OI-119`, `Q43`). ⚠ **Both must be `FlatWireDB`-local**, mirroring the `wip_skid_coils` / `wip_coil_orders` **pattern** rather than extending those tables: the client offered either and **`D-32` forbids the shared option**; the `Rod` mirror (`D-04`) is the precedent for a local mirror with enforced FKs. ⚠ **The *"raise it while it is free"* window now has a date — it closes at `S1`, 24 Aug 2026** (`OI-122`): nothing writes the table today, so it is a DDL file and a domain type; once weld capture returns it is a migration plus a backfill against certificate data. Still not a trial blocker, still an MVP-1 obligation. **`G46`** records that `Q37`–`Q40`, `OI-115` and `OI-116` were minted 19 Aug in five documents — including `G45` in this register — and never added to the registers that own them; found, backfilled and closed the same day, with the standing rule that **an id is not minted until it is in its owning register**. |
| Aug 15, 2026 | Plan team | **`G39` raised — a simulator built from our own assumptions becomes the de facto specification of machine behaviour, and nothing reconciles it against the real line.** Every channel the model drives rests on an unconfirmed reading: the `LineStatus` vocabulary is ours (**OI-35**, `C2`), the tag map is `[PROPOSED]` throughout (**G32**, **G33** — the whole map, not isolated rows), the footage→weight basis is undecided and deliberately carries no recommendation (**`Q10`** / **OI-45**), and the cadence has no target (**G9** / **OI-34**), which is already `FW-203`'s own listed blocker.<br><br>**Distinct from `G9`, and the distinction is the whole point:** `G9` is that the NFR *targets* are undefined; `G39` is that the **model is unverified even once they are**. The failure is silent and late — screens render, suites go green, operators train, and the divergence surfaces in the October commissioning window that `[PLCC §4]` already calls the worst compression in the schedule. **A more convincing simulator makes this easier to forget, not harder.**<br><br>Resolution is two-part and **only the first part is delivered**: `[SIM §5.6]`'s **A1–A10 assumption table** makes each modelled behaviour individually confirmable (A8/A9 marked permanent simplifications, A1–A7 provisional), and commissioning step **`C13`** — *record the observed value for every simulated channel and diff it against the model* — is **proposed but not yet written into `[COM]`**. Without `C13` the table is documentation nobody is required to read. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **Appendix C and Related Documents re-pointed after the Jira backlog was split three ways.** `FlatWireJiraStories.md` was reduced to **shopfloor MVP-1 only (35 stories / 147 points)**; Phase 12's 4 / 11 and MVP-2's 6 / 31 are in sibling files, and the **14 upstream stories were deleted** as another team's backlog. The upstream id list is retained with a note that the bodies are **recoverable at commit `1964086`** and that their ids, titles and descriptions remain in `05-SprintPlanAndBacklog.md` §7 — the ids still resolve, and **`FW-061` still depends on `FW-020`**, which is the dependency behind this document's *'if upstream slips, Phase 4 has no material'* risk. Related Documents gained rows for both new files and now cites the backlog's points-vs-hours cross-check (**9.5 h/point** AI-assisted, 7.7 excluding Phase 1). No gap, milestone, week-grid or effort figure in this document changed. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | Gap remediation | **Gap register repaired: two malformed rows completed, two missing entries added.** **`G27` and `G28` had four pipes where every other row has eight** — both were truncated after the Priority cell from the day they were written, so they carried no Area, Impact, Recommended resolution or Status. `G28` is **High** (*FL2 may have no way to record a weld*) and had no recorded disposition at all; both rows are now complete, and `G28`'s resolution states the single client question that settles it and warns against closing it by deleting the disagreeing fixture. **`G26` was cited by [`phase-06`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Development/Phases/phase-06-in-run-production-events.md) from 1 Aug 2026 against no register entry** — `WeldEventPopupPlan.md` §5.3 step **S7** said *"Add G25 and G26"* and only the G25 withdrawal was executed, so a phase file cited an id that resolved to nothing for twelve days. **`G25` is registered, reusing the id** the withdrawn weld-history gap released — the register's own note said *"the next free gap ID remains G25"* — for the requirement-coverage defect described under `06-TestPlanAndTestCases.md`. |

The closing figure is restated: **44/44 remains true as mapping coverage** — every story has a phase, which is what the appendix proves — but **MVP-1 builds 38 of the 44**, and the two `[NEW]` backlog stories absent from this table (`FW-N07`, `FW-N09`) are named so they are not read as uncovered. **Appendix A** gains a Scope column and the `GET /run/{runId}/weldevents` row (**G26**). **Appendix B** gains one too, and its **`4 Weld Event → 6`** row is struck: Dashboard 4 was retired 1 Aug 2026 and `FW-063` has no screen of its own. Die Management is split from Die Change. The **W6 week row** no longer lists `FW-069` among its backlog. **No row was deleted** — the same convention §11 uses, because deleting them tears holes in the coverage evidence. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | Reconcile-up | **Header block added, and Appendix C's coverage claim corrected from 44/44 to 45/45.** This file and `00-foundations.md` were the only two in `ShopfloorPlan/` with no `Project` / `Last Updated` / `Status` block, though `CLAUDE.md` mandates one and all 17 phase files carry it — leaving the live `G##` register with no modification date a reader could check. **`FW-124` · Dashboard 5A — FL2 Spool Queue** (5 pts, `High`, phase 8) was **missing from Appendix C entirely**; it is built by `phase-08`, specified by `02-SRS.md` §5.3a and owned by `SpoolQueue.md`. The row is added and the closing figure restated — **the 44/44 claim was not a miscount, the row simply was not there**, which is the failure mode a coverage appendix exists to prevent. ## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/README.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/README.md)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 24, 2026 | **Seven scripts became eight, and the folder's summary line moved from *“Four render a deliverable”* to five.** A summary-table row and a section document [`build_allocation_examples_xlsx.py`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_allocation_examples_xlsx.py). **Two properties are new to this folder and both are called out, because it is now the template someone will copy.** *(1)* **It computes rather than parses.** The other four builders take every figure from a document; this one carries reference implementations of the conversion, the rod sequence validator and the client's own spool planner, and derives every figure — with an **arithmetic guard** checking those implementations against independently published figures. *(2)* **It extends the leakage list instead of copying it**, because the shared list predates the two tables this workbook is about and an unchanged copy reported *“clean”* on them. The lesson is stated as a rule: **if you copy a guard, check it still covers what you are building** — a guard that cannot fail is worse than none, because it certifies. |
| Aug 18, 2026 | **`G44` added** — the run-end write-back is specified and designed but not built, and its three confirmation values (`Q34`–`Q36`) plus `OI-114` are in no one's queue. **`G36` narrowed** from four dependencies to three as `OI-104` closes. |
| Aug 14, 2026 | **Four scripts became five, and the folder's own one-line summary stopped being true.** It opened *"Three render a client deliverable"*; [`build_trial_run_xlsx.py`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_trial_run_xlsx.py) renders one that is **internal, shared with the client**, so the wording is now *"Four render a deliverable"* and the duplication note reads **three** workbook builders rather than two. A section documents the new script: its ten sheets, its two-source construction, **why the abbreviation guard exists** and the two rules that keep the expansion safe, and a table of all five guards **with the exact message each produced when broken deliberately**.<br><br>⚠ **A callout was added because the summary table's `Writes` column now has an exception.** Every other generator writes into `MVP-1/SRS/`; this one writes into `Development/`, because `MVP-1/SRS/` is where **leakage-guarded** deliverables live and moving the trial-run workbook there would put an unguarded file — one carrying story identifiers, hours and gap identifiers by design — among guarded ones. Also recorded, because it cost a wasted test: **exercising the reconciliation guard means changing a data row, not a footer**, since footer rows are excluded from their own sums on purpose. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | **`build_development_plan_xlsx.py` documented — the fourth script, and the second client workbook builder.** Adds a summary-table row and a full section: what it reads, the seven sheets, the two-source construction, its guards, and the two figures it deliberately withholds. **Three notes are load-bearing.** *(1)* **`Reference title` is a mirror of the plan's own title, not authored prose** — paraphrasing it leaves the drift guard checking nothing, which is exactly what happened on the first build: 60 of 107 entries had hand-typed approximations and the guard rejected every one. *(2)* The two workbook builders **share their helpers by duplication, not import**, so a change to one deliverable's guard cannot silently change the other's. *(3)* Bare `Angular`, `.NET` and `SQL` are **not** blocked by the leakage guard — they name the technology, which the client already knows. Also corrected a stale link label left by the 13 Aug consolidation: the `ClientQuestionsContent.md` reference read `../../RequirementDocuments/…` against a target that is local to this folder. |
| Aug 12, 2026 | **`build_questions_xlsx.py` — Excel no longer repairs the workbook on open, and `Recommended answer` became optional.** Two changes, one a real defect. **(1) The repair.** Excel had been reporting *"Repaired Records: String properties from /xl/worksheets/sheet2.xml"* on every open — four repair logs in `%TEMP%` on 12 Aug alone — and the cause was a **whitespace-only rich-text run**. `render()` splits authored `**bold**` into runs, so two bold spans separated by a single space emit a run containing just `" "`; **openpyxl 3.1.5 writes `xml:space="preserve"` on a whitespace-*edged* run (`" x "`) but omits it on a whitespace-*only* one**, so XML normalisation strips the content and Excel sees an invalid run. Reproduced in isolation against openpyxl directly, so it is an upstream bug, not a data problem. Fixed in our code by `_fold_blank_runs()`, which **never emits such a run** — the whitespace is folded into an adjacent run instead of dropped, so the text is byte-identical (a bold space renders the same as a plain one). The single offending cell was `D27`, the `Q83` die-life decision. Verified by opening the rebuilt workbook through Excel COM: **no new repair log**. The defect was independent of any content change and had been shipping in the client deliverable. **(2)** `Recommended answer` moved from `REQUIRED_OPEN` to the optional set beside `Options` and `Already agreed`, because `Q19` (footage-to-weight) deliberately carries no recommendation; `build()` was also switched to `.get()` for that field, a missing required field having previously been guaranteed by the guard, and the Open Questions caption relaxed from *"each with our recommended answer"* to *"almost all with"*. |
| Aug 6, 2026 | **Flowcharts, and two smaller fixes.** ```` ```mermaid ```` fences were rendering as **source** in the client deliverable — `PassScheduleGenerationSpec.md` has two, §6.1's thirteen-step calculation sequence and §6.2's route tree, and both reached the client as raw mermaid text. They are now **drawn as boxes and lines** out of table cells (see above). Also: **HTML comments are stripped**, since `<!-- TOC -->` was rendering as a literal paragraph on the first page; and the script now **refuses to render onto `MVP-2/SRS/PassScheduleGenerationSpec.docx`**, which is its own branding template — `shutil.copyfile` raises `SameFileError` on that path, so rebuilding *that* file means rendering to a temp path and moving it into place. |
| Aug 4, 2026 | **Created.** `build_docx.py` committed after being re-derived three times — `CLAUDE.md` had recorded the missing render pipeline as a recurring problem, and the scripts it named (`extract.py`, `assemble.py`, `build_docx.py`, `verify_docx.py`, `slim_docx.py`) lived only in a session scratchpad and were lost each time. This one is parameterised over source, output and header title so it is not specific to one document. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | **Documented the two undocumented scripts.** The README covered `build_docx.py` only, while `build_questions_xlsx.py` had been committed and in use since 12 Aug 2026 — the same omission being corrected across the inventories this pass. Adds a three-row summary table at the top (what each reads, writes and fails on), a full section on `build_questions_xlsx.py` including its four fatal guards and the note that **`Recommended answer` and `Why` are optional for exactly one reason** and must not be restored to `REQUIRED_OPEN`, and a section on the new `build_coverage_matrix.py`. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_development_plan_xlsx.py](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_development_plan_xlsx.py)

| Date | Changed By | Description |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **Created — the development plan as a client deliverable.** Seven sheets to `MVP-1/SRS/FlatWire_DevelopmentPlan.xlsx`: *Read Me · Delivery Options · Plan by Stage · Sprint Schedule · Work Items · Milestones and Needs · Assumptions and Risks*. Built to the same two-source shape as `build_questions_xlsx.py` — **structure parsed, prose authored** — with `DevelopmentPlanContent.md` as the only home of the client wording and `TaskBreakdown.md` / `StaffedSprintPlans.md` supplying every figure, so a plan change propagates on the next run rather than being retyped.<br><br>**The prose had to be written, not filtered, and the count is why: 68 of the 116 work-item titles trip the leakage guard** — 40 carry code spans, 11 name endpoints, 10 use screen numbers, 7 name database tables. Deleting those does not leave a sentence. The raw material is each story's `So that` clause, **only 4 of which trip the guard**; they were already client language, and every *Delivers* line is built from one. Seeded mechanically from the `As a / I want / So that` triads, then hand-edited — the seeds were a starting point, not the deliverable.<br><br>**Two presentation rules are enforced in code rather than trusted to the author.** Effort is converted to **days** at write time and hours never appear — hours invite a rate conversation. The **unit rate card is never exported**: per-stage and per-item totals are fine, *what a screen costs* is internal pricing mechanics. A small `plain()` pass rewrites internal shorthand in *parsed commentary only* (`Q4` → *the fourth quarter*), never in a figure.<br><br>**Four fatal guards**, coverage and drift keyed on the plan's work items and phases. `LEAKS` gains two patterns over the questions workbook — **library names** and **code identifiers** (anything ending `Service`, `Controller`, `Repository`, `Component`, `Hub`, `Dto`, `Handler`) — chosen because they are what survives a careless content edit. **Verified by deliberate breach**, twice: a repository path, a requirement id, a backlog id, an endpoint, a service name, a library name and a table name were injected into content fields; all seven were named with sheet and cell coordinates, the workbook was deleted and the build exited 1.<br><br>**Three defects found and fixed during verification, all of the same class — parsed structure taken at face value.** The staffed-plan tables carry a **totals row with no sprint label**, which was being counted as a sprint and made the schedule sheet contradict its own headline (11/8/6 sprints against 10/7/5); it is now detected and labelled *Total*. Sprint dates carry **the year on the closing row only**, which is fine read top to bottom and wrong on a milestone sheet where *08 Jan* stands alone and is not 2026; the year is recovered by rolling over when the month goes backwards. And **60 of 107 `Reference title` fields were hand-typed paraphrases**, which the drift guard rejected outright — they are now synced verbatim from the plan, since a paraphrased mirror checks nothing. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_trial_run_xlsx.py](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_trial_run_xlsx.py)

*The cross-document pass that produced this script is recorded once under [Repository-wide](#repository-wide). This section carries only what is about the script itself.*

| Date | Changed By | Description |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| August 25, 2026 | Plan team | **Re-baselined with `[TRP §2]`: `HOURS_PER_DAY` 8 → 6.5, and the Days column now says which.** The constant feeds `days()` and therefore the derived column on **five** sheets, so changing it silently would have left every figure readable as 8 h days — the header is renamed **`Days (6.5 h)`** at all six call sites for exactly that reason, and the docstring records that a “day” in this workbook is a 6.5 h day. Development effort now reports **869 hours (133.7 days)**, unchanged in hours. `ISSUE_DATE` → August 25, 2026.<br><br>**`SPRINT_NAME`'s four date strings were the trap** — they are **not** parsed from the plan, they are the row labels on the Sprint Allocation and Work Items sheets, and they still read *(17–28 August)* while the plan had moved. Moved by hand and a comment added saying they always must be. The Sprint Plan subtitle likewise moved from *“Four sprints from 17 August”* to *“Four blocks of work from 31 August”*.<br><br>**One parser anchor was brittle and is now keyed on identity rather than wording**: `_rows_after(text, '### 2.1 Recommended shape', …)` matched the heading's *prose*, so retitling §2.1 for the three-resource shape failed the build outright with *“heading not found”*. Anchored on **`'### 2.1'`**. The guard behaved correctly — it refused to build rather than silently reading the wrong table — but a section heading is not a stable key.<br><br>**No guard, `TOTAL_HOURS`, `PLATFORM_HOURS` or `DEFERRED_HOURS` was touched**; effort did not move, only its calendar. All five guards pass and the abbreviation scan is clean. **The over-capacity WARNING is gone** — the build emitted it for T1 and T3 on the 8 h basis, and its absence is the positive signal that the new capacity figures parsed. |
| Aug 14, 2026 | Plan team | **Created** — the trial-run workbook builder, writing ten sheets from `[TRP]` + `[TB]` + `TrialRunContent.md`. **61 work items, 778 hours**, every figure parsed rather than typed.<br><br>**Three parser problems worth recording, because each is the kind of thing that returns.** A helper that found *"the first table under a heading"* could not address the plan's sections that carry **two or three tables before their first sub-heading** — §1.3 alone has three — so it was rewritten to return the **nth** table, bounded by heading depth, with an `expect` assertion matched against the **whole header row** rather than the first cell, since several of the plan's tables deliberately leave the first column heading blank. **Footer rows are the second trap and they cut both ways**: the block table's footer restates the grand total and must be dropped or the sum double-counts to 1,556; the three-bases footer carries the totals the guards check and must be kept — dropping it made `1C` look like the total and raised a bare `StopIteration`. Both are now filtered on a *data* condition rather than on a blank first cell. Third, the plan's **final sprint carries em dashes** for capacity and utilisation because it is testing and sign-off with no development hours — a deliberate dash is not a parse failure and now defaults to zero.<br><br>**The expander and the guard must agree on their word boundary, and for one build they did not.** The expander used `[\w-]`, the guard `\b`, so a hyphen-joined compound like `DB3-FL2` was **flagged but never expanded** — an unfixable failure from the user's side. The expander now uses `\w`, matching the guard. Code identifiers stay safe because they join on word characters and underscores, never hyphens: `PLCTagService`, `FlatWireDbContext` and `sp_GetGaugeTrace` are all untouched, and no hyphen-delimited segment of `run-status-cards` or `chart-tab-strip` is an abbreviation. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_allocation_examples_xlsx.py](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_allocation_examples_xlsx.py)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| August 24, 2026 | **First issue.** The rod-to-order worked-example workbook — `MVP-1/SRS/FlatWire_OrderAllocationExamples.xlsx`, thirteen sheets, 24 rules, 60 worked examples. The repository's **eighth** script and **fourth** workbook builder, duplicating its helpers per the standing rule. Sheets: *Read Me · The Rules · Worked Examples · One Rod, One Order · Two Rods, One Spool · Order Handoff · Processing Order Options · Weight and Footage · Order Completion · Full Planned Run · Heavier Rod Run · Orders Across the Run · What We Need Confirmed*.<br><br>**Why it exists.** `RodOrderAllocation.md` states outright that the sequence tiers, the handoff state machine and the fulfilment rollup are *“our reading of the client rules, not client-confirmed text”*, and `Q48` is `Critical` and open. Twenty requirements and twenty-one test cases were written against that reading with **nothing the client could read back**. Every rule now appears in plain language with an accept case and a refuse case, and the client's own planner run is reproduced end to end.<br><br>**It is the first generator whose figures are computed rather than parsed**, and the arithmetic guard is what makes that safer than transcribing rather than riskier. It re-derives the conversion (`A = t·w` square, `A = t·w − 0.2146·t²` round, `k = 12ρ`), the four-tier sequence validator and the client planner including `VALIDATE FINAL STOP POSSIBILITY`, then checks all three against figures published independently: **all twenty conversion cells in `[DBD §6.6]`**, the three worked footage figures in the design document, and the planner's own published **11 rods · 23 spools · 45 stops · 40,400 lb · 91.82 % · 9 welded spools** — all six of which the corrected port reproduces exactly.<br><br>⚠ **The three defects `FL Alphas Plus - Analysis.md` §6 says not to port are corrected, and correcting them is free on the shipped inputs** — 1,800 mod 900 = 0 leaves two of them latent and the rod count agrees at 11 — which is precisely why the corrected port still matches the published totals. A port that reproduced the defects would agree too, and would be wrong for any other input.<br><br>**Five guards, all fatal, every one proved by breaking it deliberately**: arithmetic, coverage (each rule has an accept and a non-accept example; all twenty-four enumeration values, eight state transitions and five order statuses appear somewhere), drift, team names, leakage. Plus **two warnings that are deliberately not fatal**, on the trial-run builder's reasoning that a guard refusing to build a document for saying something uncomfortable just gets switched off.<br><br>⚠ **The leakage guard was extended, and the extension is the finding.** The copied list was written before `RodOrderAllocation` and `RodOrderConsumption` existed, so it reported *“clean”* on the two table names this workbook is most likely to leak. Also added: the internal rule-code pattern, the `DF_`/`PK_`/`UQ_` constraint prefixes, and eight further table names. **Three source strings could not be used at all** because they trip the guard: the seeded order numbers and the local coil alpha both match the backlog-identifier pattern, and are replaced by the six-digit order number and the shared coil alpha — both forms already documented elsewhere, and both the ones the client actually uses.<br><br>⚠ **A one-unit rounding tolerance was found to be wide enough to hide a formula error, and was closed two ways.** Perturbing the round-edge constant from `0.2146` to `0.2100` moved five of the twenty cells by exactly one unit each and passed as five rounding warnings. The constant is now asserted directly against `1 − π/4`, and the **count** of cells needing the tolerance is asserted at exactly one — alloy 5052, round edge, drawing-line gauge, where `[DBD §6.6]` rounds `0.077082` down to `0.0770` against the formula's `0.0771`. That single cell is the only rounding disagreement in the published table. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_coverage_matrix.py](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Tools/build_coverage_matrix.py)

| Date | Changed By | Description |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| Aug 13, 2026 | Gap remediation | **Created — requirement coverage is now measured, not asserted.** Parses the requirement rows of `02-SRS.md` and the case rows of `06-TestPlanAndTestCases.md` and **exits non-zero** if any MVP-1 requirement has neither a test case nor a §10.4 entry. Written to the same shape as the coverage guard in `build_questions_xlsx.py`. Three precedence levels count as covered — **direct** (a §5 case names the requirement in its *FR / Source* column, that column and not the whole row), **indirect** (the SRS `NFR` table names both the requirement and a `TC-###`), **declared** (§10.4 records it with a reason) — on the principle that *a declared hole is a decision and an undeclared one is an accident*, and only the second fails the build. Withdrawn requirements (`~~**FR-###**~~`, four of them) are excluded; **`FR-111` is one, and counting it is how a false positive enters**. Carries an explicit non-goal: `05-SprintPlanAndBacklog.md` §11 has the same range granularity but **no per-requirement data to check against**, so its story assignments are reported as an advisory count that never affects exit status. |

Adds a story inventory mapping each story to its **descope rung** with all-in and development hours on both delivery bases, whose per-story dev split (**24/36/20/48** hand-coded → **16/25/15/33** AI) reconciles cell-for-cell against that sheet's rung table. Adds a points cross-check (**8.1 dev-h per point** AI-assisted, 11.6 hand-coded, 11.5 / 16.1 all-in) with an explicit warning **not** to read the closeness to the conventional ~8 h/point as validation, since the points are April-dated and the factors are assumed. **The document carries a §3 stating that it is itself the only requirement text for three of its four stories** — `FW-101`, `FW-102` and `FW-110` have no `FR-` IDs and Phase 12 has no owning specification, so the acceptance criteria are load-bearing in a way no other story file's are. **Two defects raised on `FW-101`. (1)** Its dependencies are `FW-063, FW-090` here and **`FW-095`** in `ProjectPlan/05-SprintPlanAndBacklog.md`; both cannot be right, on a story that is critical-path for the welding-wire certificates. **(2)** Its last two acceptance criteria — the *"Flat Wire"* checkbox and the three field renames — **belong to a different descope rung**: the ladder puts the yield form in **rung 4** while those criteria are written into `FW-101`, which is **rung 3**, so deferring rung 3 as intended would defer two criteria the ladder believes it keeps. Effort is unaffected (same phase); the **descope decision** is not. Three editorial fixes while transcribing: `FW-102`'s duplicated `OI-68` citations consolidated (its AC read *"after OI-68 and OI-68 are decided"*), `FW-110`'s duplicated `OI-83` likewise, and an **`Open Questions: OI-60`** line **added** to `FW-101`, which had none despite `phase-12` listing `OI-60` as one of its blockers. The two stale April epic priorities (*"required by August 1 production"*) are struck rather than deleted. |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/YieldCostAndScrapSheet.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Development/YieldCostAndScrapSheet.md)

| Date | Changed By | Description |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **§4 gained a callout: rungs 3 and 4 disagree about who owns the yield form.** The ladder labels rung 4 *"footage-based weight + yield form"*, but the *"Flat Wire"* checkbox and the three field renames are written into **`FW-101`'s** acceptance criteria, and `FW-101` is **rung 3** — so deferring rung 3 as the ladder intends would defer two criteria the ladder believes it is keeping. This sheet **continues to price the ladder's split** (rung 3 carries no FE; rung 4 carries the 20 FE for the form), which is the right basis for effort either way since both rungs sit in the same phase and the total is unaffected — it is the **descope decision** that breaks, so the note is filed in §4 rather than §3. No figure changed. Surfaced while splitting the stories into [`YieldCostAndScrapJiraStories.md`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Development/YieldCostAndScrapStories.md), which carries the full statement on `FW-101`. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **Promoted to the sole home of Phase 12's figures.** Phase 12 was **removed from [`DevelopmentEffortModel.md`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Development/DevelopmentEffortModel.md)** the same day, so the rounding note no longer defers to that sheet's §2 for the 89 h development aggregate — it states which split to quote for which reconciliation (the aggregate FE 27 / BE 54 / DB 8 / RT 0 against the rate card; the rung table against the ladder) and records that the dev sheet now cites 89 h from here rather than the reverse, with its MVP-1 figure re-based to **1,397 h** and `1,397 + 89 = 1,486 h` being complete MVP-1 development. The *Related Documents* row for the dev sheet was rewritten to say its per-phase table **excludes** Phase 12. No figure in this sheet changed. |
| Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **Initial publication — a standalone sheet for Phase 12 (Yield, Cost Ledger & Scrap).** Decomposes the phase's published **177 h** to **story level** on both delivery bases, using the descope ladder's own **33 / 49 / 28 / 67** split — which is why the four rungs reconcile to the phase exactly. **Both effort tables close in both directions**: the rung totals equal the ladder's published figures and the five discipline columns equal the phase row's published 44 / 72 / 12 / 26 / 23. Adds the AI-assisted view at **126 h** (28.8% saved, the **second-least-compressible phase** after Phase 14) from `DevelopmentEffortModel.md` §1, noting that its BE **0.75** is the least favourable BE factor in the plan because yield attribution and cost standards are domain reasoning, not boilerplate — Phase 14 resists AI assistance because it needs the mill, Phase 12 because it needs **decisions**. **Two findings that should change how the phase is scheduled, not just how it is sized. (1) Three of its four stories have no requirement specification at all** — `FW-101`, `FW-102` and `FW-110` carry **no `FR-` IDs anywhere in `02-SRS.md`**, and Phase 12 has **no owning requirement document**, being absent from both `MVP-1/RequirementDocuments/` and the Dashboard Inventory; the only requirement text in existence is the Jira acceptance criteria, so the 177 h was priced against four cards. Verified, not assumed — and the scrap requirements that *do* exist (`FR-063`, `FR-066`, `FR-189`, `FR-273`, `FR-292`) govern the **check-in scrap box** and **WIP dispositions**, **not** FW-110's outlet selection, so the phase must not be read as specified because the word "scrap" appears in the SRS. `FW-100`'s formula *is* specified (`FR-332`/`FR-332a`) but under §5.16, which the SRS maps to **DB7 / Phase 9**. **(2) All four stories are blocked, and three blockers are the client's** — `Q10` (Critical, and the one register question deliberately carrying no recommendation), `OI-60`, `OI-68`, `OI-83`. Separates **what is buildable today** (FW-001 field renames, the weight-service structure, the settled `Scrap Box`/`Scrap Skid` vocabulary, the attribution mechanics) from **what no factor makes buildable** (cost-standard values, expected yield per route, the confirmed weight basis). Records that **AI assistance weakens this ladder** — the four rungs recover 126 h instead of 177 h, removing **5.3 FTE from W7 rather than 7.4**. Flags a contradiction to resolve while writing the missing spec: `FW-101`'s dependencies are `FW-063, FW-090` in `FlatWireJiraStories.md` and `FW-095` in `05-SprintPlanAndBacklog.md`. A decompose-then-round artifact is **stated rather than hidden** — FE 26 / DB 9 here against the aggregate's FE 27 / DB 8, differing by 1 h in opposite directions and therefore agreeing on both totals that matter (126 h all-in, 89 h development-only). |

## [MVP-1/ProjectPlan/02-SRS.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/BusinessRequirements.md)

| Date | Changed By | Description |
|------|-----------|-------------|
| August 22, 2026 | **§5.28 added — `FR-541`–`FR-560`, carrying rule codes `ORD003`–`ORD017`** — for the rod ↔ order many-to-many, which nothing in the repository persisted. **`FR-533`–`FR-540` are left unused**, reserved by the 20 Aug ledger's `W3` for the FL2 pre-check-in. **Two departures from the source rules are stated in the section rather than absorbed:** the per-pairing split point is held in **pounds, not footage** (weight is conserved through drawing and rolling, footage is not — the same 900 lb is ≈ 11,100 ft at FL1 gauge and ≈ 76,300 ft at FL2), and the conversion is **one implementation with a selectable basis** rather than a pluggable formula, because the formula is settled by `FR-137` and it is the *dimensional basis* (`Q10`) that is open. **`FR-550` is the one to read first:** because the rod stays mounted there is no second check-in and therefore **no second PLC tag push**, so both orders necessarily run under the first order's pass schedule — a differing schedule means the *mounted handoff* is refused, not the order. Whether planning can produce that case is **`Q48`**, `Critical`. ⚠ The section closes with the consequence for existing readers: one run now spans two orders, so `FlatWireRun.OrderId` narrows to *“the order at check-in”* and anything reading it as *“the order this run produced”* is silently wrong at a boundary (`OI-123` / `G47`) |
| Aug 2, 2026 | Plan team | Adds **§5.3a — Spool Queue (Dashboard 5A)** with `FR-097`–`FR-099`, taking the total to **366**. A new screen, numbered into the gap between §5.3 and §5.4 rather than appended, so it sits beside the check-in screen it feeds. It exists because FL2 had no view of waiting material (FL1 has DB2A; `PCI002` excludes FL2 from staging) and because `FR-090`'s *scan the label* and **Q27**'s *select it by spool number* both stand while only the first had a screen. It is also the first thing actually named "the spool queue", a phrase `FR-326` and `TC-389` use with nothing behind it. | Jul 30, 2026 | Plan team | Initial publication. Carries all **363 functional requirements** (`FR-001`–`FR-508`) forward from `FlatWire_MasterSpecification.md` §4 with numbers unchanged, in the same 25 operator-workflow groups, each with screen, source IDs, actors, preconditions, priority, validation, actions, state changes, error paths and real-time events. **NFRs are folded in** — inline `[NFR]` rows in the constraining group plus the §6 register of the ten NFRs that are actually cited, with measurable targets and `TC-###` verification. Adds the domain model with mermaid state machines, the process flows, the UI specification, the role × capability matrix, the external-interface surfaces and a full traceability appendix. | Aug 1, 2026 | Client sync (30 Jul call) | **Eight requirements changed, seven added; no existing number reused or renumbered.** FR-045/046/047: **off-schedule is no longer a deviation** — the system auto-selects the correct station, and the override columns are dropped (OQ-38). FR-048: staging **no longer sets `coils.coil_status`** (OQ-68). FR-042/FR-065: diameter validates against a **min/max band**, unseeded pending the values owed by e-mail (OQ-35). FR-044: flagged **knowingly wrong** for a multi-order rod (OQ-70 / G22). Added **FR-052a** (pre-check-out approval depends on the weld; welded needs a supervisor and goes to HOLD), **FR-053a** (a WIP rejection releases a blocked bay — the only thing that does), and **FR-130a–d** (customer min/max weight basis; short close as an unplanned stop on the 10-90 pattern; the spool is run off regardless because FL2 has no stripper; a mid-run coil break restarts the stop from zero). The domain-model note on pre-check-in coil status is **resolved** in favour of `RECEIVED → STAGED`. |
| 1 Aug 2026 | Build decision | **DB6, DB8 and DC are dialogs, not screens.** SPC checkpoint, WIP rejection and die change moved out of `dashboard_6_spc_checkpoint.html` / `dashboard_8_wip_rejection.html` / `dashboard_die_change.html` into `spc_checkpoint.js` / `wip_rejection.js` / `die_change.js`, opened as popups over the screen the operator is already on; the three `.html` files remain as launchers so every reference to them still resolves. **No requirement text changed** — the requirements are unaffected by the container. What changed is that each dialog now receives its material context from the caller instead of hard-coding one run, which is what finally lets the pre-check-in rejection path (Q36 item 3) be represented: no run, no footage position, and submitting releases the bay. Two hand-offs the spec already described also become real — a gauge-drift or size-change die change opens the SPC checkpoint it mandates, and an out-of-spec checkpoint's *suspend material* opens the WIP rejection with the failing reading carried over. DB6 gains a **read-only mode** for DB1's "SPC · Last check … · View →", which reviews a recorded checkpoint rather than opening a blank form. New open item **Q43**: DB1's "WIP Rejection**s**" nav item reads as a list screen that has never been specified. |
| 1 Aug 2026 | Build decision | **Pause/Resume redesigned; DB12 becomes a dialog; OI-14 closed at four outcomes.** The fifteen pause reasons were a flat radio list ~1100px tall — the last screen in the suite on radio buttons, and tall enough that the dialog was scaled on every window, taking its 14px labels under the shopfloor floor. They are now glove-sized cards in one column per category and the dialog fits the 1280×1024 panel at 1:1. **Rod Checkout is no longer a pause reason** — it was the only one of fifteen that did not pause, rendered identically to the fourteen that did — and is now the `CheckOutRod` **resume outcome**, which `POST /run/{runId}/resume` and `CK_RunPauseEvent_Outcome` already accepted. That closes **OI-14** and supersedes **FR-262**. **Rod Checkout itself (DB12) moved into `rod_checkout.js`**, so Mode B opens over the pause that raised it with the frozen footage carried over rather than navigating away and losing the pause. Four correctness fixes went with the redesign, all of which the mockup could previously get wrong: the payload now carries **`ReasonCode` + `ReasonCategory`** rather than a display label (`Other` used to overwrite its own code with the note text); **notes are mandatory on `Other`**, matching `CK_RunPauseEvent_NotesOther`; footage comes from the caller rather than a `#footage-val` element that **does not exist on the FL1 monitor**, where the dialog had always shown “Footage —”; and duration reads **h:mm:ss** past an hour instead of reporting a 90-minute stop as “90:00”. New requirements `FR-261a`–`FR-261b` and `FR-266a`–`FR-266b` record the rules; no existing number was reused. |
| Aug 4, 2026 | Client direction | **FM2 roller-size correction.** FM2 has **three** stands — **S1 = 8″, S2 = 6″, S3 = 6″** — with edgers at S2 and S3 and S3 final. The 8″ roller **is S1**: §3.1's *"three 6″ stands"* (from the May 21 note) had been read as a separate 8″ roller upstream of three 6″ stands, i.e. four components, and `FM2_6inS3` never corresponded to real equipment. **§3.1 rewritten** with the per-stand table; **§3.2 component vocabulary** becomes position-only `FM2_S1` / `FM2_S2` / `FM2_S3`, with roll diameter moved to the new `Stand.RollDiameterIn` column (12.000 / 8.000 / 6.000 / 6.000) so diameter can never again hide inside an identifier. **`FR-094`, `FR-096`, `FR-297`, `FR-386`, `FR-387` reworded; no number reused.** `FR-387`'s multipliers are unchanged (1.06 / 1.02 / springback) — they move from diameter labels to positions, which fixes a pre-existing defect where three formulas covered four stands and the final stand had none. **`OI-04` closed:** SRS §2.7's *"6″ S3"* and the DDL/API/`HMI008` rule *"`FM2_6inS2` must always be Active"* named **the same physical stand**, now `FM2_S3` — the phantom fourth stand created the appearance of a contradiction. **`OI-36` closed:** the client's published tag map carries exactly three FM2 stations, so it was never incomplete; PLC stations are renamed to bare position (`FL2.FM2.S1/S2/S3`) pending sign-off as **`PLC-Q04`**. See decision **D-26** in `[MS §10.2]`. |
| Aug 4, 2026 | Client direction | **HMI/SCADA descoped.** Dashboard 13 (HMI Line Schematic), Dashboard 14 (SCADA Trends) and the Machine View tab on the active run monitor are **withdrawn at client request**. `FR-111`, `FR-112`, `FR-114`, `FR-425`, `FR-440`–`FR-451` and `FR-460`–`FR-470` are marked **withdrawn** — numbers retained, never renumbered — and `FR-113` **reworded**, since it asserted a rule about "the active tab" that outlives the tabs. Both mockups and `HMIAndSCADALayout.md` are deleted. **Descope-ladder rung 7 is removed entirely:** its 67 h stops being *recoverable* effort and becomes *never-planned*, so Phase 5 drops 221 → ~154 h and the programme 3,727 → ~3,660 h, but the ladder loses its largest optional rung and Phase 5 is no longer deferrable. **Nothing structural was removed:** all six run event markers still land on the DB3 traces and no hub event, endpoint, table or column is deleted — every DB13/DB14 reference in the real-time and tag tables was a *consumer* entry, not a row. `Q51`/`OQ-51` is **superseded**, because Dashboard 14 was its answer. §5.21 and §5.22 replaced with withdrawal notices recording what survives and where it went. **Two text fixes still owed here:** `FR-073`/`FR-074` describe the tag batch as *"a single transactional batch"* that *"shall be rolled back"* — machine writes are not transactional and the recovery is a **compensating re-clear** (gap **G16**, which closes on this edit); and `FR-073` says *speed **limits*** where the interface section said *speed **targets***, which is a real ambiguity asked as **`PLC-Q06`** and deliberately left unfixed until it closes. **PLC tag surface consolidated.** The surface existed in six partial, mutually contradictory copies; it now has one home in [`PLCTagSpecification.md`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/PLCTagSpecification.md) (client-facing, `[PLC]`, with its own `PLC-Q##` register) plus `MVP-1/DevelopmentPlan/PLCTagImplementation.md` (internal). This document’s tag-surface section is reduced to a pointer, keeping only what is genuinely its own job. |

Conventions, canonical enums with the three-way mirroring rule, all **30 endpoints** with request/response shapes, validation, side effects, error codes and idempotency, the **`FlatWireHub` contract with all 10 events**, the PLC/OPC surface, the stub-first delivery contract, versioning policy and full traceability. **Corrects the four Tier-1 defects in the published April contract** — the `/passschedule/generate` worked example (`0.3732` / `0.95 %` / `Hybrid`, not `0.265` / `50.1` / `Standalone`), the missing `RollAdjustTrigger` checkpoint type, the component-state boolean, and the three edge-type vocabularies — plus two further corrections (the three `NOT NULL` fields missing from `POST /checkin/rod`, and the `ComponentName`/`PayoffPosition`/`LineState` enum fixes). Records **PP-04**: the hub event count is ten, not nine. |
| Aug 1, 2026 | Client sync (30 Jul call) | **`/staging/**`, `/checkin/rod`, `/checkout` and `/wipreject` updated.** The `offSchedule` override object is **removed** — a rod booked on the other rod line makes the client **switch station** (`scheduledLineId` from `GET /rod/{alpha}`), and a mismatched POST returns `409 WRONG_STATION` with `correctLineId` (Q38). Staging **no longer sets `coils.coil_status`** — `INFLAT` is set at check-in (Q68). A failed inspection now returns **`201` + `state:"Blocked"`** (the 31 Jul contract change, not previously carried here), and **`POST /wipreject` releases the blocked row** — the only thing that clears a `Blocked` bay (Q36 item 3). `POST /staging/rod/unstage` and `POST /checkout` Mode P gain a **weld-dependent supervisor approval** with `HOLD` (Q69/Q72), and `/checkout` gains the approval columns the table never had — retro-enforcing OQ-74 for Mode B (**G24**). `CHK007` becomes a **min/max band**, unseeded pending the values owed by e-mail (Q35). Flagged **G22** (order membership is knowingly wrong for a multi-order rod, Q70/Q73) and **Q40** (an order scheduled on neither rod line has no station to switch to). |
| Aug 4, 2026 | Client direction | **HMI/SCADA descoped.** Dashboard 13 (HMI Line Schematic), Dashboard 14 (SCADA Trends) and the Machine View tab on the active run monitor are **withdrawn at client request**. `FR-111`, `FR-112`, `FR-114`, `FR-425`, `FR-440`–`FR-451` and `FR-460`–`FR-470` are marked **withdrawn** — numbers retained, never renumbered — and `FR-113` **reworded**, since it asserted a rule about "the active tab" that outlives the tabs. Both mockups and `HMIAndSCADALayout.md` are deleted. **Descope-ladder rung 7 is removed entirely:** its 67 h stops being *recoverable* effort and becomes *never-planned*, so Phase 5 drops 221 → ~154 h and the programme 3,727 → ~3,660 h, but the ladder loses its largest optional rung and Phase 5 is no longer deferrable. **Nothing structural was removed:** all six run event markers still land on the DB3 traces and no hub event, endpoint, table or column is deleted — every DB13/DB14 reference in the real-time and tag tables was a *consumer* entry, not a row. `Q51`/`OQ-51` is **superseded**, because Dashboard 14 was its answer. **Zero endpoints and zero hub events deleted** — consumer columns only, and §5.4 renamed from *SCADA event markers* to *Run event markers*. **PLC tag surface consolidated.** The surface existed in six partial, mutually contradictory copies; it now has one home in [`PLCTagSpecification.md`](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Architecture/PLCTagSpecification.md) (client-facing, `[PLC]`, with its own `PLC-Q##` register) plus `MVP-1/DevelopmentPlan/PLCTagImplementation.md` (internal). This document’s tag-surface section is reduced to a pointer, keeping only what is genuinely its own job. The `PLCTagService` signatures and the two error codes stay here — that is the API contract’s job. Also renamed for clarity: the state enum becomes **`LineOperatingState`** and the hub event **`LineStateChanged`**, so **`LineState`** unambiguously means the machine tag (`[PLC §6]`). |

Measured per requirement for the first time: **41 MVP-1 requirements are named by no case, 32 of them `Must`**. §10.2 (*every case names its requirement*) was true, machine-verified, and **the opposite direction** — a suite can name a requirement on every case and still leave requirements untested. **The denominator was wrong too:** **363** was the pre-split MVP-1 + MVP-2 total where `02-SRS.md` now carries **263**, §5.10/§5.18/§5.19/§5.23/§5.24 having left on 11 Aug 2026. Registered as gap **`G25`**. **The 41 cases are still owed**; this pass made the number honest, not the coverage complete. |

| 1.3 | Aug 22, 2026 | **§2.6 added — the allocation-reached notification and the completion action, neither of which had a screen.** The notification is raised **server-side** on the footage crossing (not a client threshold check, not the operator noticing), is **durable** across a refresh and re-delivered on reconnect following the spool-completion prompt's contract, and shows a weight **latched at the crossing instant** that must never be replaced with a fresher live figure. **`Mark order complete` is the only thing that closes the order** `[ORD007]`, and it is available **before** the threshold too, because an early acknowledgement is permitted. ⚠ **Two things the screen must not imply:** that the line has stopped — it has not, and the material produced meanwhile is the recorded overrun — and that the acknowledgement confirms a system decision; the system notifies, the **operator decides**. Open: **`Q51`** (the early-ack remainder) and **`Q50`** (the escalation bound — warn and escalate, never stop) |
| 1.0 | Aug 11, 2026 | **First issue.** Created as the owning specification for Dashboard 3, which previously existed only inside the shopfloor dashboard design reference. Carries the trace rules including the FL2 historical-profile behaviour and the August 4 Machine View / View Trends descope, the payoff thresholds with the absolute-weight rule, the per-line action sets, the pause reason vocabulary as codes and categories, the four resume outcomes closing OI-14, and the shift-summary contribution. **Settled the Roll Adjust line scope as FL2 / FL3, not FL1** — four sources disagreed; `FR-107`–`FR-109` and all three active-run mockups agree, and were verified directly. Also added §2.5, the mid-run configuration-change acknowledgement flow, folded in from the operations-manager role document, which was its only home. Screen styling, layout dimensions, scripting detail, endpoint names and internal verification notes removed for the client issue. |
| 1.2 | Aug 15, 2026 | **Reissued as version 1.2 — the `## Document Change History` block removed.** The block is gone from the specification and its rows are folded into this section, which now carries a **Version** column; the client `.docx` no longer prints a history section, and the document's version is stated in its header block only. **No requirement, decision, open item or sign-off row changed.** This completes the 12 Aug 2026 consolidation, which had left these thirteen blocks in place as deliverable content. | 1.5 | Aug 18, 2026 | **New §13 — what the shared system records when a coil is completed, and `OI-104` answered.** The screens in §3–§8 were specified without ever stating what happens behind them: a completed coil was recorded in the flat wire system alone, so packing could not find it, shipping could not ship it, certification had no chain back to the rod, and it never reached cost or yield. §13 states the seven records now created, and confirms **no new table is built** — **`OI-104` is answered**: a flat wire skid lives in the existing shop-floor skid register, numbered by the existing rule, so §6.2's assumption was right all along. §13.3 introduces the **two identities** a coil now has, which is the one item here we ask the client to confirm explicitly: the printed `FW-#####-C##` alpha is unchanged, and a second internal identifier in the existing nine-character plant format is derived from the source rod — so **a report showing the plant identifier rather than the printed one is expected behaviour, not a defect.** §13.4 puts a real limitation on the record: the plant genealogy holds **one** parent per coil while a welded coil normally has several, so it records the primary rod and the full chain stays in the flat wire record the certificates are built from — the two deliberately disagree, and we ask whether any existing process reads the plant chain. §13.5 lists the three values IT must confirm before this runs on live data (`Q34`–`Q36`). No rule confirmed earlier in the document changed. |
| 1.3 | Aug 12, 2026 | **MIGRATED 18 Aug 2026 from an orphaned row at the foot of the document.** When the thirteen per-document change logs were deleted on 15 Aug 2026 this row's table header went with them but the row itself survived, appended to the Client Sign-off approval table where it rendered as one of its rows — and its content had never been carried here. No rule, figure, screen behaviour or open item was added, removed or altered. |

## [MVP-1/RequirementDocuments/RocCheckin.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/Screens/RocCheckin.md)

| Version | Date | Change |
|---|---|---|
| 2.6 | Aug 22, 2026 | **§3.1a and §3.1b added — the order set, and the boundary that is not a check-in.** §3.1a mirrors pre-check-in's two validations and adds the case this screen has and that one does not: a rod **already mounted and running** is **refused as a duplicate** `[ORD014]`, because treating a repeat scan as a fresh check-in would mint a second run and **re-push the PLC tags mid-material**. §3.1b states that starting the *next* order on a mounted rod **is not a check-in at all** — the previous order's completion acknowledgement starts it, nothing is scanned and nothing is dismounted. ⚠ With no second check-in there is **no second tag push**, so both orders run under the first order's pass schedule; a differing schedule refuses the mounted handoff `[ORD015]`, and whether planning can produce that case is **`Q48`** |
| 2.5 | Aug 18, 2026 | **`CI-6` and confirmed decision `D7` amended — there is no change to the existing scheduling system's database** (decision `D-32`, on client direction). The in-process status is recorded on the flat wire module's **own** rod record; the shared scheduling record is not written at all. **The timing rule the client confirmed on 30 Jul is unchanged** — the status still changes at check-in and not at pre-check-in, and there is still no intermediate status for a welded-but-not-checked-in rod — and **nothing about the operator's experience changes**. The word *"shared"* is removed from `CI-6` because it was the only thing in this specification that described where the status lands. |
| 1.0 | Apr 25, 2026 | Initial document — recommended behaviour for "Acknowledge & Begin Check-in" on Dashboard 2 and Dashboard 5. |
| 2.2 | Aug 4, 2026 | **v2.2 — FM2 roller sizes corrected** (three stands: S1 8″, S2 6″, S3 6″; the 8″ roller is S1) and **OI-04 closed**, since its two candidate stands were one stand. |
| 2.0 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.0 for client review.** Added: automatic line selection; the payoff-selector rule for staged rod; min/max dimensional limits; `INFLAT` committed at check-in; the attribute-lookup and mandatory-confirmation flow; the consumed staging record; FM2's third stand and the FL1 no-edger correction. Consolidated the rules as CI-1 to CI-10, the decisions as D1–D9, and the unresolved points as a client sign-off checklist. Internal gap references and mockup-implementation notes removed. |
| — | Aug 1, 2026 | **Check-in now ends on the rod pre-check-in station rather than the active run monitor** (Section 3.7). Pressing *Acknowledge & Begin Check-in* completes check-in outright — the records are written, the tags are pushed, the run is open and the rod is drawing — so the destination is a question of what the operator does *next*, not of what check-in still owes. The next task in the continuous-feed cycle is staging the following rod on the idle payoff position, which is the pre-check-in station; returning there closes the cycle (stage → check in → stage the next) on one path rather than landing the operator on a monitor and leaving them to navigate back. The active run monitor is unaffected and remains reachable from the application bar and the line status board. **Raised as Q84 rather than applied silently:** the change assumes operators do not expect to see the run monitor at start-up to confirm the line took the tags and the gauge trace is live. If they do, the previous destination is the correct one and this reverts. |
| 2.1 | Aug 4, 2026 | **§3.6 reduced to a pointer.** This document listed the tag-push payload and was the outlier on two points — whether **edge type** is pushed, and whether speed is a **target** or a **limit**. Both are now settled in one place: edge type is pushed on FL2/FL3 and is not applicable on FL1 (FL1 has no edger), and the speed question is open as **`PLC-Q06`**. What stays here is the acknowledgement sequence, which is this document’s job: **confirm the schedule → write every audit record → push the tags → open the run**. |
| 2.4 | Aug 15, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.4 — the `## Document Change History` block removed.** The block is gone from the specification and its rows are folded into this section, which now carries a **Version** column; the client `.docx` no longer prints a history section, and the document's version is stated in its header block only. **No requirement, decision, open item or sign-off row changed.** This completes the 12 Aug 2026 consolidation, which had left these thirteen blocks in place as deliverable content. ## [MVP-1/RequirementDocuments/RodPreCheckin.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/Screens/RodPreCheckin.md)

| Version | Date | Change |
|---|---|---|
| 2.6 | Aug 22, 2026 | **§4.1a added — sequence validation, and the order *set*.** Since `Q70` the planning lookup returns **orders, plural**, so *“belongs to the established order”* becomes membership in an **ordered set**. Two validations, both also applied at check-in because a rule enforced at one of two entry points is not enforced (`Q73` item 7): order-set membership `[ORD008]`, and tier order `[ORD004]`. ⚠ **The tier refusal is deliberately stricter than the `Q24` out-of-sequence override** — `Q73`'s own consequence 1 makes staging a multi-order rod early *“refused, not overridden”*, because the boundary that rod carries would have nowhere to run; the `Q24` override still covers a deviation **within** a tier, which is a different thing. Residual: **`Q49`**, whether multi-order-last holds with no weld |
| 1.0 | Jul 29, 2026 | Initial specification: station concept, bay state model, staging validations, traveler queue, three-step wizard, and the two-sequence model. |
| 2.0 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.0 for client review.** Applied the July 30, 2026 client decisions: shared coil status set at check-in only; pre-check-out approval determined by weld state, with welded removal a supervisor-authorised rejection to `HOLD`; failed inspection released by WIP rejection; off-schedule staging replaced by automatic line selection; min/max limits for four dimensional attributes; a rod may carry more than one order; bundles are never stacked; no welded-not-checked-in status. Document restructured to client-deliverable form — superseded rules, prior design iterations, internal schema and interface detail, and repository-internal commentary removed. New requirements `PCI009`–`PCI020` proposed for the removal, bay-state, order-resolution and sequence-authorisation rules the SRS does not currently cover. Open items consolidated into Section 11 with a sign-off checklist. New requirement identifiers span `PCI009`–`PCI017`. |
| — | Aug 1, 2026 | **Rejection captured at the station.** The WIP rejection that releases a blocked position is now raised on this screen rather than reached by navigating to a separate one, so the failing rod and its position are already known and the operator returns to a station that has not lost its state. No rule in this document changed. |
| 2.1 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Version 2.1.** Added **Section 8.1 `PCI021`** — a read-only list of the welds already recorded against the run in progress: time, rod pair, footage at the weld, weld type, operator and quality, with the mandatory failure reason on a failed weld (`WLD013`). Scoped to the **current run** (not the shift, not the coil), available whenever a run exists including while the idle position is empty or blocked, and not editable — correcting a recorded weld remains the unspecified `WLD011`. This replaces a station control labelled *Weld event log*, which navigated to the weld-**recording** form; nothing in the system previously listed the welds made during a run, although each is a certificate traceability input. Also recorded in Section 8 that marking the weld does **not** ask for an operator badge — the operator is taken from the signed-in station session, since `WLD003` requires it recorded, not re-entered, and a keyed value could disagree with the session actually stamped. |
| 2.2 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Version 2.2.** Added **Section 8.1 `PCI022` — weld quality.** Recording a weld now **requires** a Pass/Fail result, with a reason mandatory on Fail (`WLD013`), using the same six reasons as the Weld Event screen so a weld is documented identically wherever it is captured. Neither result is pre-selected: this is the check whose purpose is to make the operator look at the join, and a pre-selected Pass can be confirmed without being read. **A failing weld is recorded but does not mark the rod welded** — the join did not hold, so the rod is not joined to the running rod and the line cannot transition through it; the position keeps reading *not yet welded*, the station states that the last weld failed and why, and the operator remakes it. Previously the station recorded only *that* a weld happened, with no statement of whether it held, while the same join captured on the Weld Event screen did record a quality result — one physical join, documented two ways. Renumbered the run weld list to **8.2**. **Q24 widened** rather than a new question raised: a remade weld leaves more than one record of one physical join, which is the re-weld-on-certificate question Q24 already asks — now reachable both by a weld that breaks mid-run and by one that fails its quality check before anything runs through it. |
| 2.3 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Version 2.3. The station-level weld readiness band is removed and every control moves onto the payoff position it acts on.** The band sat between the two positions and the traveler queue, carrying a sentence of narrative and two buttons. Its narrative was already on the position cards — the weight and percentage on the payoff bar, the weld states in the position's alert line, the cold-start message in the empty-position text — so the band restated at station level what the operator was already reading on the card. Its two controls were station-level for no reason other than where they happened to sit. **Mark as welded now sits on the staged position** (Section 8): that position *is* the incoming rod, so the action carries the rod into the record instead of asking which rod is meant. Its unavailable states now state their reason on the card, which the band never did. **Welds this run now sits on the running position** (Section 8.2), because the run belongs to that position, and carries its count on the control. **Open the active run is withdrawn as a position action** (Section 4.1) — the run screen remains reachable from the application bar and the Line Status board, and this station's purpose is preparing the *next* rod rather than driving the current one. The one instruction the band alone carried, *induction-weld tail to head*, is now stated on the staged position whenever a rod is running. **One consequence needs client confirmation** (**Q83**): with the control on the running position, at cold start there is no position to show it on, so *Welds this run* is **absent** rather than shown-and-greyed as it was before. The traveler queue gained the heading **"Rods In Queue"** (Section 4.5) — the queue head had been an unlabelled empty row since the order-context line was removed, leaving the table with no name. Also recorded: where a position has an obvious next step it is emphasised, and **the running position deliberately emphasises nothing**, because both of its actions are exceptional and emphasising either would read as a recommendation. |
| 2.5 | Aug 15, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.5 — the `## Document Change History` block removed.** The block is gone from the specification and its rows are folded into this section, which now carries a **Version** column; the client `.docx` no longer prints a history section, and the document's version is stated in its header block only. **No requirement, decision, open item or sign-off row changed.** This completes the 12 Aug 2026 consolidation, which had left these thirteen blocks in place as deliverable content. It also closes a drift the earlier pass created: version **2.4** was the 12 Aug repository-wide reissue and never had a row of its own in either place. |

Ten links repointed: the four owning specs and `PassScheduleManagement.md` are now same-folder, the master spec is `../`, and the register, `FlatWirePlan.md` and `FlatWireShopfloorDashboards.md` are `../../Analysis/`. Inbound citations updated in `CLAUDE.md`, `ShopfloorAndRealTimePlan.md`, `FlatWire_MasterSpecification.md` §A.2, `FlatWireProcessWalkthrough.md` and the 6 Aug client-call ledger. **The FM2 banner is unchanged and still applies** — the equipment description below is superseded and must not be built from. |
| Aug 11, 2026 | **Screen content consolidated into the owning requirement documents.** The Dashboard 2 / Dashboard 5 comparison moved to `RocCheckin.md` §4.3 and the Dashboard 7 source-traceability capture to `OutputCoilCompletion.md` §4; both are retained here as the design record under a pointer banner. Flagged the Spool-Related Open Questions section as a **May 2026 snapshot** subordinate to the 99-item register, which it had been silently competing with. The domain reference content — physical form, material flow, the coils-table mapping and the stakeholder concepts — is unchanged and remains authoritative for what a spool is. |

| Date | Changed By | Description |
