| Apr 24, 2026 | Analysis Team | Initial spool reference document created — comprehensive material flow explanation, system integration, and traceability chain documented |
| Apr 28, 2026 | MOM — Planning & Shopfloor meeting | **Planning Role updated:** alpha generation now at planning time (not execution); stop calculation is system-driven; planner inputs weight only. Three spool-to-order allocation scenarios confirmed (full, partial+stock, multi-order). "Assign as-is" stock option documented. Spool remainder tracking status updated to Decided. Coil capacity (1,000 lb) confirmed in comparison table. |
| May 4, 2026 | Analysis Team — Tim O. review | **Q55 Decided:** Anneal step modifies the existing spool alpha (no new child alpha); traceability maintained through the event log. Re-pass through FL1 not applicable (no capability). **Q57 In Progress:** Unique spool IDs confirmed (like furnace plates); tracking workflow FL1 → furnace → cooling → FL2 confirmed by operator spool number selection; full state machine transitions TBD. **Coil capacity updated** to 1,100 lb (revised from 1,000 lb per Tim O. May 4). |
| Aug 1, 2026 | Client sync (30 Jul call) | **Spool completion is graded on the customer weight range, not TKUP-1 capacity.** 3,500 lb is the equipment ceiling; working spools are ~**1,800 lb** so two ~900 lb finished coils can be cut at FL2, and completion is graded against a customer **min/max weight** (Q60). Recorded the **short-close** rule as an unplanned stop on the mill 10-90 pattern — supervisor override + hold or a concession offer when outside the range, remake last (Q65) — and the hard constraint that **the spool is always run off because FL2 has no spool stripper**. Added the mid-run coil-break rule: the stop is removed and a new stop starts from zero. |

| 2.0 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.0 for client review.** **Part C — short close** added, graded against the customer minimum/maximum with offer-before-remake escalation and the always-run-off rule; the mid-run coil-break rule recorded with its footage caution. The assumed 2,000 lb target **withdrawn** in favour of the customer range. Acceptance criteria renumbered as A-1…A-10 and B-1…B-22. Screen styling, layout dimensions, colour tokens, mockup demo affordances and internal component notes removed. |
| 2.2 | Aug 15, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.2 — the `## Document Change History` block removed.** The block is gone from the specification and its rows are folded into this section, which now carries a **Version** column; the client `.docx` no longer prints a history section, and the document's version is stated in its header block only. **No requirement, decision, open item or sign-off row changed.** This completes the 12 Aug 2026 consolidation, which had left these thirteen blocks in place as deliverable content. It also closes a drift the earlier pass created: version **2.1** was the 12 Aug repository-wide reissue and never had a row of its own in either place. |
| 2.3 | Aug 20, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.3 — the next spool carrier, and what the labels actually print.** Two new sections from the [20 Aug client call](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-20_SyncPlan.md). **§4.7 — the completion transaction captures the next carrier, and cannot commit without it.** Two physical reasons were given and both are recorded: one rod makes roughly three spools, so a single check-in cannot name them all (*"multiple spools will come out of a bundle"*), and check-in is at the payoff on the **far side of the machine** while the spool is at the output side where the operator station is. It is a **hard gate** — *"you cannot create a spool transaction without a spool to create that transaction"* — confirmed as a showstopper for the next spool starting. New rules **S-26 to S-31**, including refusal of a carrier already holding material. ⚠ **This supersedes the May 2026 description** in `Spool.md` (*"at the start of the FL1 job"*), which is wrong in a way that matters: the job makes several spools, so the capture recurs per completion. **§4.8 — what the labels print**, which this document referred to **nine times without ever stating**. Confirmed: the **1½ × 3 inch high-temperature coil label**, **two per label, one per side**, because nothing else survives the anneal; the **carrier number and every alpha on the spool** print; and **any one identifier resolves the spool at FL2**, the furnace-plate behaviour. Weight per alpha and the order set are `[PROPOSED]` — the first depends on the conversion still owed. New confirmed decisions **D10–D13**; new open items **`Q44`** (label field list; does the etched steel plate replace or supplement?), **`Q46`** (mandrel diameter — our reading is it is **fixed by the one standard size and should not be a field**) and **`Q42`** (the stenciled format, and thirty or forty-five). §4.6's completion and result steps and rule S-11 updated to match; sign-off Parts A and B extended. |

## [MVP-1/RequirementDocuments/SpoolQueue.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/Screens/SpoolQueue.md)

| Version | Date | Author | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| — | Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **Phase 12 and its four work items removed; three of the four date levers rewritten.** 14 stages and 103 work items. *Defer the yield, cost and scrap work* is no longer a lever — it is already out — and *add a fifth developer* was written against a four-developer baseline that no longer exists. They are replaced by **let the final testing and commissioning stage start early** (the one lever that removes a sprint) and **add a fourth developer**, whose effect is corrected from *four weeks* to *about two* now that Phase 14 is held to its own sprint. The milestone covering every stage no longer lists Phase 12, and the *team is smaller than the work requires* risk was rewritten from three team sizes to one. |
| — | Aug 13, 2026 | Plan team | **Made scenario-count-agnostic, and coverage re-pointed at the sprint plan rather than the backlog.** The 3-developer re-baseline broke three assumptions at once. **(1)** `SCENARIOS` was the hard-coded tuple `(2, 3, 4)` and the sprint tables were found by fixed section numbers; both now come from the plan — the headline table's rows define the team sizes, sections are located by content, and the allocation matrix's sprint columns are matched by header. Team sizes spelled as words (*"Three developers"*) are accepted, which the old positional `zip` never had to handle; with one team size the sprint and milestone columns drop their *"— N devs"* labels. **(2)** Coverage checked every backlog work item against the content file, so excluding Phase 12 from the plan failed the build with five errors. **The workbook's scope is the sprint plan, not the backlog** — coverage now runs against the allocation matrix, and a backlog story absent from it is an exclusion rather than an error. **It is printed at the end of every build**, because a story dropped by accident and one removed by decision are otherwise indistinguishable. **(3)** Client wording written for three options was rewritten for one, the sheet renamed **Delivery Summary**, and the excluded scope named on both it and the read-me — a client should not have to infer that yield and costing are absent. |
| 1.1 | Aug 2, 2026 | Plan team | v1.1. Two-status rule and single check-in, per client. Section 3.5 rewritten from a four-status proposal to the confirmed *Ready for FL2* / *Checked in* pair plus the exclusivity rule; Section 3.4 gains the two scan-while-occupied outcomes; rules renumbered SQ-7 to SQ-12; confirmed decisions 6 and 7 added; open item 2 reduced to the stored vocabulary only; open item 6 added, since a quality-held spool has no place on a two-status screen. |
| 1.0 | Aug 2, 2026 | Plan team | Initial issue. New screen: Dashboard 5A, the FL2 spool queue. Records the reasons it is being added, the scan-resolves-order behaviour, the four ways a scan can end, and the five items still needing client input. |
| 1.3 | Aug 15, 2026 | Plan team | **Reissued as version 1.3 — the `## Document Change History` block removed.** The block is gone from the specification and its rows are folded into this section, which now carries a **Version** column; the client `.docx` no longer prints a history section, and the document's version is stated in its header block only. **No requirement, decision, open item or sign-off row changed.** This completes the 12 Aug 2026 consolidation, which had left these thirteen blocks in place as deliverable content. It also closes a drift the earlier pass created: version **1.2** was the 12 Aug repository-wide reissue and never had a row of its own in either place. |
| 1.4 | Aug 20, 2026 | Plan team | **Reissued as version 1.4 — the screen's founding premise is reversed and its central assumption withdrawn.** From the [20 Aug client call](BaseDocuments/ClientCall_2026-08-20_SyncPlan.md). **§1.2 — FL2 now gets pre-check-in**, which this document's whole rationale rested on it *not* having. Recorded with the distinction that makes both statements true: FL2 still has **no staging space**, and what the client asked for is **validation before the spool is fetched**, not a place to put it — so the screen keeps its shape and gains an action. **§8 assumption 1 withdrawn** — *"a spool belongs to at most one order at a time"* is the opposite of what the client confirmed; the presentation it warned would be needed is the one now specified. **§3.2** the order bar becomes a **set** with the operator selecting the one being made, and **any identifier on the FL1 label resolves the spool** — the carrier number or any alpha. **§3.5** gains the **carrier-versus-material** distinction: the spool is a reusable stenciled article that *"is basically free again, back into the pool"* after FL2, and that state belongs to the carrier, not to the material, so it is deliberately **not** a third status on a work queue. **§4** spool identifier becomes the **carrier**, and `Source` lists each rod **with the weight it contributed**, which is what the certificates are built from. New rules **SQ-13**, **SQ-14**; **SQ-12 superseded** (the screen may now write a validation record — shape pending). Six new confirmed decisions **8–13**. **§7 item 1 CLOSED** — open since 2 Aug, and answered on all four counts: the carrier is the reusable physical article, typed and validated, assigned at the FL1 completion transaction, read at FL2 from the high-temp label. Item 2 narrowed. Four new open items **7–10**: what an FL2 pre-check-in records, orders per spool and who selects, whether empty carriers need to be visible anywhere, and the stenciled format. ⚠ **Also removed a stray `Document Change History` row** the 15 Aug consolidation left behind at the foot of the file — the sign-off table's last row was a v1.2 change entry. |

## [MVP-1/RequirementDocuments/WeldEvent.md](MVP-1/ProjectPlan/Business/Screens/WeldEvent.md)

| Version | Date | Change |
|---|---|---|
| 1.0 | Apr 2026 | Initial specification — screen purpose, captured data, traceability chain, confirm behaviour, end-to-end flow, welding-wire requirements. |
| 2.0 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.0 for client review.** Weld type corrected to induction only. Added the alloy/temper/diameter match as a stated validation, both payoff positions as captured data, and the July 30 decisions (welded-rod release, no welded-not-checked-in status, no stacking, mid-run coil break). Raised the coil-start footage offset (OI-25) and the in-place weld reversal as blocking client questions. Interface payloads, screen navigation targets and internal schema references removed. |
| 2.2 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Version 2.2 — the Weld Event screen is retired.** Dashboard 4 was removed, and the *Log Weld Event* action was removed from the active-run monitors on **all three lines**. The weld is now recorded **only** at the Rod Pre-Check-In station (Dashboard 2A, *Mark as welded*), which since version 2.1 captures the complete record including the mandatory quality result — this is also where the weld physically happens, so it removes a walk to a separate screen. **Two capabilities were not rehoused:** the re-sequenceable rod queue and the traceability chain view (`FR-175`). **And FL2, which has no pre-check-in station, now has no way to record a weld at all** — correct if FL2 only inherits its spool's weld markers, wrong if it joins spool to spool; the shift summary reports FL2 weld events, so confirmation is needed. Both raised as open items. |
| 2.1 | Aug 1, 2026 | **Version 2.1 — weld quality is captured with the weld.** Added Section 4.1 recording that the weld event is captured wherever the weld is made, with the **same mandatory quality result** in every case — there is no lighter path that records a weld without stating whether it held. Recorded that **a failed weld does not complete the join**: where the incoming rod is pre-checked-in, a Fail leaves it staged and un-welded, and the operator remakes the weld, so only a Pass marks the rod welded. Noted that a remade weld therefore leaves more than one record of one physical join, which widens **Q24** (re-welds on the certificate) to cover a weld that fails at capture and not only one that breaks mid-run; footage attribution across the two boundaries remains **Q22**. |
| 2.4 | Aug 15, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.4 — the `## Document Change History` block removed.** The block is gone from the specification and its rows are folded into this section, which now carries a **Version** column; the client `.docx` no longer prints a history section, and the document's version is stated in its header block only. **No requirement, decision, open item or sign-off row changed.** This completes the 12 Aug 2026 consolidation, which had left these thirteen blocks in place as deliverable content. It also closes a drift the earlier pass created: version **2.3** was the 12 Aug repository-wide reissue and never had a row of its own in either place. |

Holds `FW-010`, `FW-011`, `FW-012`, `FW-013` (Epic 2) and `FW-068`, `FW-069` (Epic 7) — **6 stories / 31 points** — with their **full As a / I want / So that bodies and complete acceptance-criteria checklists**, which is precisely what [`../ProjectPlan/05-Backlog-MVP2.md`](MVP-2/ProjectPlan/05-Backlog-MVP2.md) lacks: that file already carries all six as **condensed one-line rows**, so the two are complementary and neither supersedes the other. **These are moves, not copies** — this file is now the only home for the bodies. **`FW-014` is deliberately not here**, spanning both scopes (MVP-1 trigger, MVP-2 `PassScheduleChangeLog` sink), as `05-Backlog-MVP2.md` already records. **Known-wrong criteria are flagged in callouts rather than edited**, because these are the bodies the MVP-2 team will work from and silently correcting them would hide the disagreement with the governing specifications. Three such flags: **(1)** `FW-013`'s seven algorithm steps must **not** be implemented — build to `PassScheduleGenerationSpec.md` **v1.5**, with a table naming all five errors (the pre-flatten diameter is a lower bound needing the round-edge correction; the pass-count branches are wrong; the final die may not be snapped down; the roll gap sits **below** gauge by a **load-dependent mill-spring** term rather than above it by a fixed alloy springback multiplier — a machine stiffness carried as a material property; and the finishing-mill test is not the route decision). **(2)** `FW-012`'s mandatory-stand criterion names the **retired** pre-`D-26` component `FM2-6\"S2` — the non-bypassable final stand is **`FM2_S3`**, and the story's own component list one row above is already correct, so the criterion contradicts itself. **(3)** `FW-069`'s every figure is blocked by **`OI-101`** (shift boundaries undefined), and all four of its dependencies are MVP-1 stories, making it the one MVP-2 item that cannot start until MVP-1's Phase 6/7 work is done. Also records that **`FW-061` and `FW-082`, both `Critical` MVP-1, depend on `FW-010` here**, and that `FW-006` in MVP-1's Epic 1 also claims the `PassSchedule*` tables this story creates — a both-scopes overlap listed in no scope table. |

| Aug 1, 2026 | **Reissued as version 2.0 for client review.** **The generation algorithm carried in earlier versions is removed** — superseded in full by the Pass Schedule Generation Specification, and its pass-count logic was incorrect; only the two management-level constraints on generation are retained here. Added the FL1 no-edger and FM2 stand corrections to the schedule content definition, and raised the non-bypassable-stand and edge-type vocabulary conflicts. |
